## 1. How to use this roadmap

Read this first, then the documents it links for the milestone you are working on. Update the status column in §6 in the
same change that completes or blocks work, citing evidence (a test, a file path, a PR). Do not mark anything done
without evidence. Agent working rules are in [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md).

## 2. Product summary

**Purpose.** Switchboard is a local, headless controller that lets one primary AI orchestrator delegate coding tasks to
the operator's existing AI coding subscriptions (Claude Code, Codex, Grok Build, Antigravity, Cursor CLI, OpenCode, Kimi
Code, and since 2026-10-13 Muse Code) through each vendor's documented automation surface, behind one small MCP
tool interface. It owns policy,
workspace isolation, cross-provider task state, routing and capacity estimation, so the orchestrator does not have to.

**Users.** The *operator* (the account owner who installs Switchboard and logs in to each CLI; zemdregon first; Switchboard
is published as open source so anyone can run their own installation with their own subscriptions) and the
*orchestrator* (an MCP client agent such as OpenClaw or Grok Bot). See [PRD §2](requirements/PRD.md).

**Desired outcomes.** BO-1 to BO-9 in the [BRD](requirements/BRD.md): more work per paid subscription, no credential
handling, safe unattended runs, continuity across providers, visibility, resilience to CLI churn, agent work that
reaches the repository as ordinary commits with the audit record kept internally, and cheap text work handled by an
operator-chosen inner model instead of premium capacity, and less context and fewer cache misses per run (BO-9: a
retrieval index and sticky provider/model affinity, both planned).

**In scope.** Capabilities CAP-1 to CAP-13 in the [PRD](requirements/PRD.md), including projects (the environments
agents operate from and their contexts, CAP-10), controlled commits and publication (CAP-11) and the inner agent
(CAP-12), and the retrieval index (CAP-13, planned). "Application" in this
project means the MCP tool surface plus the operator CLI; "backend" means the controller daemon and its ledger,
workspace manager, router and adapters.

**Non-goals.** Owned by [PRD §6](requirements/PRD.md#6-non-goals). In short: no web UI (a read-only usage dashboard is
deferred), no login or OAuth handling, no hosted or multi-user service, no reimplementation of provider APIs, no claim
of Terms-of-Service compliance, no automatic merges into the operator's branches, and no push that is not made by a
policy-gated Switchboard tool (off by default); agents never commit or push themselves.

## 3. Current state and target state

### 3.1 Audit findings (2026-10-12)

| Area | Finding | Evidence |

| Source code, tests, config, CI | None exist. Nothing is implemented. | Repository tree at commit `d41c10c`: only `LICENSE` and `docs/research/` |
| Agent instructions | None existed before this change. Now [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) (with `CLAUDE.md` importing it). | — |
| Roadmap / plan | **No authoritative roadmap existed.** The plan was fragmented across two research documents. The closest thing to a plan was the eight-stage "production sequence" table at the end of the verified research. | [R2 § MCP, ACP, and the recommended production architecture](research/headless-ai-coding-controller-verified-2026-10-12.md#mcp-acp-and-the-recommended-production-architecture) |
| Original blueprint | Describes intent and an architecture, but many concrete flags, permission settings, quota assumptions and the transcript-extraction approach are contradicted by later verified research. Retained as historical record, marked **superseded where it conflicts**. | [headless-ai-coding-controller.md](research/headless-ai-coding-controller.md) |
| Verified research | Current as of 2026-10-12. Reviews the blueprint point by point and recommends corrections. Used as the primary technical source. | Requirements and design documents | None existed. Created in this change (see §5). | — |
| Sticky provider/model sessions and retrieval (2026-10-13, owner decision 40) | **Sticky sessions were not planned or implemented.** Closest items: FR-SES-03 (native resume on the same provider, within one meta-session only), FR-RTE-02 (the score's handoff-cost term discourages a swap but never pins a provider) and FR-CAP-06 (failover on `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`). Nothing kept a project on one provider and model across meta-sessions, nothing forbade score-driven swapping, and nothing tracked cache continuity. Extended, not duplicated: routing (FR-RTE-06 to 09) and capacity (FR-CAP-08), [ADR-0032](adr/0032-provider-model-affinity.md). **No RAG, vector or embedding material existed** in the research or the specifications; new capability CAP-13 and M12, [ADR-0031](adr/0031-retrieval-index.md). | Search of `docs/` at `e2b6f22`; SRS §3.6, §3.7, §3.8 |

### 3.2 Target state

An open-source, single-operator-per-installation controller for Linux and macOS that passes conformance and
malicious-fixture suites for all seven providers (Kimi Code once its contract is pinned), is installable by other people
from its README, exposes the MCP tools in [SRS §3.1](requirements/SRS.md#31-mcp-interface-fr-mcp), continues work across
providers from controller-owned state, routes with explainable decisions, and reports capacity as confidence-weighted
estimates. Architecture: [HLD](design/HLD.md).

## 4. Constraints, assumptions, decisions and conflicts

### 4.1 How sources are classified

| Class | Meaning | Where recorded |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Owner decision** | Explicitly decided by zemdregon. | Listed in §4.2 only. |
| **Stated intent** | Requirements stated in the original blueprint's introduction, which zemdregon supplied as the project brief. Treated as approved intent, not as approved design. | §4.3 |
| **Delegated decision** | Made by an agent during the 2026-10-12 planning task under zemdregon's delegation. Binding for implementation until revised, not owner-approved. | ADRs marked "Delegated", and §4.4 |
| **Assumption** | Conservative choice where information is missing. Must be confirmed or replaced. | §4.5 |

### 4.2 Owner decisions

1. The two research documents are project research for Switchboard (PRs #1 and #2, merged).
2. Agent work in this project routes model tiers by task difficulty (Haiku for simple, mechanical work; Opus or Fable
   only for demanding work). Recorded in [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#subagent-routing-and-orchestration).
3. The documentation set and roadmap in this change were requested by zemdregon. Their *content* is delegated, not approved.
4. Switchboard is built for zemdregon's own use and published as open source so other people can run it with their own
   subscriptions (2026-10-12).
5. zemdregon holds every subscription named in the research. All seven providers must be integrated; Claude Code and Codex
   are the first integrations (2026-10-12).
6. Switchboard controls *projects*: the environments the coding agents operate from (likely a container of some kind)
   and the *contexts* of those projects, such as repositories (2026-10-12). Recorded in
   [ADR-0013](adr/0013-projects-environments-and-contexts.md); the mechanism is delegated.
7. The agents have no commit or push controls. Commits and pushes are made by Switchboard tools injected into the agent,
   so there are no co-author trailers, "generated by" footers or session IDs and a commit looks like any other commit
   (2026-10-12). Recorded in [ADR-0014](adr/0014-tool-only-commits-and-publication.md); the mechanism, and keeping an
   internal audit trail of which agent produced which commit, are delegated.
8. An orchestrator may create and change projects over MCP (2026-10-12, answer "yes" to Q-7). Recorded in
   [ADR-0013](adr/0013-projects-environments-and-contexts.md). The gating (client opt-in, the client's own limits,
   operator approval for containers and push, creator-only visibility) is delegated.
9. Agents may push, through the injected `push` tool, and commit signing is wanted (2026-10-12, answer "yes" to Q-9).
   Recorded in [ADR-0014](adr/0014-tool-only-commits-and-publication.md). Reading: the question had two parts and "yes"
   was taken for both. Branch scope, the per-context opt-in (push stays off until a context enables it) and the
   signing mechanism are delegated. zemdregon left Q-6 (container runtime and logins) and Q-8 (commit identity) to the agent,
   so those choices are delegated, not owner decisions.
10. Codex under `inspect-untrusted` and `edit-untrusted`: the native Codex sandbox is accepted for now, and Codex stays
    eligible for those profiles (2026-10-12). *Consequence for the plan:* commands that Codex runs inside its sandbox,
    and whether that sandbox keeps them away from Codex's own credential store, are recorded as **unverified**, not as
    confined; the FR-POL-06 gap for Codex stays open until the live malicious leg (T3.9) checks it; the README's "no
    shell" wording is wrong for Codex and is corrected (T3.14). This answers the profile part of Q-11.
11. Verification commands (FR-EXE-08) may run on the host under `edit-untrusted` (2026-10-12). *Consequence:* confined
    verification through an outer sandbox (T3.11) is optional hardening, not a prerequisite for the verification stage
    (T3.10). Verification under `inspect-untrusted` is still refused ([FRD V7](requirements/FRD.md#71-delegate_task),
    Proposed; the SRS says nothing that overrides it).
12. Profile overrides in configuration (`profiles.<name>`, FR-POL-01) may loosen the defaults when the change is
    recorded in the run's policy snapshot (2026-10-12). *Consequence:* TDD §3.1's "may only tighten" contradicted
    FR-POL-01 and is corrected in this change (T3.15); T3.2 requires a loosening to be explicit in the config,
    validated, listed in the policy snapshot and reported by `doctor`.
13. Provider permission-bypass flags (`--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--yolo` and the others in FR-POL-03) may be
    emitted only when the operator-approved `privileged` scope explicitly sets bypass and the approval row covers that
    scope (2026-10-12). *Consequence:* this narrows FR-POL-03 and BR-14 rather than widening them, but it changes an
    external interface and [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#working-rules) allows a bypass flag only where a design document
    calls for it. Before any code emits a bypass flag: an [LLD §3](design/LLD.md#3-mcp-tool-contracts) contract change
    for the `privileged_scope` shape and a new ADR (Delegated wording, citing this decision) (T3.16), and the argv audit
    and `BYPASS_FLAGS` tests changed to allow a bypass flag only for an approved privileged scope with `bypassApproved`
    (T3.17).
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
14. Switchboard has an *inner agent*: a self-hosted model, a cheap or free OpenCode model, or another model of the
    user's choosing, used for many cheap tasks so the main provider CLIs do not have to do them: commit and PR writing,
    task routing for auto mode, and other tasks the drafter judges to fit (2026-10-13). Recorded in
    [ADR-0016](adr/0016-inner-agent.md). zemdregon's wording covers only the product idea. Everything else is delegated
    and Proposed: the backend kinds, which tasks are in scope ([PRD §3.1](requirements/PRD.md#31-inner-agent-task-catalogue)),
    the rule that it has no authority over security or run status, validation, fallback, privacy and the M11
    placement. (Numbers 10 to 13 are used by the open M3 plan, PR #13.)
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
15. zemdregon told the agent to go with its recommendations on Q-23 to Q-26 (2026-10-13): no hosted API key in
    Switchboard; M11 after M9; `draft_if_missing` for commit messages and PR drafts off unless enabled; the OpenCode
    `cli` kind stays Should until T11.0. The recommendations were already the defaults in force, so no requirement
    changed. They are now owner-chosen, not only delegated.
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
16. Muse Code (Meta's `muse` CLI) is added to the provider support plan (2026-10-13). zemdregon said it is "not a must
yet", so FR-ADP-12 is `Should`, in M4 (Q-27 keeps the access question open). Evidence is third-party and unverified
    ([research note](research/muse-code-2026-10-13.md)); the adapter design is delegated.
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
17. "Initial privileged approval lasts forever" (2026-10-13, zemdregon's answer to Q-29; numbered 14 while the M3 plan was
    open, renumbered on merge because main used 14). *Reading (the orchestrator's
    interpretation of the answer, recorded so that zemdregon can correct it):* the operator's approval of the **first**
    `privileged` run in a meta-session for a given scope is durable. It never expires, is never revoked automatically,
    and covers later runs of the **same** meta-session with the **same** scope, including `continue_task`
    continuations, without a new approval. A different scope, a different meta-session or a different MCP client needs
    a new approval. A bypass flag is still emitted only when the approved scope sets `bypass: true` (decision 13).
    *Consequences for the plan:* the approval binding in [LLD §3.1](design/LLD.md#31-privileged-scope-m3) moves from one
    run to (client, meta-session, canonical scope); `continue_task` may resolve `privileged` on a meta-session with an
    `APPROVED` approval, taking the approved scope as is and never widening it ([FRD C7](requirements/FRD.md#72-continue_task),
    reversing Q-29's Proposed default); FR-POL-09, FR-POL-03, BR-14, WF-7 and
    [ADR-0017](adr/0017-approved-privileged-scope-bypass.md) are amended (IDs unchanged); T3.3 and T3.4 change. Whether
    the operator needs a way to revoke a durable approval is Q-30.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
18. Design principle (2026-10-13): zemdregon prefers bypass modes for his own workloads. The less restrictive modes are
    for him; the more restrictive profiles exist for other people who deploy Switchboard. Bypass runs should be very
    loose and give agents the most freedom, so the restrictive defaults stay and loosening is explicit. *Consequence:*
    no default changes; every loosening (an approved `bypass: true` scope, a `profiles.<name>` override, the
    `privileged_approval` setting of decision 22) stays opt-in, explicit in the scope or the operator's configuration,
    and recorded. Later choices between a restrictive and a permissive option for bypass runs lean permissive, within
    decision 22's limits. *Residual risk:* the permissive choices below (R-38, R-39) apply to anyone who opts in, not
    only to zemdregon.
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
19. Answer to Q-33 (2026-10-13): for an approved `bypass: true` run on Claude Code, the adapter omits `--restricted`
    (and keeps the other isolation flags) instead of making `privileged` with bypass `Ineligible`. *Consequence:*
    recorded in [ADR-0018](adr/0018-claude-bypass-omits-restricted.md), which partly supersedes
    [ADR-0015](adr/0015-claude-untrusted-isolation-flags.md) for approved bypass runs only; T3.5 compiles that cell; the
    lifecycle no longer refuses every `privileged` run at dispatch, and the adapter's
    compile decides (T3.18). *Residual risk:* R-38 (whether the `--disallowedTools` Git deny rules still hold under
    bypass is unverified).
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
20. `edit-untrusted` stays as in M2 (2026-10-13): a `profiles.edit-untrusted` override that loosens `network` or
    `repo_config` is not enforced; the run uses the strict settings. *Consequence:* a known limit, documented in the
    [README](../README.md#trust-profiles) and the configuration table ([TDD §3.1](design/TDD.md#31-configuration)); no
    code change. As the code stands (checked 2026-10-13), the Claude adapter compiles `edit-untrusted` with network off
    at the tool level and repository configuration disabled whatever the override says, while the Codex adapter makes
    such a run `Ineligible: CAPABILITY_MISSING` (it compiles only network `off` and repository configuration
    `disabled` for every profile). The override is still listed by `doctor` and recorded in the policy snapshot.
    *Residual risk:* R-25 (an operator may believe a loosening applies when it does not); the opposite risk (silent
    widening) does not arise.
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
21. Codex `trusted-development` stays eligible with `shell: broad` (2026-10-13): Codex runs it in its `workspace-write`
    sandbox, and Git writes are limited only by that sandbox, which is unverified (decision 10's reasoning extended to
    `trusted-development`). *Consequence:* T3.6's matrix keeps that cell compilable (as the code already does); FR-GIT-01
    for Codex `trusted-development` rests on the sandbox Assumption until the live check X-G1 (T3.9). *Residual risk:*
    R-23 and R-37 (sandbox behaviour unverified); a Codex `trusted-development` agent may be able to write Git if the
    sandbox does not block the worktree's Git directory.
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
22. In approved bypass runs only the **per-scope approval** is lifted (2026-10-13); Git-write denial (FR-GIT-01, owner
    decision 7) and worktree isolation (FR-WS-05) stay enforced. *Delegated realisation (not owner-approved):* a
    per-client configuration setting `mcp_clients[].privileged_approval`, `"required"` (default, the restrictive
    choice for other deployers, decision 18) or `"not_required"`. With `not_required`, a `privileged` request from that
    client (ceiling still `privileged`; scope still validated, canonicalised, hashed and recorded; `bypass` still
    explicit in the scope) is admitted straight to QUEUED, with its approval row written `APPROVED` and
    `decided_by = "client_policy:<client_id>"` in the same admission transaction, so the audit trail is kept and the
    durable-approval rule of decision 17 applies to its continuations; `approve` and `deny` keep working for clients
    with `required`. Recorded in [ADR-0019](adr/0019-per-client-privileged-approval.md), which amends
    [ADR-0017](adr/0017-approved-privileged-scope-bypass.md)'s approval requirement for those clients; FR-POL-09,
    FR-POL-03, FR-MCP-06, BR-13, BR-14, WF-7 and [LLD §3.1](design/LLD.md#31-privileged-scope-m3) carry dated notes;
    T3.18. *Residual risk:* R-39 (approval-free privileged runs for those clients).
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
23. Answer to Q-36 (2026-10-13; zemdregon: "Refuse network off at tool level"): a Claude Code `privileged` scope with
    network `off` or `allowlist` stays `Ineligible: CAPABILITY_MISSING`, as T3.5 implements, so a privileged scope never
    claims a network limit that Claude enforces only at the tool level. *Consequence:* no code or requirement change;
    Q-36's Proposed default is now owner-chosen, and [TDD §4.1.1](design/TDD.md#411-claude-code-native-controls-per-profile-and-platform-t30)
    cites this decision. *Reading (recorded so that zemdregon can correct it):* the answer is taken for Q-36 only. Q-34
    (whether tool-level `off` is enough for `edit-untrusted` and `trusted-development`) and Q-37 (repository
    configuration `enabled` in a privileged scope) are not answered and stay open with their defaults.
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
24. Bypass without prompts; host and container agents (2026-10-13). zemdregon, verbatim: "My main concern for my usability
    of the product is having a bypass mode where all agents never have to ask for my permission, but since we are
    containerizing, we could simply not include a git tool, then git is handled by switchboard on the host. However,
    switchboard should support host-run agents as well as container agents, depending on how the user wants to set them
    up." *Owner direction:* (a) his main usability goal is a bypass mode in which agents never ask for his permission;
    (b) Switchboard supports both host-run and container-run agents, chosen per project (its environment) by whoever
    deploys it; (c) in a container environment the image ships without a Git tool (no `git`, no `gh`), so FR-GIT-01
    holds by construction rather than through provider deny rules, and Git (commit, push) is done by Switchboard on the
    host through the injected tools (FR-GIT-02 to FR-GIT-09, [ADR-0014](adr/0014-tool-only-commits-and-publication.md));
    (d) host-run agents keep the existing controls (Git deny rules where the provider has them, FR-GIT-03 ref
    snapshots, FR-GIT-06). The mechanism is Delegated. *Already in the plan:* (a) is decisions 13, 17, 19 and 22
    (approved `bypass: true` scope, durable approval, per-client `privileged_approval: "not_required"`; T3.18 done),
    though not every provider compiles a bypass scope yet (Codex does not); (b) is FR-PRJ-04's two environment kinds,
    `host` in M9 and `container` in M10, set per project in configuration ([LLD §11.1](design/LLD.md#111-configuration-shape)),
    with "always run in a container" already rejected in [ADR-0013](adr/0013-projects-environments-and-contexts.md);
    for (c), a container already mounts neither the Git directory nor any credential store (FR-PRJ-05,
    [TDD §3.11](design/TDD.md#311-container-environments)), the injected tools already run in the controller, and BR-31
    already allows `push` in a container with no credentials mounted. *What changes (Delegated, dated notes, no new
    IDs):* FR-PRJ-05 now requires an image with no Git client, checked before a run; FR-PRJ-04 says both kinds are
    first-class and chosen per project; FR-GIT-01 says how it holds in each kind; [TDD §3.10](design/TDD.md#310-git-commits-attribution-and-publication)
    and §3.11 follow; T10.0, T10.1 and T10.3 gain the checks (§6.4). Sequence unchanged (M3, then M9, then M10); Q-38
    was decided in decision 25. *Residual risk:* R-38 for host-run Claude bypass until C-G2; R-40 (provider
    CLIs that need Git; a Git client fetched or Git files written by an agent with shell and network).

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
25. Delegation rule, Q-34, Q-38 and the container-image ADR (2026-10-13). zemdregon, verbatim: "Q37: explain. Q34 unless
    it affects bypass mode you make the decisions (add that to agents) my only concern is with bypass mode, other modes
    are for other users, so choose the industry best choices/practices. Q38 you pick. Container ADR sure make it".
    *Owner direction:* (a) a decision that does not affect bypass mode is the agent's, made by industry best practice;
    this is now a working rule in [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) ("Best-practice defaults outside bypass mode"); (b) Q-37
    was asked to be explained, not answered, and stays open because it affects bypass runs; (c) the container-image
    ADR is wanted. *Delegated decisions made under (a), not owner-approved:* **Q-34** (no bypass effect): tool-level
    network `off` stays acceptable for `inspect-untrusted`, `edit-untrusted` and `trusted-development` when every shell
    command is an explicit allowlist entry the operator chose (least privilege), and stays `Ineligible:
    CAPABILITY_MISSING` with a `broad` Claude shell, as T3.5 implements; to avoid claiming an unenforced control,
    `list_capabilities` and the docs describe such `off` as "tool level only", never as an enforced network block, and
    R-36 stays open until the outer sandbox (T3.1) or the live check (T3.9) enforces it. **Q-38** (the order only
    affects when the container route arrives, not what bypass may do): the order stays M3, M9, M10, because M10 needs
    the M9 projects and injected tools; the document-only spike T10.0 may start any time after M3 (it needs no M9 code)
    so the container route is verified early. **Container image:** [ADR-0020](adr/0020-container-image-without-git-client.md)
    records the no-Git-client image rule of decision 24 (c) and amends ADR-0013's container mechanism.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
26. Answer to Q-37: repository configuration in approved bypass runs (2026-10-13). zemdregon, verbatim: "bypass mode allow
    all agents to make the changes". *Consequence (Delegated realisation, not owner-approved in detail):* in an
    approved bypass run (a `privileged` scope with `bypass: true` and the run's `bypassApproved`), `repository_config`
    modes `approved` and `enabled` compile, so repository hooks, MCP definitions and instruction files may load. Claude
    Code: the bypass argv omits `--safe-mode` for both; `--strict-mcp-config` is kept for `approved` (no repository MCP
    definition loads) and omitted for `enabled` (the repository's `.mcp.json` can load); `--restricted` stays absent
    ([ADR-0018](adr/0018-claude-bypass-omits-restricted.md)); `--permission-prompts none`, the tool lists and the
    FR-GIT-01 Git deny rules are unchanged, and `--mcp-config` stays barred. Recorded in
    [ADR-0021](adr/0021-repository-config-in-approved-bypass-runs.md); implemented by T3.19. A non-bypass `privileged`
    scope, a bypass scope without approval, and every other profile keep `Ineligible: CAPABILITY_MISSING` for
    `approved` and `enabled`. Codex stays refused for repository configuration because its `bypass: true` is itself
    `Ineligible` (flag undocumented); the decision applies once a Codex bypass is documented. Worktree isolation
    (FR-WS-05) and Git-write denial are unchanged. *Reading (recorded so that zemdregon can correct it):* "allow all agents
    to make the changes" is read as "allow every provider adapter's bypass run to load repository configuration", not
    as lifting any other limit; the split `approved` (MCP still blocked) versus `enabled` (MCP loads) is the agent's
    choice. **Unverified:** that omitting `--safe-mode` and `--strict-mcp-config` loads exactly those items under
    `claude --help` 2.1.296; the help text does not say. A live check is required (T3.9). *Residual risk:* R-41.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
27. Answer to Q-31: nested repositories in a run worktree (2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent), under [owner
    decision 25](#42-owner-decisions); not an zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** It does not affect bypass mode (it
    fails no run, so zemdregon's bypass runs stay unobstructed), so by decision 25 (a) it is the agent's, by best practice.
    The controller never lets Git descend into a nested repository and does not fail the run. Before each controller
    Git operation that scans the worktree (`assessMutation`: `status`, `diff`; `finalize` and `rollback`: `add -A`,
    `diff --cached`), it enumerates every directory below the worktree root that holds a `.git` entry of any kind
    (directory, gitdir-pointer file or symlink) without following symlinks and without running Git, and excludes each
    with a `:(exclude,top,literal)<path>` pathspec (`glob` with escapes for a name that is not valid UTF-8);
    `status` and `diff` also pass `--ignore-submodules=all`. The worktree root's own `.git` is never nested. Each skipped
    path is a non-fatal warning: `nested_repositories_skipped` in the `workspace_assessment` run event and in the
    terminal run payload (no schema change). **Consequence, stated plainly:** files inside a nested repository are not
    captured in checkpoints, diffs or commits, so change detection is weaker there; a gitlink entry the run itself had
    staged is still committed as a pointer. Recorded in [ADR-0022](adr/0022-nested-repositories-excluded-from-controller-git.md);
    implemented by T3.20; residual risk R-35 (mitigated).

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
28. Answers to Q-18 and Q-19, and the fix for R-42: retention GC (2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent), under
    [owner decision 25](#42-owner-decisions); not an zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** None of it affects bypass
    mode (retention touches only idle, terminal meta-sessions and refuses nothing a bypass run needs), so by decision 25
    (a) it is the agent's, by best practice. (a) **Q-18:** the retention unit is a whole idle meta-session (no lease, only
    terminal runs, newest activity older than `retention.days`), never a single run, because collecting one run can
    break the checkpoint chain of the rest. (b) **Q-19:** `run_event` rows are kept: the table is append-only by trigger
    ([ADR-0005](adr/0005-sqlite-ledger.md)) and is the audit record, so NFR-DATA-01 is amended (dated note in the SRS):
    retention applies to worktrees, controller branches and artifact files, not events. (c) **R-42:** GC marks first.
    One `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction re-checks idleness, sets the meta-session `ARCHIVED` and appends `gc_intent`;
    only then are worktrees, `switchboard/` branches and artifact files removed, and a second transaction sets
    `deleted_at` and appends `gc_outcome`. Admission refuses an `ARCHIVED` meta-session with `INVALID_REQUEST` (no new
    error code), both in validation and inside the admission transaction, and `continue_task` already refused a session
    that is not `ACTIVE`. An `ARCHIVED` session whose collection is unfinished is a GC candidate again and rolls
    forward. Wording in FR-SES-06 gains a dated note; no ID changes and no schema or migration change.
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
29. Answer to Q-14: the adapter preflight cache (2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent), under [owner
    decision 25](#42-owner-decisions); not an zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** It has no bypass effect (it only
    avoids re-running status commands the controller already runs; no permission, approval or flag changes), so by
    decision 25 (a) it is the agent's, by best practice. NFR-PERF-02 becomes task **T3.21** in the M3 table (§6.5): an
    in-process `PreflightCache` (`src/adapters/preflight-cache.ts`) wraps `probe` and `authPreflight` in `delegate_task`
    stage-1 eligibility, dispatch PREFLIGHT and `list_capabilities`. It caches **successes only** (every probe step ok;
    auth `OK`); a failed or throwing probe, `AUTH_REQUIRED` and `UNKNOWN` are never cached, so an install or a login is
    seen on the next call (fail closed, NFR-SEC-06). An entry is keyed by adapter id and invalidated when the resolved
    binary's path, `mtime`, size or inode changes, or after the TTL (**Assumption**: 60 s, config
    `timeouts.preflight_ttl_seconds`, integer 0 to 3600, 0 disables; the pre-existing `timeouts.*` row in
    [TDD §3.1](design/TDD.md#31-configuration) named a "preflight TTL" but no key existed). Only the status results the
    adapters already return are stored; no credential or token passes through. `doctor` and `adapters validate` stay
    uncached (they are explicit checks). *Numbering:* this entry uses 29, leaving 28 to a parallel change. No ADR: the
    cache realizes [ADR-0009](adr/0009-version-gated-adapters.md) and [SDD](design/SDD.md) NFR-PERF-02 (preflight cache
    keyed on binary version plus TTL) and changes no component boundary or persistence; the config key is recorded in
    TDD §3.1 and LLD §11.1. *Unverified:* real provider CLI probe cost and whether an in-place upgrade always changes
    `mtime` or size (a same-size, same-`mtime` replacement is caught only by the TTL).
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
30. Answer to Q-39: minimum Git version (2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent), under [owner
    decision 25](#42-owner-decisions); not an zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** It has no bypass effect (no
    permission, approval or flag rule changes; it only refuses a Git too old for the controller's own isolation
    variables), so by decision 25 (a) it is the agent's, by best practice. `MIN_GIT_VERSION` in `src/workspace/git.ts`
    goes from 2.30 to **2.32**, because `scrubbedGitEnv()` already relies on `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` (Git 2.32.0 release notes).
    It changes the technology stack's stated floor, so it is recorded in
    [ADR-0023](adr/0023-minimum-git-version-2-32.md). `doctor`, the workspace manager's Git check, the README and TDD §2
    state 2.32; no requirement ID changes and no SRS requirement names a version. Residual risk R-44 is mitigated.
    *Unverified:* the 2.32 introduction version rests on the release notes, not on a test against an old Git.
<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
31. Project columns and the project block in the policy snapshot (2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent), under
    [owner decision 25](#42-owner-decisions); not an zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** Ledger migration 0002
    adds the nullable `project_id` to `meta_session` and `run` (the DDL was already proposed in LLD §1); the resolved
    project is frozen into the policy snapshot as `project`; `mcp_clients[].projects` is added to the config schema,
    with an absent list meaning no project (fail closed, workspace-path requests unaffected). A persistence-format
    change needs an ADR: [ADR-0024](adr/0024-project-id-columns-and-project-snapshot.md). No requirement ID changes.
    *Unverified:* nothing ran against a live provider.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
32. Commit provenance tables, publish ref, crash ordering and the sanitiser's pattern language (2026-10-13).
    **Delegated decision (agent), under [owner decision 25](#42-owner-decisions); not an zemdregon quote and not
    owner-approved.** Ledger migration 0003 creates `git_commit` and `git_push` (DDL of LLD §1 plus a unique index on
    `(run_id, commit_sha)` and an index on `git_push(run_id)`). A commit is recorded as intent event, compare-and-swap
    `update-ref`, then the `git_commit` event and row in one transaction, with `recover()` resolving a crash at any step.
    The local publish ref is `refs/heads/switchboard/pub/<sess>` (not `push.branch_prefix`, which names the remote
    branch). Operator sanitiser patterns (`git.sanitize_patterns`, now in the config schema) are limited to a validated
    regular-expression subset with a cost bound; no RE2 dependency is added. A persistence-format change needs an ADR:
    [ADR-0025](adr/0025-git-commit-provenance-and-sanitiser.md). No requirement ID changes. *Unverified:* nothing ran
    against a live provider; Git 2.43.0 is the only Git exercised.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
33. Per-run injected tool channel (2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent), under
    [owner decision 25](#42-owner-decisions); not an zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** The `switchboard tool-shim`
    subcommand, a per-run Unix socket (`<data_dir>/runs/<run_id>/tools.sock`, or a private short directory when that
    path exceeds 100 bytes), a 256-bit token kept only as a digest in memory, one request per connection with fixed
    per-call checks and limits, and a start-up sweep of sockets a crash left behind; `GitCapabilities` in the adapter
    contract (fake `injected`, Claude Code and Codex `none` with the reason); the policy snapshot's project block
    freezes `writable`, `identity` and `push.policy` (Q-41). A trust boundary and an external interface need an ADR:
    [ADR-0026](adr/0026-per-run-tool-channel-socket-and-token.md). No requirement ID changes. No bypass run is affected
    (no adapter that runs a bypass scope declares a channel). *Unverified:* nothing ran against a live provider.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
34. Ref snapshots and Git policy violation handling (2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent), under
    [owner decision 25](#42-owner-decisions); not an zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** At LEASED, before spawn,
    a `git_ref_snapshot` run event (version 1) records every ref of the main repository as a digest plus a list bounded
    to 5000 refs and 1 MiB (a tail digest covers the rest), and the main and worktree `HEAD`s; a run whose repository cannot
    be read fails `TOOL_FAILURE` before spawn. At FINALIZING, before the checkpoint, the controller compares, appends
    `git_policy_violation` (ref names, kinds and SHAs only) before acting, and then restores only its own refs with a
    compare-and-swap: the run branch to the `pre` checkpoint (the agent's tree stays as checkpoint content), the
    worktree `HEAD` to the run branch, and the publish ref to the newest controller commit whose recorded chain reaches
    the base. Every other ref (default branch, tags, remote-tracking refs, the operator's `HEAD`) is reported, never
    changed (FR-WS-05). The run's outcome is unchanged (FRD BR-27). Bypass runs are handled identically (owner decision
    22). A persistence format needs an ADR: [ADR-0027](adr/0027-ref-snapshots-and-violation-handling.md). No requirement
    ID changes. *Unverified:* only Git 2.43.0 exercised; nothing ran against a live provider.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
35. Controller-side push, credential-reach rule and operator push command (2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent),
    under [owner decision 25](#42-owner-decisions); not an zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** The only push is the
    meta-session's publish ref to `refs/heads/<push.branch_prefix><sess>` on the configured remote, as an explicit
    `<sha>:<ref>` refspec without `+`, after checking that every pushed commit has a provenance row of the session and
    that the target is not the base ref, the remote's `HEAD` branch or a checked-out branch. `push.remote` must be a
    remote name (the loader refuses a URL without echoing it) and is resolved by Git at push time; URLs are classified
    in memory and never recorded. The push is the one controller Git process that sees the operator's configuration:
    an allowlisted environment, no terminal, hooks off, `push.default=nothing`, no tags, only `https`/`ssh`/`file`, and
    refused when the repository's own configuration sets a command or redirect key. BR-31 is read as "shell `none` or
    an empty allowlist, never a bypass run, `host` only until M10"; any `git_policy_violation` in the meta-session
    refuses every later push (Q-44). `switchboard git push` does the same push for the operator (without BR-31) under
    the data-directory lock. The T9.5 ref guard expects the remote-tracking ref of an accepted controller push. A trust
    boundary and an external interface need an ADR: [ADR-0028](adr/0028-controller-push-and-credential-reach.md). No
    requirement ID changes. *Superseded in part by decision 37 (2026-10-13): approved bypass runs are exempt from BR-31
    and are offered `push`; the "never a bypass run" wording here records the first reading.* *Unverified:* only Git
    2.43.0 and local bare remotes; no `https` or `ssh` remote, credential helper or SSH agent was exercised; nothing ran
    against a live provider.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->

    through the same sanitiser and provenance as the commit tool. The opt-in is per project; **zemdregon wants it enabled
    for his own projects**, and T9.7 is to be built. FR-GIT-09 keeps the priority the SRS gives it. (c) **Q-3:** yes,
    TypeScript is fine (ADR-0010 stands). **Q-4:** the orchestrators to test against are a Grok bot ("orchestrator Grok

    each one connects to MCP is UNVERIFIED:** nobody has checked that these can launch or consume a stdio MCP server

    per-provider tool injection; findings are in [TDD §3.10](design/TDD.md#310-git-commits-attribution-and-publication)
    ("External reference (T3 Code ...)") and R-18, labelled as observed in that project's source and unverified on our
    CLIs. Not covered by zemdregon's answers: Codex, Cursor, Grok Build, Antigravity, OpenCode and Kimi Code channels (T9.0
    items stay open). Residual risk: R-50 (not blocking). *Unverified:* nothing ran against a live provider or a
    live orchestrator.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
37. BR-31 does not block Git operations on bypass mode (2026-10-13). zemdregon's words, verbatim: "BR-31. Don't block
    git operations on bypass mode". **The words are zemdregon's; the narrow reading below is a Delegated interpretation
    (agent), recorded so zemdregon can correct it.** zemdregon's priority is that an approved bypass run is never
    blocked from Git operations through Switchboard. Reading: in an approved bypass run (scope `bypass` true AND an
    approval the controller verifies, the same `bypassApprovedFor`/`evaluateBypass` evaluation the argv audit gate
    uses), the controller-mediated `push` tool is offered and accepted regardless of the BR-31 credential-reach rule; the
    channel-open event and every `git_push` event and row carry the reason `bypass_exempt`, so `runs show` shows it.
    Everything else about `push` stays: the controller alone pushes (the agent never receives credentials; the push
    process's allowlisted environment is unchanged), policy `branch` is required (policy `none` offers nothing), the
    explicit verified refspec, never forced, provenance-verified commits only, protected targets and repository-config-
    unsafe refusals, limits, the Q-44 violation refusal and recording. Not relaxed: owner decision 22 keeps the provider's
    Git-write denial (the agent still cannot run `git commit` or `git push` itself; the `--disallowedTools` Git deny
    rules and the FR-GIT-03 ref snapshots stay) and the operator command is unchanged. Non-bypass runs keep BR-31
    exactly (the other profiles are for people who deploy Switchboard, decision 25). The exemption derives only from
    controller-held facts (the approval row and policy snapshot), never from anything the agent or an MCP client can
    set; a snapshot or scope that claims `bypass` without a verified approval is still refused (`bypass_run`). **This
    is the one place a bypass run's agent can reach push credentials by design of the owner's choice**: an approved
    bypass agent with a shell can read the operator's push credentials while the `push` tool is offered; the
    controller still never gives them to it. Amends [ADR-0028](adr/0028-controller-push-and-credential-reach.md)
    (then unmerged, so edited in place under "Amended before merge"; now merged); no requirement ID changes (dated notes
    on BR-31 and FR-GIT-06). Residual risk: R-51 (not blocking).

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
38. Finalize-only mode as built (T9.7, 2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent), under [owner decisions
    25](#42-owner-decisions) and 36; not an zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** The opt-in is the repository context
    key `finalize_commit` (boolean, default `false`; it needs an `identity` and only the primary, writable context may
    set it), frozen into the run's policy snapshot like the identity. It applies only to an adapter whose
    `commitChannel` is not `injected` (Claude Code and Codex today); a `none` adapter without the opt-in runs as before
    (diff and checkpoint only), and no ineligibility rule exists (Q-43). At FINALIZING, after the ref check and the
    evidence checkpoint, a run about to end SUCCEEDED gets ONE commit on the publish ref through the T9.3 publisher (a
    separate instance that accepts only FINALIZING runs): the checkpoint's changed paths (nested repositories already
    excluded), the context identity, and the agent's structured final summary as the message after the FR-GIT-04
    sanitiser. Fail closed, never changing the outcome, each recorded as a `git_finalize_commit` event with a reason:
    no commit for a failed or cancelled run, after any `git_policy_violation` in the meta-session (BR-27, as Q-44 does
    for pushes), without a usable summary (`no_message`; the controller never writes a stand-in), for a summary over
    8192 characters (not truncated), for more than 256 paths (both limits lifted for approved bypass runs by decision
    44), or when any path is refused (counts only). Provenance is
    the existing `git_commit` row with `origin = 'finalize_only'`; the `git_commit` and intent events carry `via:
    "finalize"` and the FINALIZING transition's event id in place of a tool-call event (FR-GIT-07), so no ledger column
    or migration is added. Approved bypass runs are handled identically (except for the two size limits, decision 44).
    No ADR: the persistence format
    (`origin = 'finalize_only'`) and the mode itself were already decided (ADR-0014, ADR-0025, LLD §1); no boundary,
    trust or credential change. No requirement ID changes. Residual risk: R-52 (not blocking); Q-45 decided (decision 44).
    *Unverified:* nothing ran against a live provider; only the fake adapter and Git 2.43.0 were exercised.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
39. Orchestrator-managed projects, T9.9 (2026-10-13). **Delegated decision (agent), under owner decision 25; not an
    zemdregon quote and not owner-approved.** zemdregon's only input is "yes" to Q-7 (decision 8); FR-PRJ-10 and FRD
    BR-32 were already Proposed. None of these choices changes what a bypass run may do: they only narrow what an
    orchestrator can define. Choices, all fail closed: (a) **persistence** ([ADR-0030](adr/0030-dynamic-project-revisions-and-approval.md),
    migration 0004): append-only `project_revision` (without LLD §1's `approved_at`, which an append-only row cannot
    take), `project_revision_decision` (the operator's approval or rejection) and `project_event` (every call, accepted
    or refused, since `run_event` needs a run); state (in force, pending, approved, rejected, deleted) is derived;
    deleted ids are never reused. (b) **config:** `mcp_clients[].manage_projects` (default off) and `max_push_policy`
    (`none`/`branch`, default `none`, refused without `manage_projects`). (c) **limits:** the client's ceiling and
    `max_push_policy`; with no client provider list in the SRS or LLD, the client's provider set is read as every
    provider the operator has not disabled (Q-47); repositories under an allowed root and not inside `data_dir`; strings
    free of control, separator and bidirectional characters; strings at most 4096 characters, arrays at most 32 items,
    32 live projects per client and 100 revisions per project (Assumptions). (d) **approval:** besides `container`, push
    and widening (BR-32), a commit `identity` and `sign: true` also wait for the operator (stricter than BR-32; LLD
    §11.2's `identities` list is not built); widening is measured against the revision in force; `projects approve`
    needs `--revision` once a project has more than one revision (time-of-check guard) and, like `approve`/`deny`, writes
    its row while `serve` runs. (e) **use:** a configured id always wins; a dynamic project resolves only for its
    creator and only while that client keeps `manage_projects`, to its in-force revision, after re-checking the client's
    current limits and every filesystem rule; the snapshot shape is unchanged and the route decision names the revision.
    (f) **codes:** a configured project and another client's get the same `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` message; delete with a
    non-terminal run (or a request being admitted) is `INVALID_REQUEST` as SRS FR-PRJ-10 and LLD §3 say, read as
    winning over BR-32's general sentence. (g) **not built:** push targets of approved dynamic projects (a push is
    refused, Q-46). No requirement ID changes. Residual risks: R-53 and R-54 (not blocking).

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
40. Retrieval index and sticky provider/model affinity (2026-10-13). **zemdregon's words, verbatim, are only this:**
    "Add this to the plan: per project automatic RAG/Vector DB for reduced context requirements, as well as possibly a
    deployment wide RAG for general info across all projects. If its not already planned or implemented, also sticky
    sessions to increase cache hits. Generally only swapping providers and models when maxing out usage, or on user
    request, so that we dont round Robin a single project all the time causing a ton of cache misses." *Everything below
    is Delegated (agent), not owner-approved, and Proposed.* (The number 39, R-53, R-54, Q-46, Q-47 and ADR-0030 are
    reserved by PR #36.)
    - **Audit finding.** Sticky sessions were not previously planned or implemented (§3.1). The closest items are
      FR-SES-03 (native resume on the same provider, within one meta-session), FR-RTE-02 (a handoff-cost term in the
      score that never pins a provider) and FR-CAP-06 (failover on `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`). They are extended, not replaced:
      routing gains FR-RTE-06 to 09 and capacity gains FR-CAP-08. No RAG, vector or embedding material existed anywhere.
    - **Retrieval (Delegated).** A per-project automatic index (FR-RAG-01 to 08, 11, 14) and an optional deployment-wide
      index (FR-RAG-09, 10), new capability CAP-13, outcome BO-9, new milestone M12 (§6.7), after M9 and using the M11
      backend port and its privacy gates. Mode `auto` means on if and only if an embedding backend is configured (the
      reading of "automatic", Q-49). The deployment-wide index is "possibly" in zemdregon's words, so it is off by
      default, holds operator-added sources and explicitly contributed project content, and is read only by projects
      that set `use_global` (Q-50). Retrieved content is untrusted data and has no authority over any security or policy
      decision (FR-RAG-07). Storage is Proposed as SQLite with in-process search, settled by the T12.0 spike (Q-48).
      Decided in [ADR-0031](adr/0031-retrieval-index.md). Residual risks: R-55 to R-57 (not blocking).
    - **Affinity (Delegated).** The controller keeps a project on its last provider and model across meta-sessions and
      swaps only for a closed list of reasons: a user request, capacity exhausted (the confidence estimate of ADR-0007
      or `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`), ineligibility, or a failure after retry (FR-RTE-06 to 08). A better score, lower cost or
      lower load is never a reason, and there is no round-robin for one project. This reads "maxing out usage, or on
      user request" as the first two entries and adds the other two as safety floors; zemdregon did not name them. After
      a switch the project stays on the new provider (Q-51). Thresholds are Assumptions and no number is invented. Lands
      in M6/M7. Decided in [ADR-0032](adr/0032-provider-model-affinity.md). Residual risk: R-58 (not blocking).
    - **Not claimed.** That any provider's cache is hit more often, or that retrieval reduces tokens, is unmeasured.
      FR-CAP-08 records cache token counts where a CLI reports them (Q-53) so the operator can check.

<!-- markdownlint-disable-next-line MD029 -->
41. Claude Code tool channel only for approved bypass runs (2026-10-13). *Delegated wording of zemdregon's choice, not
    his words:* the operator-machine live check (`docs/research/live-checks-2026-10-13.md`, PR #41, its first
    recommendation) found that `--safe-mode` stops the injected MCP server from starting, while omitting it loads the
    repository's `CLAUDE.md`, hooks and `.mcp.json` (R-41). The agent put options to zemdregon and **zemdregon chose
    option A**: the Claude channel (T9.4b, ADR-0029) is offered **only** to an approved `privileged` run whose scope
    sets `bypass` (ADR-0017), where decision 26 already treats repository configuration as acceptable and bypass is
    deliberately loose. In that case, and only with the opt-in `providers.claude-code.git_channel`, the adapter omits
    `--safe-mode` so the server can start (`--strict-mcp-config` kept except under decision 26's `enabled`). Every
    other run (`inspect-untrusted`, `edit-untrusted`, `trusted-development`, `privileged` without bypass) keeps
    `--safe-mode`, gets no channel and an unchanged argv, and commits through finalize-only (FR-GIT-09, T9.7).
    Git-write denial (deny rules, `--tools`) and worktree isolation still apply in bypass (live check 14, R-38 HELD).
    *Delegated mechanism:* [ADR-0033](adr/0033-claude-channel-bypass-only-without-safe-mode.md) (per-run
    `offersChannel`, `removeFlags`, three new audit refusals). No requirement ID changes. The channel stays
    UNSUPPORTED: it has not run end to end through Switchboard (the live check used a hand-made server). Residual
    risk: R-59 (not blocking); open question Q-54.

42\. Per-run worktrees for concurrent agents in a project (2026-10-13). **zemdregon's words, verbatim, are only this:**
    "Is this already being tracked somewhere in the roadmap or documents? The anytime multiple agents spawn within
    the same project, they should be given their own work tree."
    *Everything below is Delegated (agent), not owner-approved, and Proposed.*
    - **Audit finding.** Partly covered, not as a project rule. Each write-capable run gets a controller-created
      worktree (FR-WS-01), and parallel tasks get separate worktrees with one write lease each (FR-WS-02); LLD §5 says
      parallel tasks use separate meta-sessions and therefore separate worktrees, and ADR-0006 records the worktree
      model. Not covered: a project-scoped rule that concurrent runs of one project never share a worktree (added as
      FR-WS-08), and when a concurrent run's worktree is removed after its work is merged or abandoned. FR-WS-07 and
      [FRD §4.4](requirements/FRD.md#44-worktree-disposition-per-terminal-state) keep worktrees until retention GC.
      One meta-session never runs two runs at once (FR-SES-06), so the rule concerns different meta-sessions.
    - **Requirement (Delegated).** FR-WS-08 (Must, M9): one worktree and one write lease per concurrent run of a
      project, including runs in one `container` environment (FR-PRJ-05). FR-WS-09 (Should, Proposed, M9): removal of
      those worktrees on merge or abandon, with the branch and diff artifact kept until retention GC. The worktree is
      created when the run is leased, not at process spawn (LLD §5). Until FR-WS-09 is accepted, FRD §4.4 applies
      unchanged.
    - **Task.** T9.11 in §6.3. **Open question:** Q-55. No ADR is needed: the worktree model is ADR-0006's and the
      boundary does not change. No numeric caps are set.

43\. Optional agent subscriptions to PR and merge events (2026-10-13). **zemdregon's words, verbatim, are only this:**
    "Also, is an optional feature to add later once everything else is done. PRs and merges and stuff like that should
    be able to be subscribed to by an agent so that it can track it so that if anything changes like if CI passes or
    fails or another agent commits to their PR it notifies them so that they can make changes as necessary."
    *Delegated (agent), not owner-approved, and Proposed. Deferred (§8).* It is not planned work: it needs a PRD
    use case, SRS requirements and a milestone before any build (AGENTS.md, "Start here"). It is recorded as CAP-14
    (PRD §3) and FR-SUB-01 (SRS §3.16), both Deferred. The existing PR subscription in the agent tooling is not part
    of Switchboard's specification.

44\. Loosen the finalize-only size limits for approved bypass runs (2026-10-13; answers Q-45). **zemdregon's words,
    verbatim, are only this:** "Yes to Q-45. Loosen restrictions always for bypass"
    *Everything below is Delegated (agent), not owner-approved.* The "yes" accepted the agent's recommendation on Q-45:
    in finalize-only mode (T9.7, FR-GIT-09) an **approved bypass run** (an approved `privileged` scope with `bypass`,
    ADR-0017, derived only from `bypassApprovedFor`, the evaluation the BR-31 exemption of decision 37 uses) no longer
    skips the commit for more than 256 changed paths (`too_many_paths`) or a summary over 8192 characters
    (`message_too_long`). All changed paths go into the one commit, and the summary is sanitised **in full** first
    (FR-GIT-04) and only then truncated, at a line boundary or at the character limit, to at most 8192 characters with
    a one-line note that the provider's complete output is in the run's artifacts. Every other run keeps today's skip
    behaviour and reasons exactly. Realisation: `approvedBypass` on the finalize request (a plain argument) and
    `looseLimits` on the publisher's input, honoured only with `origin: "finalize_only"`, so the agent's `commit` tool
    keeps its 256-path and 8192-character limits. *Applies "always for bypass" only to size limits.* Not loosened: the
    path refusals (traversal, symlink, `.git`, nested repository, ignored, missing), the sanitiser (including its own
    65,536-character input bound), the controller-owned temporary-index plumbing and compare-and-swap, the
    `git_policy_violation`, `no_message`, `no_identity`, `environment_not_host`, `context_not_writable` and
    run-outcome skips, Git-write denial (FR-GIT-01), worktree isolation, BR-31 and the push policy. A summary over the
    sanitiser's bound still ends as a recorded `SANITISER_FAILED`, not a commit. No ADR: the controller already owns
    every Git commit in bypass, and no component boundary, persistence format, trust or credential boundary or external
    interface changes. No requirement ID changes. Residual risk: none beyond R-52, which is narrowed.

45\. Verification commands under push policy `branch` (2026-10-13; answers Q-57). **zemdregon's words, verbatim, are
    only this:** "Stick with your recommendation"
    *Delegated wording of the recommendation, not his words, and not owner-approved beyond his answer.* The agent put
    four options for Q-57 to zemdregon: whether verification commands should be refused, or have `HOME` withheld, for
    a run whose project context has push policy `branch`. The recommendation he accepted:

    1. **Approved bypass runs:** host verification stays allowed as it is today. The R-61 exposure (a verification
       command can read the operator's Git configuration, SSH keys and credential files, and push) is accepted for
       zemdregon's own workloads, consistent with decisions 25, 37, 41 and 44: bypass is deliberately loose.
    2. **Other deployments:** verification is not broken now. Once T3.11 (confined verification inside the outer sandbox
       from T3.1) exists, a refusal or `HOME`-withholding rule for push policy `branch` is added as an opt-in setting.
       *Proposed:* no task exists yet; the setting will be specified when T3.1 and T3.11 are scheduled. No config key
       name or number is chosen here.

    No code change follows from this decision. The README verification section and R-61 already state the exposure. No
    requirement ID changes and no ADR: no boundary, persistence format or interface changes. Residual risk: R-61,
    accepted by zemdregon for approved bypass runs; still open for other deployments.

46\. Bypass on a provider without documented Git-write denial (2026-10-13; answers Q-58). **zemdregon's words,
verbatim, are only this:** "Yes"
    *Delegated wording of what he answered, not his words, and not owner-approved beyond his answer.* An approved
    `privileged` scope with `bypass` (ADR-0017) **may compile on a `host` project** for a provider whose Git-write
    control (FR-GIT-01) is `none-documented`, or whose bypass mode is not shown to keep its deny rules, relying only on
    FR-GIT-03 (ref snapshots and violation handling) and FR-GIT-06 (the push and credential rules). It is no longer
    `Ineligible` until the provider's live leg shows denial holding. The fail-closed default proposed in Q-58 is **not
    adopted**. Consistent with decisions 22 (Git-write denial and worktree isolation stay where the provider can
    express them), 25 and 37.

    1. **Consequences, stated plainly.** In such a run the agent may write Git refs and history directly. The T9.5
       check detects ref moves at FINALIZING and restores controller-owned refs only; other refs are reported, not
       restored, and BR-27 does not fail the run. Worktree isolation (FR-WS-05) and the BR-31 push rules still apply.
    2. **Unchanged.** A provider whose bypass flags are undocumented stays unavailable for that reason (as Codex's
       cell is, T3.6), not for Git-write denial. Non-bypass modes are unaffected, and so are `container` projects
       (M10).
    3. **The live leg still runs** (`T4.n8`, T3.9 checks): it records whether denial holds, as evidence and for the
       manifest notes, but no longer gates eligibility.

    No requirement ID changes and no ADR: no boundary, persistence format or interface changes (decision 22 already
    accepts the less restrictive reading). Residual risk: R-38 (dated note), accepted by zemdregon for his own bypass
    runs; no new R number.

47\. Antigravity terms reviewed (2026-10-13; answers Q-59). **zemdregon's words, verbatim, are only this:** "I have
reviewed the terms."
    *Delegated wording of what he answered, not his words, and not owner-approved beyond his answer.* zemdregon has read
    Google's terms for his own use. The Antigravity adapter (T4.32 onward) therefore **need not ship disabled by
    default** on his account. He did not say which plan, or that the terms permit the adapter, so nothing here is a
    compliance statement: **the README still makes no compliance claim** (NFR-COMP-01, R-16) and tells other operators to
    review Google's terms for their own plan. The separate ACP server of TDD §4.4.4 is covered by the same answer only
    if it falls under the same terms, which was not asked (Assumption). No requirement ID changes.

48\. ACP permission requests in an approved bypass run are auto-approved (2026-10-13; answers Q-60). **zemdregon's
words, verbatim, are only this:** "bypass runs dont need permissions requests, aka auto approve"
    *Delegated wording of what he answered, not his words, and not owner-approved beyond his answer.* In an approved
    `privileged` scope with `bypass` (ADR-0017), the controller answers **every** `session/request_permission` with
    `allow_once` regardless of kind or location ([ADR-0036](adr/0036-per-run-acp-stdio-client.md) item 9.4). That rule is
    bypass-class under FR-POL-03 and is reachable only with `bypassApproved` true. ACP bypass is no longer held
    `Ineligible` for want of this answer (it can compile once an ACP adapter exists, T4.8 onward).
    1. **Delegated detail, not asked:** `allow_always` is still never selected, because a remembered grant would outlive
       the run and its snapshot (FR-POL-08) and the run loses nothing by being asked again. Revisit if a provider
       re-asks so often that runs stall.
    2. **Delegated detail, not asked:** the optional extra layer that rejects requests recognisably Git writes is **not
       adopted**; his answer was auto-approve, and decision 46 already accepts that a bypass agent may write refs. The
       Git-write denial and worktree isolation that decision 22 keeps are enforced by the provider's own controls,
       the ref snapshots (FR-GIT-03) and the worktree (FR-WS-05), not by permission answers.
    3. Non-bypass profiles are unchanged: deny by default from the frozen profile.
    No new ADR (ADR-0036 is still Proposed and is amended in place); residual risk is covered by R-38 and R-62.

Decisions 10 to 13, 17, 19, 21 and 22 each choose the less restrictive of the options put to zemdregon (decision 18 is the
principle behind the 2026-10-13 ones); decisions 20 and 23 keep the stricter behaviour already implemented. Their residual
risks are recorded for zemdregon as R-23 to R-26, R-34, R-38 and R-39 in §7.1 (not blocking); decision 24's as R-38 and
R-40; decision 26's as R-41; decision 27's as R-35 (mitigated); decision 28's as R-42 (mitigated);
decision 29 has none beyond the stale-for-TTL window noted in it; decision 30 closes R-44; decision 32's is R-45 (not blocking);
decision 33's is R-46 (not blocking); decision 34's is R-47 (not blocking); decision 35's are R-48 and R-49 (not
blocking); decision 36's is R-50 (not blocking); decision 37's is R-51 (accepted by the owner for bypass); decision
38's is R-52 (not blocking); decision 39's are R-53 and R-54 (not blocking); decision 40's are R-55 to R-58
(not blocking); decision 41's is R-59 (not blocking); decisions 42 and 43 have none, and their open question is Q-55;
decision 44 has none beyond R-52; decision 45's is R-61 (accepted by zemdregon for approved bypass runs; open for
other deployments); decision 46's is R-38 (dated note; accepted by zemdregon for his own bypass runs, not blocking).

### 4.3 Stated intent (confirmed constraints)

From the [original blueprint's
introduction](research/headless-ai-coding-controller.md#introduction-to-the-cli-native-orchestration-paradigm):

- C-1 Wrap the seven provider CLIs through their headless or documented automation modes, not raw model APIs.
- C-2 No web interface, except restricted dashboards purely for usage monitoring and telemetry.
- C-3 Never interact with OAuth or subscription login flows; rely on credentials cached by the native CLIs.
- C-4 Own model definitions and route by task complexity.
- C-5 Tier-aware load balancing across subscriptions.
- C-6 Persistent cross-provider meta-sessions.
- C-7 Expose capabilities to a primary orchestrator (for example OpenClaw or Grok Bot) as an MCP or ACP server.

### 4.4 Material delegated decisions

| Decision | Record |
| --- | --- |
| The verified research supersedes the original blueprint wherever they conflict (see §4.6). | This roadmap; banner on the original research doc |
| Controller-owned canonical state; no transcript parsing | [ADR-0001](adr/0001-controller-owned-canonical-state.md) |
| MCP upstream (async-first tools); ACP as downstream transport; upstream ACP deferred | [ADR-0002](adr/0002-mcp-upstream-acp-downstream.md) |
| No credential handling; compliance manifests; no "ToS compliant" claims | [ADR-0003](adr/0003-no-credential-handling.md) |
| Five trust profiles; no automatic bypass flags | [ADR-0004](adr/0004-trust-profiles-no-bypass-flags.md) |
| SQLite ledger | [ADR-0005](adr/0005-sqlite-ledger.md) |
| Worktree per write-capable run; no blind replay | [ADR-0006](adr/0006-worktree-checkpoints-no-blind-replay.md) |
| Capacity as confidence estimate; bounded priority queue | [ADR-0007](adr/0007-capacity-as-confidence-estimate.md) |
| Two-stage routing; learned routing deferred | [ADR-0008](adr/0008-two-stage-routing.md) |
| Version-gated adapters | [ADR-0009](adr/0009-version-gated-adapters.md) |
| TypeScript on Node.js | [ADR-0010](adr/0010-typescript-node-stack.md) |
| Single-operator, local, Linux-first | [ADR-0011](adr/0011-single-operator-local-deployment.md) |
| Telemetry at the wrapper boundary | [ADR-0012](adr/0012-telemetry-at-wrapper-boundary.md) |
| Projects (environment plus contexts) defined in configuration; `host` environments first, `container` with a Proposed rootless runtime (Q-6, delegated); an orchestrator can create projects only within its client's limits and with operator approval for containers and push | [ADR-0013](adr/0013-projects-environments-and-contexts.md) |
| Agents never commit or push; injected `commit` and `push` tools do, push off by default; published commits sanitised and separate from internal checkpoints; provenance kept in the ledger | [ADR-0014](adr/0014-tool-only-commits-and-publication.md) |
| Operator CLI commands run as short-lived processes that write only operator records; one `serve` daemon per data directory performs all side effects | [ADR-0005](adr/0005-sqlite-ledger.md), [SDD §6](design/SDD.md#6-design-questions-settled-during-reconciliation) |
| `adapters validate` moved from M8 to M4 so new CLI versions can be accepted as soon as most adapters exist | This roadmap, FR-OPS-02 |
| `privileged_scope` is machine-readable (2026-10-13; zemdregon left Q-28 to the agent: "pick the best choice"). Each dimension is a closed enum, with an argv-exact command list or host list where the mode is `allowlist`; adapters compile the privileged profile from it and return `Ineligible` with a reason for any dimension they cannot express. Rationale: a free-text scope cannot be compiled by adapters, cannot be validated or compared, and makes the scope hash cover words rather than behaviour | [LLD §3.1](design/LLD.md#31-privileged-scope-m3), [ADR-0017](adr/0017-approved-privileged-scope-bypass.md) (amended before merge) |
| Delivery order: a thin MCP vertical slice with one adapter (M2) comes *before* adapter expansion, unlike the research's stage order where the MCP protocol came seventh. Rationale: the orchestrator can use and test the controller early; routing and capacity still come after execution semantics are trustworthy, which is the research's actual concern. | This roadmap |

### 4.5 Assumptions

| ID | Assumption | Impact if wrong |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A-1 | *Resolved by owner decision 4.* Each installation serves one operator using that operator's own logins; open-source users run their own installations. Hosted or shared multi-user operation remains out of scope. | A hosted service would change compliance scope, auth model and deployment (ADR-0011, ADR-0003). |
| A-2 | Linux is the first host; macOS follows in M4 because open-source users are likely to run it. | If most users turn out to be on macOS, move NFR-PORT-02 earlier. |
| A-3 | *Resolved by owner decision 5:* Claude Code and Codex are the M2 adapters. | — |
| A-4 | The first orchestrator is any stdio MCP client; acceptance tests use a scripted MCP test client rather than a specific product. | A specific orchestrator may need HTTP transport or different tool shapes. |
| A-5 | *Resolved by owner decision 5:* all seven subscriptions are held and all seven adapters are Must. | — |

### 4.6 Conflicts between sources and how they were resolved

Resolution rule: the verified research is a direct, dated review of the original blueprint against provider
documentation, so its technical findings win. Where the original carries zemdregon's *stated intent* (§4.3), the intent
is kept and only the mechanism changes.

| Topic | Original blueprint | Verified research | Resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Permission flags | `--allowedTools "*"`, `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--yolo`, `--trust --approve-mcps --force` | `"*"` is skipped; bypass flags grant too much; trust, MCP approval, command approval and sandbox are separate | Trust profiles, no automatic bypass ([ADR-0004](adr/0004-trust-profiles-no-bypass-flags.md)) |
| Codex interface | `codex -p`, custom JSON wrapper | `codex exec --json` emits JSONL natively | Use native JSONL (TDD §4) |
| Kimi Code | `kimi run`, K2.7, 256K context | Legacy CLI archived 2026-09-23; new contract unknown | Adapter blocked until pinned (FR-ADP-09) |
| Context windows | Hard-coded per CLI | Model properties, change over time | Model registry (FR-ADP-05) |
| Cross-provider state | Parse provider transcripts, summarize trajectories | Controller-owned state; Git is canonical | [ADR-0001](adr/0001-controller-owned-canonical-state.md); intent C-6 kept |
| Quota / NCU | NCU as precise share of remaining quota | Only confidence-weighted estimates are defensible | [ADR-0007](adr/0007-capacity-as-confidence-estimate.md); intent C-5 kept |
| Rate limiting | Leaky bucket ingress, token bucket egress, blind retry | Priority queue, semaphores, breakers; no replay after mutation | [ADR-0006](adr/0006-worktree-checkpoints-no-blind-replay.md), [ADR-0007](adr/0007-capacity-as-confidence-estimate.md) |
| Routing | Brand-to-tier table, IRT | Eligibility then empirical scoring | [ADR-0008](adr/0008-two-stage-routing.md); intent C-4 kept |
| Telemetry | Inject global Claude hooks | Observe at wrapper boundary | [ADR-0012](adr/0012-telemetry-at-wrapper-boundary.md) |
| MCP vs ACP | "MCP or ACP server" | MCP upstream, ACP downstream | [ADR-0002](adr/0002-mcp-upstream-acp-downstream.md); intent C-7 met via MCP |
| Compliance claim | "Absolute ToS compliance" | Documented automation is not a blanket contractual grant | NFR-COMP-01 |
| OpenClaw "Lane Queue" | Relied on for write serialization | Could not be verified | Controller enforces its own leases (FR-WS-02) |
| "Frees 96% of context" | Claimed | Not defensible | Dropped |

### 6.1 M1 Foundation: tasks

Each task lists objective, references, dependencies and completion checks. Tasks are in dependency order; T1.3 to T1.6
can run in parallel after T1.2.

Task status: **T1.1 to T1.11 Done** (2026-10-12). Evidence is local: `npm test` passes (13 files, 166 tests),
alongside typecheck, lint and `node scripts/check-docs.mjs`. Caveats: the first CI run is unconfirmed, and GitHub
Actions are not yet pinned by SHA (`setup-node` uses a tag).

- **T1.1:** `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `tsconfig.json`, `biome.json`, `vitest.config.ts`,
  `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; versions recorded in [TDD §2](design/TDD.md#2-technology-choices).
- **T1.2:** `src/config/` and `src/lock/`; `src/config/config.test.ts` (precise rejection messages, symlink
  canonicalization), `src/lock/lock.test.ts` (second acquisition fails clearly, stale lock recovered).
- **T1.3:** `src/ledger/`, `src/ids/`; `src/ledger/ledger.test.ts` (migration from empty DB, WAL, append-only
  triggers on `run_event`), `src/ids/ids.test.ts`.
- **T1.4:** `src/artifacts/`; `src/artifacts/artifacts.test.ts` (SHA-256 addressing, redaction before write).
- **T1.5:** `src/workspace/`; `src/workspace/workspace.test.ts` (second lease refused, operator tree untouched, mutated
  failure rolled back, non-Git refused for write profiles).
- **T1.6:** `src/exec/`; `src/exec/supervisor.test.ts` (child and grandchild killed on cancel, env allowlist, oversize
  and malformed lines give `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`), `src/exec/jsonl.test.ts`.
- **T1.7:** `src/adapters/`; `src/adapters/adapters.test.ts` (fake adapter scenarios, conformance harness, no bypass
  flags in compiled argv).
- **T1.8:** `src/lifecycle/`; `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.test.ts` (state-machine table, admission, queue bound,
  priority, concurrency, one active run per meta-session, per-edge outcomes, cancel in each state).
- **T1.9:** `src/reconcile/`; `src/reconcile/reconcile.test.ts` (real `SIGKILL` of a controller child during RUNNING,
  restart ends `FAILED/PROCESS_CRASH`, orphan killed, PID-reuse guard, per-state WF-8 outcomes).
- **T1.10:** `src/cli/`; `src/cli/cli.test.ts` (no login command, `doctor`, `runs list|show|cancel` with `--json`,
  cancel with and without a lock holder, daemon-side application).
- **T1.11:** this reconciliation; design-doc changes are listed in the change description and carried in the documents
  named in §6.1.1.

#### 6.1.1 M1 requirements not fully evidenced

These requirements are in the M1 row of §6, but the evidence above does not fully cover them. They stay `Partial`
until the gap closes; none blocks M2 work.

| Requirement | Status | Gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FR-OPS-01 | Partial | `doctor`, `runs list`, `runs show`, `runs cancel` are tested; `serve` is M2 (T2.6) |
| NFR-PORT-01 | Partial | CI targets `ubuntu-24.04` (x86-64) only, the first CI run is unconfirmed, and arm64 is untested (TDD §2 says an arm64 runner is to be confirmed) |
| NFR-SEC-01 | Partial | **Scan added (2026-10-14, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).** `test/secret-scan.test.ts` runs full fake-adapter runs (success, failure, file edit) through the real lifecycle and MCP server, the operator CLI (`runs list`, `runs show`, `doctor`, text and `--json`) and a real `serve` child process, with synthetic canary credentials in the controller environment (outside the allowlist), in provider output (tool command, message, stderr, error text, summary, written file) and in the prompt. It scans every row of every ledger table, every file under the data directory (SQLite file and WAL included; worktrees excluded), MCP responses, CLI output and the daemon log, for the exact canaries and for the redactor patterns. The scan found two real leaks, now fixed in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`: provider-reported `summary` (in `run_event` payloads and the finalize commit message) and `failure_detail` (in the `run` row, so in `runs show`) were stored unredacted. **Objective redacted at admission (2026-10-14, Q-68 answered by the orchestrator; Delegated, not owner-approved; [ADR-0042](adr/0042-objective-redaction-at-admission.md)):** `RunLifecycle.validate` (`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`) applies the artifact secret redactor to the objective and constraints before they are stored, keeps no original, and records the count as `admission_redactions` in `run.request_json`; the stored text is what the first run and every continuation send. Evidence: the former `it.fails` case in `test/secret-scan.test.ts` is a normal passing test (the prompt credential is absent from every ledger column and from the raw SQLite file and WAL, in-process and under `serve`), and `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.test.ts` ("objective redaction at admission (Q-68, NFR-SEC-01)": stored and compiled text redacted with the count, a clean request stored unchanged without the field, a continuation's instruction and inherited constraints redacted); both new lifecycle cases fail against the unpatched lifecycle. **Still Partial (Delegated assessment):** the scan drives the fake adapter only, so no real adapter's output has been scanned, and the redactor patterns are best effort |
| FR-TEL-03 | Partial | `test/secret-scan.test.ts` asserts that the prompt text and every environment value are absent from `run_event` payloads, artifacts, MCP responses, CLI output and the `serve` log (evidence as in NFR-SEC-01; Delegated agent work). **Still open:** no telemetry exporter exists yet, so the attribute-allowlist test of [LLD §7](design/LLD.md#7-telemetry-spans) has nothing to run against; the stored objective is excluded from the prompt check by design |
| NFR-SEC-04 | Partial | Inspection-only requirement; no check or recorded review that the controller runs unprivileged |
| NFR-PRIV-01 | Partial | Inspection-only requirement; no recorded review (no network code exists, but nothing asserts it) |
| NFR-SEC-06 | Partial | Fail-closed cases are tested (unreadable worktree, unreadable pending outcome, bypass flag, unknown schema version); "ledger cannot be opened, refuse to serve" waits for `serve` |
