**Blocks Done.** M2 stays `Partial` against its acceptance criteria because:

. The opt-in live smoke test (`npm run test:live`) has not been run by the operator against real Claude Code or Codex.
   Event shapes, error categories, flag spellings and auth behaviour are therefore unconfirmed (R-21, R-22, Q-10, Q-13),
   and the criteria "polls to `SUCCEEDED`, retrieves a diff artifact and continues the same native session" and
   "missing login yields `AUTH_REQUIRED`" are shown on fakes and synthetic fixtures only.
2. Codex cannot pass the version gate or `doctor` until an operator records a real `codex --version` in its manifest
   range (Q-12). `switchboard adapters validate` (T4.4) records one; running it for Codex is the operator task T4.11
   (FR-OPS-02). *Update 2026-10-14 (operator run on Ampere):* Claude Code 2.1.294 is validated by the command
   (`src/adapters/manifests/claude.json` evidence line; range `>=2.1.294 <2.2.0`). Codex 0.162.0 is validated too:
   `adapters validate codex` passed after the operator's re-login (an earlier attempt failed at the smoke step with
   a 401 from an expired login and wrote nothing); the range is now `>=0.162.0 <0.163.0`. T4.11 is done for both
   providers.
   providers `[warn]`, exit 0, which proves the environment and config only). A machine with a provider installed has
   not been checked, and an installed Codex fails `doctor` by design today.
4. FR-GIT-01 (Claude Code, Codex) and the M2 criterion "no compiled invocation leaves a Git write operation (commit,
   ref change, push) permitted" are shown by argv inspection only (T2.2 row). Claude Code: every invocation carries
   `--disallowedTools` Git write deny rules and `--restricted`, has no Bash tool without an allowlist, and an allowlist
   entry that could write Git is `Ineligible`; that the CLI accepts and enforces the rules as assumed is unverified.
   Codex: no control is documented, so the adapter declares `none-documented` and a non-empty allowlist is
   `Ineligible`; the remaining controls are FR-GIT-03 (built for every worktree run by T9.5 on 2026-10-13, tested with
   the fake adapter only) and FR-GIT-06, and whether the
   `workspace-write` sandbox blocks the worktree's Git directory is an Assumption. The live smoke test now reports
   G-1 (no foreign ref or commit after a benign run), C-G2 and X-G1 (`NOT_CHECKED`: they need a deliberate Git write
   attempt; T9.5 added the detection and fake-adapter fixtures, but C-G2 and X-G1 still need a live provider). No
   provider flag disables commit attribution (R-19).

Criteria met on fakes or fixtures: "unknown CLI version yields `ADAPTER_VERSION_UNVERIFIED`" (manifest, routing, MCP and
`doctor` tests) and "no bypass flag appears in any compiled invocation" (argv tests in `claude.test.ts`, `codex.test.ts`,
`policy.test.ts`).

it); `require_provider` naming a provider outside `allowed_providers` is `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` (SRS FR-PRJ-06 wording)
while a set that leaves no registered provider is `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` (FRD BR-23 "ineligible"); `list_projects` also
returns `primary` per context and `effective_max_profile`. The project's `base_ref` now starts the first worktree of a
meta-session instead of `HEAD` (`src/workspace/manager.ts`). Scope kept out: push policy, commit identity and image
digest in the snapshot (T9.3 onward, M10), `manage_projects` and dynamic projects (T9.9). `allowed_providers` uses
config ids (`claude-code`), mapped to adapter ids by the existing `CONFIG_TO_ADAPTER_ID`; the test-only `fake` adapter
is never allowed by a project that lists providers. Persistence:
[ADR-0024](adr/0024-project-id-columns-and-project-snapshot.md).

**T9.3 status (2026-10-13; Delegated): Done** for the controller-side library and ledger; every "Done when" clause has a

Not run against a live provider.

**T9.5 status (2026-10-13; Delegated): Done for `host` runs on fakes (Git 2.43.0); not run against a live provider.**
"Done when", clause by clause, all in `src/lifecycle/ref-guard.test.ts` (fake adapter scenario `git_attempts`, plain Git
commands in the run worktree against temporary local repositories and a local bare remote):
*every scripted attempt leaves the publish ref unchanged* where it must: `git update-ref` on the publish ref is undone
(deleted when the controller never published, put back to the tool commit otherwise), and a tool commit that T9.3
parented on an agent-moved publish head is not kept published; `git commit` and `git reset --hard` on the run branch are
put back to the pre-run commit, with the agent's tree kept as the checkpoint's content; `git checkout -b` gets the
worktree `HEAD` pointed back; `git branch`, `git tag`, `git update-ref` on the default branch and `git push` to the bare
remote (seen through the remote-tracking ref) are reported and left as they are (operator refs are never changed;
FR-WS-05, ADR-0027). *Appends `git_policy_violation`*: every case above asserts the event and its entries (scope, kind,
ref, old and new SHA, foreign commits; no message text). *Legitimate tool commits are not flagged*: "legitimate tool
commits (T9.4 channel) and the controller checkpoint are not flagged". Also: no-op runs record a clean check; the
violation does not change the outcome (BR-27) and the terminal payload and `runs show` flag it (`src/cli/cli.test.ts`,
"runs show flags Git policy violations"); the LEASED snapshot survives a SIGKILL restart and reconciliation flags and
restores before the evidence checkpoint (`src/reconcile/reconcile.test.ts`, "T9.5: the LEASED ref snapshot survives
..."); large ref counts are capped (5000 refs, 1 MiB; tail digest) and other runs' controller moves are not flagged,
the check is idempotent, an unreadable repository is `check_failed`, a missing snapshot is `snapshot_missing`, and a
privileged bypass run is handled identically (`src/git/ref-guard.test.ts`). Code: `src/git/ref-guard.ts`, wiring in
`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` and `src/reconcile/reconcile.ts`, `runs show` in `src/cli/runs.ts`, snapshot list hidden
from `get_run` in `src/mcp/envelope.ts`. Decision 34, [ADR-0027](adr/0027-ref-snapshots-and-violation-handling.md).
**Not covered:** Claude Code and Codex runs (no live provider; their compiled deny rules are unchanged and the guard runs
for every worktree run whatever the adapter, so C-G2 and X-G1 in the live smoke test remain to be run by the operator),
per-worktree refs (`refs/worktree/`, `refs/bisect/`) and objects written without a ref (FR-GIT-03 is about refs), a
push that updates no local ref, and adversarial escape scenarios (T3.8b, out of scope). False positives from operator
activity are accepted (R-47); push after a violation is Q-44.

*policy `none` offers no tool*: `src/lifecycle/git-push.test.ts` ("policy none offers no push tool ...": the channel

the same (`src/cli/git-push.test.ts`, "pushes the publish ref under the prefix ..."). *Force, default branch, tags and

a tag or `+` target ("the argv never forces ..."); a non-fast-forward is rejected by Git and recorded as

remote's `HEAD` branch or the base ref is `TARGET_PROTECTED`; a publish ref moved onto an agent commit or onto the run

`push`*: a `broad` shell and a non-empty allowlist each give `git_push_withheld` with the reason, no `push` tool and no
push (`src/lifecycle/git-push.test.ts`); the rule's table, including `privileged` scopes, bypass and `container`, is in
`src/git/push.test.ts` ("credential-reach rule"). Also covered: a URL or credential-bearing value as the remote, a
configured remote whose URL carries credentials or uses `http`/`ext::`, and an unknown remote are refused, and none of
those values, nor the remote's path, appears in the ledger or data directory; the config loader refuses a URL remote
without echoing it; repository-configured `core.sshCommand`, `credential.helper`, `receivepack`, `insteadOf`,
`protocol.*` and `http.sslVerify` refuse the push and run nothing; `pre-push`, `reference-transaction` and `post-update`
hooks in `.git/hooks` and in a repository `core.hooksPath` never run; a failing `pre-receive` hook in the bare remote is

passing BR-31 verdict is refused with the reason; the current configuration is re-read at call time; push arguments
and attempts beyond 10 per run are refused; concurrent pushes are serialised; every attempt is a `git_push` row and run
event (append-only triggers checked); the operator command refuses while the lock is held and writes nothing; `runs
show` prints commit provenance (SHA, tree, patch ID, project, context, session, run, provider, harness, model, adapter

still shows the `git_push` events). Host runs with a shell cannot
use the agent `push` tool (BR-31): the operator command, or a `container` environment once M10 exists, is the route.

*Amendment 2026-10-13 (decision 37; Delegated reading of zemdregon's "BR-31. Don't block git operations on bypass mode").*
An approved bypass run (scope `bypass` and a controller-verified approval) is exempt from BR-31 and is offered `push`
whatever its shell: `evaluateCredentialReach` takes the controller-held `approvedBypass` fact (from `bypassApprovedFor`,
at channel open in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` and at every call in `src/git/channel.ts`) and returns `ok` with the
reason `bypass_exempt`, written to the `git_channel_opened` event and to each `git_push` event (`credential_reach`) and
row (`detail`, so `runs show` shows it). Evidence: `src/lifecycle/git-push.test.ts` ("approved bypass runs ...": an
approved bypass run with a broad shell pushes only the publish ref to a local bare remote and records the exemption;
policy `none` offers nothing; an approved scope without `bypass` still gets `shell_broad`; an unapproved bypass run
never starts) and `src/git/push.test.ts` ("credential-reach rule": exempt only with the controller fact, forged
snapshot fields ignored, policy and container checks first; an exempt verdict still refuses after a violation and
records the exemption in event and row). Unchanged: the push process, refspec, never-force, provenance, protected
targets, limits, Q-44, the provider's Git-write denial (decision 22) and non-bypass runs. Still unverified: nothing ran
against a live provider or a network remote.

covered:** Claude Code and Codex runs (no live provider; their `commitChannel` stays `none`, so they get the mode when

*Fix, 2026-10-13 (Delegated):* a live run showed `switchboard gc` and `doctor` reporting the publish refs
`switchboard/pub/<sess>` of sessions that have ledger rows as orphans, because the orphan sweep (`findOrphans`,
`src/workspace/gc.ts`) knew only run and checkpoint branches. It now skips a publish ref whose session exists (tests in
`src/workspace/gc.test.ts`); an unknown session's publish ref is still reported. Open question: LLD §5 says retention
GC collects the publish refs with their meta-session from M9, but `runRetentionGc` deletes only `switchboard/<sess>/`
branches, so an expired session's publish ref is kept. That is deliberate for now (the ref holds the delivered
commits); deleting it needs an owner decision.

the client rejected:* "rejects ceiling, provider, push, paths outside the roots and inside the data dir" (including
`..` and a symlink escape) and "a client cannot raise its own limits through a definition"; rule units in
`src/projects/projects.test.ts`. *`container` and push revisions stay `PENDING_APPROVAL` until `projects approve`:* "a
push or container revision is PENDING_APPROVAL and cannot start runs until approved" (no run row until the decision; an
approved `container` revision still fails `ENVIRONMENT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` until M10), and the command itself in
`src/cli/projects-dynamic.test.ts` ("projects approve": single revision, `--revision` required with several, superseded
and repeated decisions refused, `--reject`, configured and unknown ids, written while a daemon holds the lock).
*Delete with an active run refused:* "delete is refused while a run is active, allowed after; history stays; the id is
not reused". *Every call is an event:* "every call is one event, accepted or refused, and survives a new server on the
same ledger" and the malformed-input case. Also covered: widening an approved revision returns to pending with the old
revision in force and a narrowing needs no approval; rejection; revalidation at admission (a repository swapped for an
escaping symlink, the client's `max_push_policy` lowered, `manage_projects` removed); the run's policy snapshot has the
same shape as for a configured project; append-only guards and checks on all three tables and the upgrade from schema
version 3 (`src/projects/projects.test.ts`); restart persistence; malformed input (oversized strings and arrays,
look-alike and upper-case ids, `__proto__` and `constructor` keys, NUL, newline and bidirectional characters) is
refused, stores no malformed id and pollutes no prototype; config keys in `src/config/config.test.ts`. Code:
`src/projects/` (store, rules), `src/mcp/project-tools.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/mcp/schemas.ts`,
`src/cli/projects-dynamic.ts`, `src/cli/main.ts`, `src/cli/serve.ts`, `src/config/schema.ts`, `src/config/load.ts`,
`src/ledger/migrations/0004_project_revision.ts`. [ADR-0030](adr/0030-dynamic-project-revisions-and-approval.md).
**Not covered:** pushing from an approved dynamic project (refused at push time, Q-46), `container` environments
(M10), and adversarial escape scenarios beyond these validation cases (T3.8b). Nothing ran against a live provider.

**T9.10 status (2026-10-13; Delegated, [ADR-0034](adr/0034-controller-side-commit-signing.md)): Done for `host` runs
on fakes, verified only with GnuPG and a throwaway unprotected key (Git 2.43.0, GnuPG 2.4.4); not run against a live
provider or the operator's real key or agent.** "Done when", clause by clause: *a signed commit verifies*:
`src/git/commit-sign.test.ts` ("signs the published commit; it verifies ...": `git verify-commit` passes, `%G?` is `G`
and the signer is the throwaway key, the message and identity are unchanged and carry no run or session id, the
`git_commit_intent` and `git_commit` events add `signed: true`, and the ledger holds neither the fingerprint nor the
keyring or home path), end to end through the real shim and through finalize-only in `src/lifecycle/git-sign.test.ts`
("the commit tool's commit is signed and verifies ...", "finalize-only signs its commit too"), and the signing step
alone in `src/git/sign.test.ts`. *Failure returns `SIGNING_FAILED` and leaves the publish ref unchanged*: an unknown
key, a missing signing program and a hanging one (timeout) fail with no commit object, no `git_commit_intent`, the
publish ref where it was and a `git_commit_signing_failed` event holding reason codes only (`src/git/sign.test.ts`,
`src/git/commit-sign.test.ts`, and the agent's `commit` call answered `SIGNING_FAILED` in
`src/lifecycle/git-sign.test.ts`).
*The key and agent variables never reach the provider process (environment inspection)*: with `HOME`, `GNUPGHOME`,
`SSH_AUTH_SOCK` and `GPG_AGENT_INFO` set in the controller process, the fake provider reports its environment names and
none of them (nor any `GPG*`, `GNUPG*` or `SSH_*` name) is there while the same run's commit is signed
(`src/lifecycle/git-sign.test.ts`); the Claude Code and Codex invocations' allowlists name none of
`SIGNING_AGENT_VARIABLES` (`src/adapters/claude/claude.test.ts`, `src/adapters/codex/codex.test.ts`); the signing
environment is an allowlist that drops every inherited `GIT_*`, `DISPLAY` and the tool token (`src/git/sign.test.ts`).
*Refused where BR-31 withholds `push`*: `evaluateSigningReach` returns the push rule's verdict for every case once push
policy is `branch` (decision 37 exemption included) and needs no push policy itself (`src/git/commit-sign.test.ts`); a
`host` run with a broad shell gets `SIGNING_FAILED` (`credential_reach`, `shell_broad`) on both paths and publishes
nothing (`src/lifecycle/git-sign.test.ts`). *`projects validate` test signature*: `src/cli/projects.test.ts` ("projects
validate: commit signing": passes with the operator's setup, `SIGNING_UNAVAILABLE` without a usable key, no fingerprint
or keyring path printed; `SIGNING_CONFIG_UNSAFE` for a repository `gpg.program`, never run; `sign` refused by the loader
without an identity or outside the primary writable context). Repository configuration: a malicious repository-local
`gpg.program` (also through `include.path` or `config.worktree`) is refused and never runs, and with `sign` off a
repository `gpg.program` and `commit.gpgsign = true`, or an operator configuration that signs every commit, change
nothing (`src/git/commit-sign.test.ts`, `src/git/sign.test.ts`). With `sign` off the snapshot, the code path and the
event payloads are unchanged (the existing T9.3 to T9.7 suites pass unmodified). Code: `src/git/sign.ts`,
`src/git/commit.ts`, `src/git/credential-reach.ts`, `src/git/channel.ts`, `src/git/finalize.ts`,
`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/policy/types.ts`, `src/config/projects.ts`,
`src/cli/projects.ts`; test fixture `src/git/fixtures/throwaway-gpg.ts` and the fake CLI's `report_env` plan flag.
**Not covered:** SSH-format, X.509, passphrase-protected and hardware keys; the operator's real keyring or agent; an
operator `includeIf "gitdir:..."` that selects the key by repository path (not applied, LLD §12.3.1); `container`
environments (M10); Claude Code and Codex end to end (no live run). Q-56 answered Yes (the decision 37 exemption for
signing), R-60.

**T9.11 status (2026-10-13; Delegated, owner decision 42): Done for `host` environments (FR-WS-08); FR-WS-09 not
started (Proposed, not accepted; Q-55 open).** Finding: the existing model already met FR-WS-08. Worktrees and
controller branches are keyed by meta-session and run, the write lease is one column pair on the `meta_session` row,
and a meta-session has at most one non-terminal run (FR-SES-06), so concurrent runs of one project are always in
different meta-sessions with their own worktree, branch and lease; nothing serialises or shares them by project or
context, and the FR-WS-02 rule (one write lease per worktree) is unchanged. No ADR: no boundary, persistence format or
interface changes. "Done when", clause by clause, in `src/lifecycle/concurrent-worktrees.test.ts` (real Git in a
temporary directory, a gated fake CLI that holds both runs RUNNING at once, no provider):
*two concurrent runs in one project get distinct worktrees and leases*: "two concurrent runs of a project: own
worktrees, branches and leases; GC of one keeps the other" (distinct `<data_dir>/worktrees/<sess>/<run>` paths and
`switchboard/<sess>/<run>` branches, both registered with `git worktree list`, `lease_run_id` set on both meta-session
rows at once with one `lease` event each, each run's file only in its own worktree and never in the operator's
checkout; retention GC of the finished session removes its worktree and branch while the other run keeps running with
its worktree, branch, file and lease intact; no `git_policy_violation` on either run; operator `HEAD` and tree
unchanged) and "no sharing across meta-sessions: a continuation gets its own worktree, an active session refuses"
(`continue_task` into a meta-session with an active run is `INVALID_REQUEST`; a continuation of another meta-session
while the first runs gets a third worktree and branch from its own session head, with its own lease).
*A `container` run mounts only its own worktree*: **not testable until M10**; container environments are not built and
a `container` project fails `projects validate` and admission (`ENVIRONMENT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`). FR-WS-08's SRS row
notes the `container` clause as M10. *FR-WS-09 tests stay skipped*: three placeholders in the same file under
`describe.skipIf(!FR_WS_09_ACCEPTED)`, titled with the reason (Proposed, awaiting owner acceptance, merge trigger
Q-55); they throw if enabled without an implementation. FRD §4.4 is unchanged.
**Defect found and fixed (Delegated):** with two runs in one repository, one run's FINALIZING ref check (T9.5) could
run while the other run's checkpoint commit had moved its branch but its `post` row was not yet recorded, and reported
that controller move as a `switchboard` `git_policy_violation` (which BR-27 then holds against the innocent run's
meta-session, for example skipping a finalize-only commit). The lifecycle now runs the ref check, the mutation
assessment and the checkpoint of each run as one critical section per repository root (`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`,
`serialisedPerRepo`; LLD §5 and §12.5 as-built notes). Regression test: "a ref check never sees another run's
checkpoint before its post row (no false violation)", which fails without the fix. **Second defect found and fixed
(2026-10-13, Delegated):** concurrent `git worktree add` calls on one repository raced inside Git, which takes no lock
on `.git/worktrees/`; one run failed at LEASED with `TOOL_FAILURE` ("workspace preparation failed: ... fatal: failed to
read .git/worktrees/<other run>/commondir: Success", or "fatal: could not open '.git/worktrees/<run>/locked' for
writing" when a sibling's `git worktree prune` removed the new admin directory). This was the cause of the intermittent
`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.test.ts` failures ("isolates a controller-side failure to its run", "never holds more slots
than the global limit"): under CPU load both failed in 3 of 3 processes of 15 repeats each before the fix and in 0 of
6 after. Worktree repair and creation now run one at a time per repository root (`src/workspace/manager.ts`,
`serialisedWorktreeCreate`; LLD §5 as-built note). Regression test: "creates worktrees for concurrent runs on one
repository one at a time (T9.11)" (`src/workspace/workspace.test.ts`; a `git` shim records overlapping adds
deterministically), which fails without the fix. **Not covered:** two controllers with different data directories on
one repository (outside the one-`serve`-per-data-directory model); Claude Code and
Codex end to end (no live run); FR-WS-09 and Q-55; retention GC (`git worktree prune`/`remove`) and the push path
(`git worktree list`) also enumerate worktrees and are not in the creation queue (no failure observed; not tested).

**T9.8 status (2026-10-13; Delegated): documentation closure done; M9 stays `Partial`, not `Done`.** Done: the README
now describes projects (configured and dynamic), the three commit paths, the publish ref, the push default, signing and
the bypass differences, each checked against `src/config/` and `src/git/`; AGENTS.md lists the provider set the roadmap
owns; the PRD carries a status note for CAP-9; ADR-0010 and ADR-0017 carry dated notes (decision text unchanged); the
README `test-sandboxed` row matches both adapters (`src/adapters/claude/adapter.ts`, `src/adapters/codex/adapter.ts`).
The task's "Done when" says §6 shows M9 `Done`. That would contradict the status blocks above and the M1 precedent
(`Done` means every acceptance criterion has a passing test), so it is not claimed. Reading of the rule (Delegated;
zemdregon has not been asked): `Partial` with every exception named, rather than "Done for host environments", because
exceptions E1 and E6 are unmet clauses of the M9 acceptance and verification columns, not only out-of-scope extras.

### 6.4 M10 Container environments: tasks

*Proposed.* Q-6 was left to the agent, so the runtime and login mechanism are delegated and verified by T10.0 before any
other M10 task.

**M10 and the bypass goal (note, 2026-10-13; not a re-sequencing).** Owner decision 24 makes container environments the
route to zemdregon's stated main usability goal, bypass runs with no permission prompts and no Git risk: in a container
the image has no Git client and neither the Git directory nor any credential store is mounted, so an approved bypass run
there does not depend on provider Git deny rules holding under bypass (R-38). Host-run agents stay supported with the
existing controls. The current sequence is unchanged: M3, then M9 (projects, `host` environments, the injected `commit`
and `push` tools that container runs also need), then M10. Q-38 (bring M10 forward) was decided on 2026-10-13 (owner decision
25): the order stays, and the document-only spike T10.0 may start early.

| Task | Objective | References | Depends on | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T10.0 | Spike: verify the Proposed rootless runtime and in-volume native login for each provider, and that each provider CLI works in an image with no Git client (owner decision 24; R-40); amend ADR-0013 or supersede it (the image rule is [ADR-0020](adr/0020-container-image-without-git-client.md)) | Q-6, FR-AUTH-01, FR-PRJ-05, [TDD §3.11](design/TDD.md#311-container-environments) | M9 | Findings recorded, ADR updated, R-13 and R-40 updated; a provider whose login cannot work in a container, or that cannot run without a Git client, is marked `host`-only |
| T10.1 | Environment port with a container implementation: mounts, limits, network policy, labels, teardown; refuse an image with a Git client | FR-PRJ-04, 05, [LLD §11.3](design/LLD.md#113-environment-port) | T10.0 | Opt-in tests show only the worktree and declared read-only contexts mounted and the container removed at run end; an image with `git` or `gh` on its `PATH` starts no run |
| T10.2 | Startup orphan sweep and `environment` run events | FR-PRJ-07, FR-EXE-06 | T10.1 | Fault injection: `SIGKILL` mid-run, restart, container gone before worktree assessment |
| T10.3 | Container leg of the malicious-fixture suite, including Git-write attempts under an approved bypass scope; `projects validate` checks, including the no-Git-client check; retention of containers and volumes | NFR-SEC-03, FR-PRJ-05, 08, 09, FR-GIT-01, 03 | T10.1 | Fixtures show no escape; a bypass run's Git-write attempts leave every ref unchanged; GC removes containers with worktrees |

#### 6.5.1 Order, waves and hot files

- **Wave A (can start now; none needs a live provider):** T3.0, T3.1, T3.2, T3.7 (the BR-3 mapping part), T3.8, T3.12
  (algorithm). T3.14, T3.15 and T3.16 are done.
- **Wave B:** T3.3 (after T3.2, T3.16), T3.17 (after T3.16), T3.10 (after T3.2), rest of T3.7 (after T3.2).
- **Wave C:** T3.4 (after T3.3), T3.5 and T3.6 (after T3.0, T3.2; their `privileged` cell after T3.3, which defines
  the structured scope type, and its bypass after T3.4 and T3.17), T3.11 (after T3.1, T3.10), T3.8b (after zemdregon's
  review).
- **Wave D:** T3.9 (operator), T3.13.
- **Hot files:** `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` (T3.3, T3.4, T3.7, T3.10), `src/mcp/*` (T3.3, including `continue_task`,
  and T3.10), `src/cli/serve.ts` (T3.4, T3.12), `src/ledger/repo.ts` (T3.3, T3.4, T3.10, T3.12) and
  `src/policy/types.ts` (T3.2, T3.3, which narrows `PrivilegedScope`). Tasks sharing a hot file run one after another,
  never in parallel (AGENTS.md); T3.3 goes before T3.10 in the lifecycle and MCP code.
- No code that emits a bypass flag merges before T3.16 and T3.17.

#### 6.5.2 What M3 cannot complete without live providers

M3 can reach `Partial` at best on fakes and fixtures. These need operator-run live checks (T3.9) and, for Codex, the M2
items Q-10 (auth status) and Q-12 (version range) first:

- NFR-SEC-03 for Claude Code and Codex: "no escape" on real CLIs.
- FR-POL-06: Codex credential confinement (decision 10) and Claude `test-sandboxed` confinement (R-10).
- FR-POL-02 per-profile compilation on real CLIs (flag spellings and behaviour, R-21, R-22).
- FR-GIT-01 live checks C-G2 and X-G1.
- Bypass behaviour under an approved `privileged` scope (decision 13).

FR-POL-09, FR-OPS-02 (`approve`, `deny`), FR-EXE-08, FR-WS-07 and NFR-DATA-01 can be shown on fakes.

### 6.6 M11 Inner agent: tasks

*Proposed breakdown, written when M11 was added; revisit when M9 completes.* Design: [ADR-0016](adr/0016-inner-agent.md),
[LLD §13](design/LLD.md#13-inner-agent), [TDD §3.12](design/TDD.md#312-inner-agent), [PRD
§3.1](requirements/PRD.md#31-inner-agent-task-catalogue). Order of delivery: the core and the `http` backend first, then
the commit-message task (the highest-value one), then the others as the milestones they need complete. No task here may
call a live model in the default test run.

| Task | Objective | References | Depends on | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T11.0 | Spike: on the installed OpenCode, find whether a one-shot `opencode run` with an operator-chosen model can be run with no tools, an empty working directory and no repository config. Record the flags verified, or record that it cannot and mark the `cli` kind `Ineligible` | FR-INN-04, Q-26, [R2] OpenCode | — | The result and the checked flags are written into TDD §4.6 with the version and date, or the limitation is recorded and FR-INN-04 re-prioritised |
| T11.1 | Config schema for `inner_agent`, the backend port, a scripted fake backend, the `inner_call` table, the disabled-by-default path, and the import-boundary test | FR-INN-01, 02, 05, 06, LLD §13.1–13.3 | M2 | Config rejects unknown keys and a credential-looking value; with no section the suite behaves as before; a test fails if COMP-POL, an adapter's profile compiler, the retry logic or the push path imports the inner-agent service |
| T11.2 | Call pipeline: redaction, input budget, call, schema validation, one re-ask, per-call timeout, circuit breaker, fallback result, `inner_call` and run events | FR-INN-07, 08, 16 (redaction, no body logging), LLD §13.4 | T11.1 | Fake-backend tests: invalid, empty, oversized, slow, erroring and non-UTF-8 outputs all fall back within the bound; breaker opens and probes; a planted secret never appears in the captured prompt or in any log |

Stays open until T11.10, T11.11 and T11.13 land (M5, M7, OpenCode), and until an injected hook or `inner` command calls the PR-draft, summary and title tasks |

**T11.1 status (2026-10-13; Delegated): Done on fakes. No live inner backend, provider CLI or network was used.** "Done
when", clause by clause: *config rejects unknown keys and a credential-looking value*: `inner_agent` is a strict subschema
with no credential field (a key, token, header, password or environment-variable field is an unknown key), and a string
value matching the artifact redactor's secret patterns, or a `base_url` with userinfo, a query or a fragment, is refused
at load without echoing the value (`src/config/config.test.ts`, "inner_agent"). *With no section the suite behaves as
before*: `ResolvedConfig.innerAgent` is absent, `createInnerAgent(undefined)` is `off` with every task disabled, and no
existing test changed except the two that counted migrations (`src/ledger/ledger.test.ts`, `src/projects/projects.test.ts`;
`src/inner/inner.test.ts`). *A test fails if COMP-POL, an adapter's profile compiler, the retry logic or the push path
imports the inner-agent service*: `src/inner/import-boundary.test.ts` (direct imports, with a synthetic-tree test that it
detects violations; the protected list is in the file). Also delivered: the backend port (`src/inner/port.ts`, LLD §13.2),
a scripted in-process fake backend (`src/inner/fake-backend.ts`) and the `inner_call` table with its append-only guards
(`src/ledger/migrations/0005_inner_call.ts`; upgrade from schema version 4, checks, keys and guards in
`src/ledger/ledger.test.ts`). Code: `src/config/inner-agent.ts`, `src/config/schema.ts`, `src/config/load.ts`, `src/inner/`.
Delegated choices: the config schema and resolution live in `src/config/` (not `src/inner/`) so config never imports the
service; the migration holds only `inner_call`, and `inner_task_state` (T11.6), the `git_commit` columns (T11.4), the
`usage_observation.source` widening (T11.5) and the `pr_draft` artifact kind (T11.8) ship as their own forward migrations
with those tasks, because a released migration is immutable ([ADR-0005](adr/0005-sqlite-ledger.md)); no new ADR is needed
(the persistence change is covered by ADR-0016 and ADR-0005). Assumptions: value bounds in the schema, `base_url` limited
to `http`/`https` (the Unix-socket form of LLD §13.1 is undefined in §13.2 and waits for T11.3), `shares_subscription_with`
checked against the known provider ids, `lifecycle.ts` protected whole because the BR-7 retry set lives in it. **Not
covered:** the pipeline (T11.2), the `http` backend (T11.3), any task contract, `doctor` and `inner` commands, the
per-project `inner_agent` setting (T11.3), and the transitive import gap noted in the test.

**T11.2 status (2026-10-13; Delegated): Done on fakes. No live inner backend, provider CLI or network was used.** "Done
when", clause by clause: *invalid, empty, oversized, slow, erroring and non-UTF-8 outputs all fall back within the
bound*: `src/inner/pipeline.test.ts` drives each through the scripted fake backend and asserts the task's fallback, the
`rejected_validator` or `fallback_unavailable` row with its reason code, the single re-ask that carries only a fixed
error class, and the wall-clock bound (a backend that ignores the abort signal is still cut off at the deadline, which
covers both attempts together). *The breaker opens and probes*: unit test of `CircuitBreaker` (`src/inner/breaker.ts`)
and an integration test: consecutive transport failures across tasks open it, no backend call is made while open
(`CIRCUIT_OPEN`), after `open_ms` exactly one probe goes through, a failed probe reopens it and a successful one closes
it, with one `inner_degraded` event per transition; a validator rejection never moves it. *A planted secret never
appears in the stored rows, events or logs*: the test plants an `sk-` key, a bearer token, a home path, a controller ID,
a native session ID and a provider name in the input and asserts the text the backend received, every `inner_call` row,
every `run_event` and every captured log entry (a backend error message that echoes the secret included) are free of
them. Delivered: the pipeline (`src/inner/pipeline.ts`: gate, redact, budget, hash, slot, breaker, call under one
deadline, decode and strip, Ajv, one re-ask, optional sanitiser, record), `src/inner/breaker.ts`,
`src/inner/truncate.ts`, `InnerAgentService.call`, `Ledger.recordInnerCall` and `listInnerCalls` (`src/ledger/repo.ts`:
the `inner_call` row and the run events in one transaction), and the import-boundary test stays green (nothing protected
imports `src/inner/`; `src/git/sanitize.ts` only gained an `export` on `CONTROLLER_ID`). Delegated choices and
Assumptions: no new table or column (ADR-0016 and ADR-0005 cover it); the §13.5 privacy-gate table is implemented as a
pure function because the pipeline must fail closed, while the project/context override plumbing, the `http` locality
classification and their tests stay in T11.3; a gate denial or a slot/breaker denial writes `attempts` 0, `latency_ms`
0, and for a gate denial `input_bytes` 0 with the SHA-256 of the empty string; the raw-output byte cap defaults to
`max_output_tokens * 4` unless the contract sets one; "not UTF-8" is a lone surrogate or U+FFFD in the string the port
returns; the re-ask appends a fixed `[previous answer rejected: <class>]` line to `user` (the port has no other channel)
and `input_sha256` and `input_bytes` describe the first attempt; `output_bytes` and `model_id` describe the last answer
received and token counts are summed only when every attempt reported them; the identifier scrub replaces controller IDs
and native session IDs with `[REDACTED]` and provider names with `[provider]`; `inner_degraded` is written on the
`closed` to `open` and `half_open` to `closed` transitions only, and only for a call tied to a run; the diff-aware
truncation is left to the task contracts (T11.4, T11.8), the default being head 25% and tail 75%.
**Not covered:**the `inner.call` telemetry span (no telemetry exporter exists in the tree yet), masking of environment
values in the prompt (no telemetry redactor defines it; only the artifact redactor's secret patterns and the home path
are applied), the `Ineligible` probe (`INELIGIBLE`, with the `cli` kind), quality-window suspension and
`fallback_suspended` (T11.6), the `shares_subscription_with` observation (T11.5), the `http` backend (T11.3) and every
task contract.

**T11.3 status (2026-10-13; Delegated): Done on fakes. No live inner backend, provider CLI or real network host was
used; every server in the tests is a fake bound to 127.0.0.1 and host names go through an injected resolver.** "Done
when", clause by clause (all in `src/inner/http-backend.test.ts` unless noted): *a `remote` backend is refused without
opt-in*: a name resolving to a loopback and a private address is `remote`, the call is `fallback_disabled` /
`REMOTE_NOT_ALLOWED`, and the fake server saw no request; an unresolvable name is `remote` too (fail closed). *Diffs are
refused without the per-task opt-in*: a source-content task on a `remote` backend with `allow_remote` but no
`allow_source_content` is `SOURCE_CONTENT_NOT_ALLOWED` with no request, and with the opt-in it reaches the server (the
real task contracts, T11.4 onwards, only set `sends_source_content`). *`local_only` and `off` projects never reach a
remote backend*: `PROJECT_LOCAL_ONLY` and `PROJECT_OFF`, server request count 0; `off` does not even resolve the name
(the pipeline now checks it before `resolveLocality`); a `local` backend is still used under `local_only` (LLD §13.5 row
3). *Redirects, oversized responses, slow responses and non-JSON bodies fall back*: a 302 or 307 is never followed (the
target saw nothing), a body over `max_output_tokens * 16 + 16 KiB` or a larger declared `content-length` is cut off, a
response slower than `timeout_ms` is `TIMEOUT` within the bound, and a non-JSON body, an envelope with no
`choices[0].message.content`, a non-2xx status, a compressed response and a dropped or refused connection are
`fallback_unavailable` (transport-level, so they count for the breaker); content that is not JSON is
`rejected_validator` after one re-ask. *No credential, header from config or environment value ever appears in rows,
events or logs*: the test plants an API key, a proxy variable and a header value in the process environment and asserts
the request carries only the fixed headers (`content-type`, `accept`, `accept-encoding`, `content-length`, `host`,
`connection`), and that rows, run events and log entries hold no prompt, completion, key or header value even when a
hostile endpoint echoes them. Also delivered: locality classification (`classifyAddress`: loopback, private, remote;
`local` only when every resolved address is loopback), the address pinned per call so the connection cannot differ from
the classification and the socket's peer re-checked (`LOCALITY_CHANGED`; the branch is not exercised because a test
cannot make a loopback bind appear remote), the project and context `inner_agent` setting (`src/config/schema.ts`,
`src/config/projects.ts`, `effectiveInnerSetting`; `projects show` prints it; `src/config/config.test.ts`), and
`createConfiguredInnerAgent` (`src/inner/index.ts`) that builds the `http` backend from settings. The import-boundary
test stays green. Delegated choices and Assumptions (also in [LLD §13.2](design/LLD.md#132-backend-port)): the
chat-completion mapping (`POST <base>/v1/chat/completions`, `response_schema` not sent), the response size cap, the
pinned-address rule, `private` addresses are `remote`, `probe` refuses a `remote` endpoint without `allow_remote`; the
effective setting is computed on demand from configuration by `effectiveInnerSetting(project, contextId)` and is **not**
frozen in the run's policy snapshot (the callers, T11.4 onwards, pass it as `project_setting`); an orchestrator-created
project may also carry `inner_agent` (it can only restrict, so it needs no approval). **Not covered:** the Unix-socket
form of `base_url` (LLD §13.1 mentions it, §13.2 does not define it; `base_url` stays `http`/`https`), the wiring of the
service into `serve` (the first caller is T11.4), TLS behaviour against a real certificate, `doctor` and `inner`
commands (T11.7), and any live endpoint.

**T11.4 status (2026-10-13; Delegated): Done on fakes. No live inner backend, provider CLI or network was used; the
inner backend is the scripted in-process fake and every commit is read back from the publish ref of a temporary
repository.** "Done when", clause by clause (all in `src/git/commit-message.test.ts`, calls over the real tool socket):
*a hostile fake backend returning `Co-authored-by:` lines, a run ID and a "generated by" footer yields a clean commit*:
the draft also carries the session ID, the native session ID, a session URL and a `Claude-Session:` trailer; the
committed message holds none of them nor any provider name, `git_commit` has `message_source = 'inner'` and the
`inner_call_id` of the `accepted` row, and the sanitiser counts are recorded (a mutation that commits the raw draft
fails this test). *Empty or unavailable output returns the tool error and no commit*: empty output, non-JSON output, a
backend error and a draft that is empty after the sanitiser each answer `MESSAGE_REQUIRED` with the publish ref
unmoved and no `git_commit` row (`rejected_validator`, `fallback_unavailable`, `rejected_sanitiser`); the agent's next
call with a message commits. *The agent's own message wins in the default mode*: no backend request and no
`inner_call` row. Also tested: `always` (a valid draft wins and the agent's message is never sent; an unusable draft
falls back to the agent's message, else `MESSAGE_REQUIRED`); `never`, a disabled task, a disabled agent and no service
leave the shim command, the schema, the `INVALID_ARGUMENTS` answer for a missing message and the commit exactly as
before; the privacy gates refuse a diff to a `remote` backend without `allow_remote` (`REMOTE_NOT_ALLOWED`) and
without the per-task `allow_source_content` (`SOURCE_CONTENT_NOT_ALLOWED`), with no request; the project setting
`off` at open keeps `message` required, and `off` or `local_only` set after open is honoured at call time
(`PROJECT_OFF`, `PROJECT_LOCAL_ONLY`), while `local_only` still uses a local backend; a planted secret in the diff
never reaches the prompt; the prompt holds no run, session or native session ID, ref name, provider name or agent
text. The resolver of the effective setting (`src/projects/inner-setting.ts`) is tested for configured and dynamic
projects. Delivered: the task contract (`src/inner/commit-message.ts`), the injected `CommitMessageTask` and
`MESSAGE_REQUIRED` in the commit algorithm (`src/git/commit.ts`), the optional-message channel and shim
(`src/git/channel.ts`, `src/git/tool-protocol.ts`, `src/cli/tool-shim.ts`), the service wired into `serve`
(`src/cli/serve.ts`, the first caller, as the T11.3 status expected), migration 0007 with `message_source` and
`inner_call_id` on `git_commit` (`src/ledger/migrations/0007_git_commit_message_source.ts`, upgrade test in
`src/ledger/ledger.test.ts`), and both columns in `runs show` (`src/cli/runs.ts`, `src/cli/git-push.test.ts`). The
import-boundary test stays green and now also protects `src/git/commit.ts`, which receives the task only as an
injected function (`src/inner/import-boundary.test.ts`). No new ADR: the columns are named by LLD §12.7 under
ADR-0016, and a forward migration follows ADR-0005. Delegated choices and Assumptions (recorded in
[LLD §12.1](design/LLD.md#121-tool-contracts), [§12.7](design/LLD.md#127-provenance-and-audit) and
[§13.6](design/LLD.md#136-task-contracts)): trailer lines in the draft body are removed by the sanitiser rather than
rejected by the validator (LLD §13.6 read against this Done-when); the subject is rejected when it is trailer-shaped;
the schema is chosen at channel open and the project setting is resolved again per call, an unknown project reading as
`off`; the draft is requested once per call; the prompt scrub adds a fixed list of product names to the configured
provider ids; the diff-aware truncation and the raw-diff pre-cap. **Not covered:** suspension of the task after a high
rejection rate (T11.6), the `inner.call` telemetry span (no exporter yet, as in T11.2), a live backend, and the
real provider tool channels (only the fake adapter declares `injected`; Claude Code only for an approved bypass run,
unverified live).

choices and Assumptions: an observation is written only when a backend request was made (`attempts >= 1`), because a

**T11.8 status (2026-10-13; Delegated): Done on fakes. No live inner backend, provider CLI, pull-request API or network

**T11.6 status (2026-10-13; Delegated): Done on fakes. No live inner backend, provider CLI or network was used.** "Done
when", clause by clause (all in `src/inner/suspension.test.ts` unless noted): *rejection rate above the configured
threshold over the window suspends the task*: with the configured `window_calls`, `min_calls` and `rejection_threshold`,
a rate exactly at the threshold does not suspend and one above it does; only the last `window_calls` quality calls
count, so old rejections roll out; `fallback_*` outcomes are not evidence. *Which then falls back until re-enabled*: the
next call returns the task's fallback with a `fallback_suspended` row (reason `TASK_SUSPENDED`, no backend request, no
input touched), and a suspension writes one `inner_task_suspended` run event (`task`, `window_calls`, `rejected`, `total`,
`threshold`) for a run-linked call. *Per task*: suspending `run_title` leaves `commit_message` calling the backend.
*Survives a restart*: the state is `inner_task_state` (migration 0008, `src/ledger/migrations/0008_inner_task_state.ts`,
upgrade from version 7 in `src/ledger/ledger.test.ts`); a new database handle, ledger and service still fall back, and the
gate reads the state on every call. *A minimum sample size*: `min_calls` (LLD default 20, range 1..`window_calls`, from
T11.1); below it even all-bad calls do not suspend. *Re-enabling clears it and is recorded*:
`InnerAgentService.resumeTask(task)` clears the suspension, sets `resumed_at`, increments `version`, restarts the window
(old rejections no longer count; a second suspension needs a fresh minimum sample) and returns `undefined` when the task
was not suspended. The window is evaluated in the same transaction as the `inner_call` row
(`Ledger.recordInnerCall` in `src/ledger/repo.ts`); the pipeline gate is `src/inner/pipeline.ts`. No new ADR: the table
is named by LLD §13.3 under ADR-0016 and ships as a forward migration (ADR-0005). Delegated choices and Assumptions
are in [LLD §13.3](design/LLD.md#133-data) (T11.6 note): the "since `resumed_at`" boundary is strict, a re-enable
clears the basis columns, an unreadable state falls back, and re-enable is a service method rather than a config edit.
**Not covered:** the `inner resume` and `inner status` commands and the `doctor` suspended-state line (T11.7), the
`inner.call` telemetry span (no exporter yet), and a live backend.

is not a Delegated addition. Delegated choices and Assumptions: (1) with no `inner_agent` section `doctor` prints
nothing about the inner agent, to keep its output byte-identical, while `inner status` says `off`; (2) inner-agent
findings in `doctor` are at most warnings and never change its exit code, because the inner agent never fails a run

contacts the backend only for the fixed canary, `local` or with `allow_remote`; it writes no `inner_call` row; (4)
the circuit breaker is in the daemon's memory (LLD §13.4 step 6), so the CLI reports its own process's state, which
starts `closed`, and says so; (5) `inner test` runs the real pipeline with the ledger, so it writes one `inner_call` row
with no run (LLD §13.8) and its outcome counts in the quality window like any call; it follows the `approve` locking
pattern; (6) the fixture for `commit_message` reuses the real `commit_message.v1` template, the other five tasks have
no task contract yet and use a `<task>.fixture.v1` stand-in with the LLD §13.6 output schema, and no fixture sends source

seam. **Not covered:** the `cli` backend (reported `Ineligible`, T11.13), the Unix-socket form of `base_url`, project
and context overrides in `doctor` (installation level only), the `inner.call` telemetry span (no exporter yet), and a
live backend.

*Fallback is deterministic head and tail*: on any non-accepted outcome (empty, error, not JSON, schema or rule

green: the envelope builder reads the event by constant and shape and does not import `src/inner/`. Delegated choices and
Assumptions are in [LLD §13.6](design/LLD.md#136-task-contracts) (T11.9 note): the facts and the output come from the

### 6.7 M12 Retrieval index: tasks

*Proposed breakdown, written when M12 was added; revisit when M9 and T11.3 complete.* Design:
[ADR-0031](adr/0031-retrieval-index.md), SRS §3.15 (`FR-RAG`). Order of delivery: the spike, then the per-project core,
then retrieval at admission, then sharing. Provider/model affinity is not part of M12; it lands in M6/M7 (FR-RTE-06 to
09, FR-CAP-08, [ADR-0032](adr/0032-provider-model-affinity.md)). No task here may call a live embedding model or embed
real source in the default test run.

| Task | Objective | References | Depends on | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T12.0 | Spike: storage and embedding backend. Measure in-process brute-force cosine search over SQLite BLOB vectors against a chunk cap, and pick the first `local` and `http` backend shapes and the default `max_context_tokens` | FR-RAG-04, 05, 11, Q-48 | — | The result, the measured sizes and timings, the chosen cap and the default are written into [TDD](design/TDD.md) with date and versions, or an approximate-nearest-neighbour index is recorded as needed; no number is stated before it is measured **Status: Done (2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved).** Evidence: `scripts/bench-rag.mjs` (not in `npm test`) and [TDD §3.13.4](design/TDD.md#3134-t120-spike-result-2026-10-14): measured on the Ampere VM (Neoverse-N1, 2 vCPUs, Node v24.21.0, SQLite 3.53.4) at 1,000 to 100,000 chunks and 384, 768 and 1536 dimensions. Streaming vectors from SQLite per query is 5 to 13 times slower than a scan of vectors loaded into memory, so search scans memory; no approximate-nearest-neighbour index is needed. Chosen: `caps.max_chunks` 25,000 (memory scan p95 25.5 / 48.4 / 108.0 ms at 384 / 768 / 1536 dimensions), 40-line windows bounded to 4,096 bytes, `top_k` 8 and `max_context_tokens` 4,000 (from this repository's measured chunk sizes), `http` first (`/v1/embeddings`, Assumption) and `local` refused at load until an in-process model has its own ADR. T12.5 must scan in yielding slices (Q-48 answer) |
| T12.1 | Config schema for `rag` (global section and per-project override), per-project store schema and migration, the disabled-by-default path | FR-RAG-01, 03, 09, 11 | M2, T9.1 | Config rejects unknown keys and a credential-looking value; with no backend the suite behaves as before; one store per project; the store is deleted with its project **Status: Done (2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved).** Evidence: `src/config/rag.test.ts` (unknown keys at every level, credential-looking values refused without echo, `local` kind and `mode: "on"` without a backend refused, caps only lowered, global sources under an allowed root, no backend means every project off), `src/retrieval/store.test.ts` (one `0600` store per project under `rag/projects/`, LLD §14.2 schema as migration 1, newer schema refused, delete and prune, a planted symlink removed without touching its target, nothing created with no backend) and `src/mcp/project-tools.test.ts` (`delete_project` removes the store; a dynamic definition cannot set the sharing flags). Code: `src/config/rag.ts`, `src/retrieval/store.ts`, the prune at `serve` start. Defaults and the as-built notes are in [LLD §14.1](design/LLD.md#141-configuration-shape) and [§14.2](design/LLD.md#142-stores-and-tables); the `rag_record` ledger table moves to T12.4, its first writer |

### 6.8 M4 Adapter expansion: tasks

*Proposed breakdown (2026-10-13, Delegated agent work, documentation only; not owner-approved), written under §9.1
step 2 from the M4 row in §6 and the requirements it lists.* M4 depends on M3 and M9, both `Partial`; under §9.1 step 1
agents may start the tasks below whose dependencies need neither the operator nor zemdregon. Design:
[TDD §4](design/TDD.md#4-provider-adapter-strategy) (every M4 provider row reads "to be verified in M4"),
[TDD §5](design/TDD.md#5-implementation-strategy-by-milestone), [LLD §2](design/LLD.md#2-adapter-contract),
[LLD §6](design/LLD.md#6-compliance-manifest), [ADR-0002](adr/0002-mcp-upstream-acp-downstream.md) (ACP downstream),
[ADR-0009](adr/0009-version-gated-adapters.md), [ADR-0026](adr/0026-per-run-tool-channel-socket-and-token.md),
[ADR-0029](adr/0029-claude-code-receives-tool-shim-via-mcp-config.md) and
[ADR-0033](adr/0033-claude-channel-bypass-only-without-safe-mode.md). Provider facts come from [R2] (the
[verified research](research/headless-ai-coding-controller-verified-2026-10-12.md); "L" numbers below are its lines)
and, for Muse Code, the [research note](research/muse-code-2026-10-13.md). Every one is **Unverified** against an
installed CLI (R-7, R-33): no task may state a flag, event shape, model name, limit or price that its spike did not
source, and where the research is silent the task is to find the fact, not to assume it.

**Facts the breakdown relies on (checked against `main` at `81d7ffe`, 2026-10-13).**

- NFR-MAINT-01 is not met yet: adding an adapter today also edits `ProviderId` in `src/adapters/types.ts` (it lacks
  `muse`, which [LLD §2](design/LLD.md#2-adapter-contract) lists), `PROVIDER_IDS` in `src/config/schema.ts` (it lacks
  `muse-code`), `CONFIG_TO_ADAPTER_ID` in `src/routing/collect.ts`, the registrations in `src/cli/serve.ts` and
  `src/cli/doctor.ts`, the exports in `src/adapters/index.ts` and the single `BYPASS_FLAGS` list in
  `src/adapters/audit.ts`.
- No ACP transport exists: `Transport` names `acp-stdio`, but nothing in `src/exec/` speaks ACP and every adapter's
  `parseEvent` ignores non-string input.
- No `adapters` command exists (`src/cli/main.ts`); versions are recorded by reviewed manifest edits (Q-12), and the
  FR-ADP-02 step 7 smoke test is skipped (§6.2.1).
- CI runs on `ubuntu-24.04` only (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). `src/exec/supervisor.ts` (process-tree enumeration) and
  `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` (the start-tick PID-reuse guard, LLD §8) read `/proc`, which is Linux-only, and every
  profile is ineligible on `darwin` (`SANDBOX_DEPENDENT_PROFILES`, Q-35).
- The usage ledger already stores `harness` and `backing_provider` (`src/ledger/repo.ts`, `src/usage/observations.ts`)
  and `ProbeResult.modelsListed` exists, but there is no model registry (FR-ADP-05).
- Reusable pieces: the conformance harness (`src/adapters/conformance.ts`, `REQUIRED_CASE_KINDS`), the replay-fixture
  pattern (the one shared helper `src/adapters/fixtures/replay.mjs` with a per-provider dialect in
  `src/adapters/fixtures/replay-dialects/`, and `cases.ts` under `src/adapters/claude/fixtures/` and
  `src/adapters/codex/fixtures/`; T4.3), the controller leg and the per-adapter leg of the malicious-fixture suite
  (`test/malicious/`, `test/malicious/adapters/`), the opt-in live smoke script (`scripts/live-smoke.mjs` with its
  provider plug-ins in `scripts/live/providers/`) and the `GitCapabilities` declaration (`src/adapters/types.ts`).

**Conventions for every provider group (Proposed, Delegated).**

- Groups are numbered by tens: Grok Build T4.20 to T4.29, Antigravity T4.30 to T4.39, Cursor T4.40 to T4.49, OpenCode
  T4.50 to T4.59, Kimi Code T4.60 to T4.69, Muse Code T4.70 to T4.79. In each, `T4.n0` is a documentation-only spike,
  `T4.n1` and `T4.n8` are **operator-only** (an installed CLI with the operator's native login, like T3.9 and T9.4b),
  and the rest run on fakes and synthetic fixtures in CI. Shared groundwork is T4.1 to T4.12 (no T4.0: by this
  roadmap's convention `.0` is a spike, and M4's spikes are per provider).
- A new manifest starts with a range that admits no version (`<0.0.0`, as Codex had until Q-12), so the adapter fails
  the version gate with `ADAPTER_VERSION_UNVERIFIED` until a version passes `adapters validate` (T4.4).
- Git declarations start at the fail-closed values: `gitWriteControl.kind` `none-documented` unless the spike sources a
  deny control, and `commitChannel` `finalize-only` (FR-GIT-09; owner decision 36: the controller commits at FINALIZING
  when the project opts in). An `injected` channel needs its own ADR on the ADR-0029 and ADR-0033 pattern, an operator
  opt-in and a live check; none is planned here.
- No bypass-class flag is emitted outside an approved `privileged` scope that sets bypass (FR-POL-03, ADR-0017). Each
  spike lists its provider's bypass-class flags, and T4.1 puts them in the argv audit. An approved bypass scope compiles
  on a `host` project even where the provider's Git-write control is `none-documented` or denial is not shown to hold
  under its bypass mode, relying on FR-GIT-03 and FR-GIT-06 (owner decision 46, answering Q-58); the cell is
  `Ineligible` only for a different reason, such as undocumented bypass flags.
- Synthetic fixtures are labelled synthetic and replaced by scrubbed captures from the operator task (no credential,
  token, account identifier or private prompt text).

| Task | Objective | References | Depends on | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T4.1 | Make adding an adapter touch only its module, manifest, fixtures and one registry entry: derive `ProviderId`, `PROVIDER_IDS`, `CONFIG_TO_ADAPTER_ID`, the `serve` and `doctor` registrations and the exports from one registry table; let an adapter declare its provider's bypass-class flags ("or equivalents", FR-POL-03) as static data that the argv audit adds to the shared `BYPASS_FLAGS`, which stays | NFR-MAINT-01, FR-POL-03, FR-ADP-01, [LLD §2](design/LLD.md#2-adapter-contract), `src/adapters/audit.ts`, `src/adapters/registry.ts`, T3.17 | — | A test registers a scratch adapter from a fixture module with no other source edit, and it appears in `doctor`, `list_capabilities` and config validation; every adapter-declared flag is refused before spawn except in the approved bypass case, as in the T3.17 test; existing tests pass unchanged; a new ADR (next free number) records the audit change, since the audit is a security control; the LLD §2 provider-id table is updated *Audit gaps found by the 2026-10-13 spikes (Proposed, Delegated; each a requirement for this task, sourced in the TDD sections named).* (a) `src/adapters/audit.ts` matches only long spellings and `flag=value`, so Cursor's short `-f` for `--force` would pass ([TDD §4.5](design/TDD.md#45-cursor-cli-m4); whether `-pf` is accepted: Unverified: T4.41). (b) Bypass-class settings that are values, not flags, need a value-aware rule: Cursor `--sandbox disabled` (§4.5); Muse Code `--approval-mode never` and `--sandbox-network unrestricted` ([§4.8](design/TDD.md#48-muse-code-m4)); OpenCode inline permission `allow` as a whole or `"*": "allow"` (with `--auto`, [§4.6.2](design/TDD.md#462-inventory-and-decisions-for-later-tasks-t450)); Grok Build `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` ([§4.3.1](design/TDD.md#431-grok-build-native-controls-per-profile-and-platform-t420)). (c) Candidate adapter-declared entries, all Proposed and pending each operator capture: Grok Build `--always-approve`, `--yolo`, `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, a catch-all `allow` rule (`*`, `**`) and `auto_allow_bash`/`GROK_SANDBOX_AUTO_ALLOW_BASH`, with `--trust`, `-w`/`--worktree` and `--fork-session` never emitted (§4.3.1); Antigravity `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, with `--remote-control` never emitted, `--mode=accept-edits` not emitted until T4.31, and a detective check that `init.permission_mode` is not `always-proceed` outside an approved bypass scope ([§4.4.2](design/TDD.md#442-antigravity-native-controls-per-profile-and-platform-t430)); Cursor `-f`, `--sandbox disabled`, and refusal outside an approved bypass run of `-w`/`--worktree`, `--worktree-base`, `--skip-worktree-setup`, `--plugin-dir`, `--api-key`, `--auth-token`, `-H`/`--header` and `--workspace` (§4.5); OpenCode `--auto` and the inline `allow` forms, with `--share` and `--attach` never emitted (§4.6.2); Kimi Code `--yolo`, `--auto` and `--plan` outside an approved `privileged` scope ([§4.7](design/TDD.md#47-kimi-code-m4-blocked-by-b-1)); Muse Code `--yolo`, `--disable-approval`, `--disable-sandbox` and `--trust-workspace` (documented), plus the two values in (b) (§4.8). (d) Open design question, not decided here (for T4.62): Kimi Code `-p` always runs "Never Ask" and has no documented non-bypass approval mode, so should a headless Kimi Code run count as bypass-class under FR-POL-03 and be limited to an approved `privileged` scope? (§4.7) *Status 2026-10-13 (audit gaps (a) to (c) closed for argv, Delegated, [ADR-0037](adr/0037-argv-audit-short-flags-and-bypass-values.md), which extends ADR-0035):* registry entries may declare `bypassShortFlags` and `bypassValues`, counted only once the entry has an adapter; the audit refuses a declared short flag alone, as `-x=value` or inside any single-dash cluster (deliberate over-refusal), and a declared value as `--flag value` or `--flag=value` (case-insensitive), in argv and `policyFlags`, except in the approved bypass case. Declared: Claude Code `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` (active now; the adapter never emits it); Grok Build `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` and catch-all `--allow`/`--allowed-tools` `*`, `**`; Cursor `-f` and `--sandbox disabled` (both inert until T4.22 and T4.42); Muse Code's two values are declared with T4.72. Evidence: `src/adapters/bypass-forms.test.ts` (the T3.17 matrix over each form through the scratch fixture), [LLD §2](design/LLD.md#2-adapter-contract). **Not covered by the argv audit** (ADR-0037 limits): `OPENCODE_PERMISSION`/`OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` with `allow`, `GROK_SANDBOX_AUTO_ALLOW_BASH`/`auto_allow_bash`, Antigravity `toolPermission` `always-proceed` and Cursor `approvalMode` in settings files. Long-flag candidates in (c) (OpenCode `--auto`, Kimi Code `--auto`/`--plan`, the Cursor and Muse Code flags) are declared by their adapter tasks; (d) stays open |

Evidence: (1) one replay helper, `src/adapters/fixtures/replay.mjs` (launcher: `src/adapters/fixtures/replay.ts`), with a dialect file per provider in `src/adapters/fixtures/replay-dialects/` (`claude.mjs`, `codex.mjs`); both per-adapter `replay.mjs` copies are deleted, and `src/adapters/claude/claude.test.ts`, `src/adapters/codex/codex.test.ts`, `src/mcp/mcp.test.ts` and `src/lifecycle/claude-channel.test.ts` pass unchanged in what they assert. (2) The per-adapter leg: `test/malicious/adapter-leg.ts` (harness), `test/malicious/adapter-leg.test.ts` (discovers every `test/malicious/adapters/*.case.ts`), `test/malicious/adapters/claude.case.ts` and `codex.case.ts`. It checks that the compiled argv carries the provider's isolation flags and none of the flags that would enable repository hooks, MCP definitions, settings or plugins, and names no path of the hostile fixture repository; that a non-`disabled` repository configuration is refused or compiled with the same isolation; that replayed credential-path read, out-of-worktree write and network streams end `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` or `SANDBOX_VIOLATION` (at exit 0 and non-zero) with the hostile checkout, canary logs and outside sentinel untouched; and that unknown event kinds parse as `unknown` and end `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`, never success; each check has a positive control that shows it fails on a broken variant. (3) The live plug-in point: `scripts/live/providers/<id>.mjs` (`claude.mjs`, `codex.mjs`; contract in their header), listed by `scripts/live-smoke.mjs`; `test/live-smoke.test.ts` shows the skip and the plug-in contract. Honest limits: every hostile stream is SYNTHETIC, in the shapes of the adapters' existing synthetic fixtures, so the leg proves compile and parse behaviour only and not that a real CLI contains an escape (that stays `T4.n8`/T3.9). A new provider adds the leg's one case file, and also its replay dialect file and its live plug-in file (three additive files, no edit to a shared one) besides its own adapter module |

The first runs failed in 13 files and found: (1) a **product bug**: `kill(-pgid, 0)` answers `EPERM` for a group whose only members are zombies on macOS (measured: `Z<` rows, `EPERM` until reaped), so `groupAlive` in `src/exec/supervisor.ts` counted a dead group as alive and escalated to `SIGKILL`; `EPERM` now falls through to the process table (LLD §8.3), and an unreadable table still counts as alive; (2) test assumptions: the `alive()` helper of `src/exec/supervisor.test.ts` read `/proc` (now `ps` off Linux), macOS adds `__CF_USER_TEXT_ENCODING` to every process (`PLATFORM_INJECTED_ENV_NAMES` in `test/malicious/detectors.ts`; four tests filter it), and macOS temporary paths exceed the 100-byte socket bound (`src/git/channel.test.ts` and three more use `/tmp` on darwin; the bound itself is unchanged and below macOS's `sun_path` size, which this repository does not cite). **Skipped on macOS, with reasons:** `src/cli/serve.test.ts` blocks "serve over stdio (process level)", "injected commit tool through serve" and "privileged end to end" (11 tests): the test-only fake adapter's compile matrix is verified on Linux only, so `PROFILE_PLATFORM_UNVERIFIED` ends them by design (Q-35 unchanged; they run on the Linux leg); `test/secret-scan.test.ts` "`serve` daemon as a process" (added by PR #108 after T4.10 and found failing on the macOS leg for the same reason; skipped by the same rule); `src/workspace/nested.test.ts` "a name that is not valid UTF-8" (APFS rejects the name); `src/lifecycle/concurrent-worktrees.test.ts` (3 tests, skipped on every platform until Q-55). The signing tests did not skip on the runner (it has `gpg`). Not covered: the `serve` tests that need an eligible profile therefore do not exercise the macOS supervisor end to end; T4.12 (operator) is the live check; the macOS job is not a required status check until zemdregon sets that in repository settings (Assumption) |

The session id is never taken from text. Update (2026-10-14, Delegated): usage records only the six token counters the live check saw (4a) plus `num_turns`, an unknown line's type is clipped to 64 characters (as Cursor and Antigravity), and "cases with no recorded native shape" pins the observed outcomes: an 8f denial (`stopReason` `cancelled`) is `TOOL_FAILURE`; an unrecognised `error` line or a bare non-zero exit (5d, rate limit never forced) is `TOOL_FAILURE`, never `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT` or success; an 8c rule denial and a 7g sandbox violation end `end_turn` at exit 0 and are **not detected** (the run succeeds with `unknownEventCount` > 0), because the only signal is tool text and FR-ADP-04 forbids reading status from it. Hardening (2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved; "parser hardening (T4.23, synthetic shapes; mirrors Antigravity T4.33)" in `grok.test.ts`): a second `end` line and a session id that changes mid-run end `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` (the first id stays bound, an empty id binds nothing); an `error` line without a string message is a provider error (`TOOL_FAILURE`), never ignored or `unknown`; rate-limit or quota text in an `error` line ends `TOOL_FAILURE`, never `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`; negative, non-finite and non-number usage values are dropped and no usable counter emits no usage. Guard verdict (2026-10-15, Delegated, not owner-approved; the same ADR-0039 gap T4.36 and T4.43 fixed for Antigravity and Cursor): `extractResult` now treats a `tool_call` carrying `violation` as not clean, so a guard-flagged Git write with an `end_turn` result at exit 0 ends `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` and a `provider_state` verdict `SANDBOX_VIOLATION` (tests "a guard-flagged Git write with an end_turn result ends PERMISSION_BLOCKED (ADR-0039)" and "any guard verdict on a tool_call makes an end_turn result not clean"); it takes effect once the parser emits `tool_call`, and "pins the gap" records that today a Grok `tool_call` line stays `unknown`, so the guard has nothing to flag. Further hardening (2026-10-15, Delegated, not owner-approved; "parser hardening (T4.23, synthetic shapes; mirrors Cursor T4.43 and OpenCode T4.53)" in `grok.test.ts`): a `sessionId` that resume could not pass as one safe argument is never bound and ends `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` (an earlier good id stays bound); a `tool_call` after the `end` line is not success (a guard verdict on it keeps its own code); tests pin that only an exact `end_turn` succeeds, that prototype-named types and `__proto__` keys never smuggle a result, and that non-string `tool_call` fields are omitted, never coerced (a call with none stays `unknown`, invisible to the guard). Fixture files (2026-10-15, Delegated, not owner-approved; synthetic lines, no new capture; merged [PR #260](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/260)): every Done-when case now has a JSONL fixture under `src/adapters/grok/fixtures/` replayed end to end through the supervisor: success (`success_edit`), task failure and the 8f denial (`task_failure`), malformed (`malformed_output`), plus `sandbox_violation` (7g) and `permission_denied_rule` (8c), whose tool lines carry only their recorded `type` and which replay to an `end_turn` success with `unknownEventCount` 2 (the gap, pinned), `rate_limit` (5d never forced: an empty stream at exit 1 ends `TOOL_FAILURE`, never `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`) and `unknown_events` (invented types, an unread `tool_call_update` and an `end` without a string `stopReason`: `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`, four lines counted, no session bound); tests "fixture ..." in "cases with no recorded native shape" in `grok.test.ts`. No native shape was added and the parser is unchanged. **Not done, so not `Done`:** the shapes of `tool_call` and `tool_call_update`, rate limit, permission denial and sandbox violation events were not recorded by the T4.21 check (it names the line types only; denial and sandbox failures showed as the tool's own status and text, and a `rate_limit` was never forced), so those lines are `unknown` events and the parser emits no `tool_call`, `permission_denied`, `sandbox_violation` or `rate_limit`. Consequence, stated plainly: Switchboard's tool-call guard (ADR-0039, T4.27) cannot see Grok Build's commands, so for an approved bypass run the Git-write denial rests on FR-GIT-03 and FR-GIT-06 (owner decision 46) until a capture records the tool-call shape. Needs the operator: a scrubbed `streaming-json` capture of one edit, one shell command and one denied command (T4.21 step 4 and 8 extension), then update this parser and add the fixtures |

| T4.33 | Antigravity parser, result, failure classification and resume on synthetic fixtures; usage counters recorded as `provider_reported` only | FR-ADP-04, FR-SES-03, FR-CAP-01, [LLD §4](design/LLD.md#4-failure-taxonomy) | T4.32 | As T4.23 **Status: Partial (2026-10-14, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved; synthetic lines in the shapes of the T4.31 live check).** Evidence: `parseEvent`, `extractResult` and `resumeArgs` in `src/adapters/antigravity/adapter.ts` (event names, status and error prefixes in `src/adapters/antigravity/protocol.ts`); `src/adapters/antigravity/antigravity.test.ts` "antigravity parser and result (T4.33)": success (conversation id and model from `init`, the response as the summary, the envelope's run-total usage recorded `provider_reported` once); a soft-denied tool (`denied_actions`) is `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` although `status` is `SUCCESS` and the exit is 0; `SUCCESS` with no reported tokens (a print timeout) and every other status fail (Q-86); an invalid model and a failed login map to `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` and `AUTH_REQUIRED`; a non-zero exit, a missing result, a timeout and a cancel never succeed; malformed and unknown lines are counted `unknown` events; the conversation id never comes from text. Hardening (2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved; "antigravity parser hardening (T4.33, synthetic shapes)"): a `denied_actions` value that is not an array and a non-string `error` fail the run (never ignored); a second `result` line and a conversation id that changes mid-run end `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` (the first id stays bound); negative or non-number usage values are dropped; a tool step first reported without parameters is reported again when a later update names its command or path, so the tool-call guard sees it; unrecorded rate-limit text in `error` ends `TOOL_FAILURE`, never `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`; resume is `Ineligible` for every profile and binding id and `--conversation` is never compiled. Further hardening (2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved): a second `result` line no longer records a second `usage` row, so the run total is counted once (FR-CAP-01); a `step_update` whose `conversation_id` differs from the bound one ends `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` while its tool call still reaches the guard, and a step never binds the session; a test pins the classification precedence (cancel and timeout over a denial, a denial over an envelope error and a non-zero exit, the ADR-0039 guard over all of them). Hardening (2026-10-15, Delegated, not owner-approved; synthetic lines): a later step update that names a new command or path is reported to the guard again, so it cannot swap a checked command for an unchecked one; a `conversation_id` that is not one safe token binds nothing and ends `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`; tests pin exact `status` and event matching, `SUCCESS` without `total_tokens` failing, that `init.permission_mode` is not read and that a command under an unrecorded parameter key is invisible to the guard. **Not done, so not `Done`:** (1) resume is not compiled (Q-86): an unknown `--conversation` id exits 0 with `SUCCESS` in a new conversation; (2) no rate-limit, sandbox-violation or exit-3 `AGY_ERROR` shape reached stdout in the live check (the `AGY_ERROR` line is on stderr, which the adapter contract does not pass to `extractResult`), so the parser emits neither and those failures end `TOOL_FAILURE`; (3) the keys of `tool_info.parameters` were not recorded, so the `command` and path names are Assumptions and a call without them is invisible to the tool-call guard. Needs the operator: a capture of a tool step's parameters, of a rate-limited run and of a resume with a known id that shows how a resumed conversation is marked (T4.31 extension). Design evidence (merged [PR #231](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/231)): HLD [§2.2 T4.33](design/HLD.md#t433--parser-result-failure-classification-and-resume-partial), LLD [§2.0](design/LLD.md#20-antigravity-adapter-implementation-evidence-m4-t432-t433-t437), TDD [§4.4.7](design/TDD.md#447-antigravity-parser-implementation-evidence-m4-t433), SDD [§4.2](design/SDD.md#42-comp-adp-m4-parser-implementation-evidence-partial) |

Hardening (2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved; "cursor parser hardening (T4.43, synthetic shapes)"): a second `result` line and a session id that changes mid-run end `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` (the first id stays bound); an `apiKeySource` that is present but not the string `login` (including a non-string) is `AUTH_REQUIRED`; negative, non-finite and non-number usage values are dropped and no usage row is written without one; a `tool_call` object with several keys reports each, so a decoy key cannot hide a shell call or a refusal (Assumption: one key was recorded); a shell call without a string command reaches the guard without one (pinned); unrecorded rate-limit, quota or sandbox text ends `TOOL_FAILURE`, never `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`, `SANDBOX_VIOLATION` or success. Guard verdict (2026-10-15, Delegated, not owner-approved; merged [PR #238](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/238); the same ADR-0039 gap T4.36 fixed for Antigravity): `extractResult` now treats a `tool_call` carrying `violation` as not clean, so a guard-flagged Git write with a `success` result at exit 0 ends `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` and a `provider_state` verdict `SANDBOX_VIOLATION` (tests "a guard-flagged Git write with a success result ends PERMISSION_BLOCKED (ADR-0039)" and "any guard verdict on a tool_call makes a success result not clean"). Update (2026-10-15, Delegated, not owner-approved; synthetic lines, no new capture; merged [PR #248](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/248)): own-key `tool_call` lookup so keys such as `constructor`, `__proto__`, `toString` and `hasOwnProperty` map to `other`; a `session_id` that resume could not pass as one argument is never bound (`OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`; an earlier good id stays bound); a `tool_call` after the `result` line is not a success (`OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` with detail `tool call after the result line`, or `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` when the guard flagged it); non-string path, command and model values are dropped, not coerced (Assumption gap pinned). Update (2026-10-15, Delegated, not owner-approved; synthetic lines, no new capture): structured backing_provider on init or result sets backingProvider on session_bound (clipped 128 chars, non-strings dropped) and feeds provider_reported usage observations with harness "cursor" and backing vendor side by side (T4.6, FR-ADP-10); without backing_provider it stays unset (never inferred from model display name); a completed tool_call reporting a newly provided or swapped command/path is emitted for the ADR-0039 guard without duplicating identical reports; own-property checks on all structured property reads and prototype-named types/`__proto__` smuggling prevented; an unreadable `tool_call` line after the `result` line fails closed as a trailing tool (`OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`); a guard-flagged trailing `tool_call` ends `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` at exit 1, after a failed result and after a second result (ADR-0039). **Not done, so not `Done`:** (1) no rate-limit or sandbox-violation event shape was recorded (a failed run prints no stream, live check rows 4 and 14), so the parser emits neither and such failures end `TOOL_FAILURE` on the exit code; the stderr text is not read because the adapter contract passes none to `extractResult`; (2) native Cursor CLI captures (live check row 102) report only a model display name and no vendor, and TDD §4.5 forbids deriving one from the display name, so without an explicit structured backing_provider (supported for fronted harnesses, T4.6) backingProvider stays unset. Needs the operator: a capture of a rate-limited or quota-stopped Cursor run, and of any native field naming the backing vendor if one exists (T4.41 extension). Design evidence (merged [PR #231](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/231), [PR #238](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/238) ADR-0039 `extractResult` guard): HLD [§2.3 T4.43](design/HLD.md#t443--parser-result-failure-classification-and-resume-partial), LLD [§2.0.1](design/LLD.md#201-cursor-adapter-implementation-evidence-m4-t442-t443), TDD [§4.5.5](design/TDD.md#455-cursor-parser-implementation-evidence-m4-t443), SDD [§4.2](design/SDD.md#42-comp-adp-m4-parser-implementation-evidence-partial) |
