fixtures only, no `opencode` was run by this task).** Evidence: `src/adapters/opencode/` (`adapter.ts`, `protocol.ts` with every spelling cited to a TDD §4.6 source tag or the T4.51 live check, the `--` before the prompt marked an Assumption), `src/adapters/manifests/opencode.json` (range `<0.0.0`), and one registry entry (`create` and `--auto` declared bypass-class in `src/adapters/providers.ts`), with no other source edit apart from tests that listed the registered adapters and a `serve` comment. `src/adapters/opencode/opencode.test.ts`: every non-`privileged` profile is `PROFILE_UNSUPPORTED` with or without an approval; the `scopeShapes()` sweep compiles exactly one shape (approved bypass, worktree files, shell `broad`, network `on`, repository configuration `enabled`) as `run --format json --standalone -- <prompt>` with `OPENCODE_DISABLE_AUTOUPDATE=1` and no `OPENCODE_PERMISSION` or `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`; `--auto` is refused outside the approved bypass case and admitted inside it, and a prompt of `--auto` is refused; probe, auth, parser and resume placeholders fail closed; a source inspection finds no credential file read. **Delegated decisions (fail closed):** (1) the four non-`privileged` profiles and a `privileged` scope without `bypass` are `Ineligible` (Q-64); (2) the approved bypass cell compiles per owner decision 46 for the scope OpenCode cannot narrow below and emits no `--auto` (Q-83); (3) `--standalone` is always emitted so the tools run inside the supervised process group rather than the host's shared service; (4) `gitWriteControl` `none-documented`, `commitChannel` `finalize-only`, `resumeCwd` `unverified`. **Not done here (their own tasks):** parser and resume (T4.53), auth preflight (T4.54, `UNKNOWN` until then), conformance, malicious leg and README (T4.55 to T4.59). Limits: the prompt is an argv word, so it is visible in the process list, and an objective that is exactly a bypass-class flag is refused by the audit; whether `--` is honoured and whether `OPENCODE_DISABLE_AUTOUPDATE` has an effect are unobserved (T4.58) |
| T4.53 | OpenCode parser, result, failure classification and resume, with `backingProvider` and model on `session_bound` | FR-ADP-04, 10, FR-SES-03 | T4.52, T4.6 | As T4.43 **Status: Partial (2026-10-14, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved; synthetic lines in the shapes of the T4.51 live check).** Evidence: `parseEvent`, `extractResult` and `resumeArgs` in `src/adapters/opencode/adapter.ts` (event names, error types and token keys in `src/adapters/opencode/protocol.ts`); `src/adapters/opencode/opencode.test.ts` "opencode parser and result (T4.53)": success (session id from the first `sessionID`, text joined, one `provider_reported` usage row per `step_finish` with a repeated part counted once, `stop` as the result); a run that exits 0 with text but no `step_finish` `stop` fails (Q-85); `provider.no-route`, `provider.quota`, `provider.auth` and an unknown error type map to `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`, `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`, `AUTH_REQUIRED` and `TOOL_FAILURE`; shell, read and write tool calls carry their command or path for the tool-call guard; a `tool_call` the guard flagged with a `violation` makes a `stop` finish at exit 0 not clean, so a Git write ends `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` and a `provider_state` verdict `SANDBOX_VIOLATION` (ADR-0039, as T4.36; fixed 2026-10-15, Delegated, not owner-approved; "a guard-flagged Git write with a stop finish at exit 0 ends PERMISSION_BLOCKED (ADR-0039)"); a non-zero exit, timeout and cancel never succeed; malformed and unknown lines are counted `unknown` events; the session id never comes from text; resume is `--session <id>` through the same matrix, refusing another provider's binding, a flag-like id and a non-`privileged` profile. Update (2026-10-14, Delegated): usage records only finite non-negative numbers for the observed token keys and cost; negative, non-number and unexpected token keys are dropped; an unknown line's type is clipped to 64 characters; a session id that changes mid-run fails `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` (the first id stays bound); non-string and unstructured error fields fail the run (never ignored); non-object root documents pollute nothing and are counted `unknown`; resume refuses every non-privileged profile and dangerous session id; "cases with no recorded native shape" pins the observed outcomes: an out-of-worktree denial (live check row 11) ends with status `error` and exit 0 and is not detected as a denial (the run succeeds, gap pinned); an unrecognised error line or bare non-zero exit (row 14c, rate limit never reached) is `TOOL_FAILURE`, never `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT` or success; tool input keys without command/target leave command and target omitted (assumption gap pinned). Update (2026-10-15, Delegated, not owner-approved; synthetic lines, no new capture; merged [PR #245](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/245)): tool names and error types that name `Object.prototype` members map to `other` and `unknown`; a `sessionID` resume could not pass as one argument is never bound and fails `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`; a `tool_call` after the last `stop` step is not a success (Q-85); non-string command and path values are omitted, never coerced (gap pinned). **Not done, so not `Done`:** (1) no rate-limit, permission-denial or sandbox event shape was recorded (no deny took effect and no rate limit was reached, live check rows 7, 14c), so the parser emits none of them; (2) the keys of `tool_use.part.state.input` were not recorded, so `command` and `filePath`/`path` are Assumptions and a shell call without them is invisible to the guard; (3) the T4.6 fixture "backing vendor unlike the harness" cannot be written: events name no provider or model (live check row 4c) and the adapter passes no `--model`. Needs the operator: a capture of a shell and a write `tool_use` line with their input keys, and of a rate-limited run (T4.51 extension). Design evidence (merged [PR #231](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/231)): HLD [§2.4 T4.53](design/HLD.md#t453--parser-result-failure-classification-and-resume-partial), LLD [§2.0.2](design/LLD.md#202-opencode-adapter-implementation-evidence-m4-t452-t453), TDD [§4.6.5](design/TDD.md#465-opencode-parser-implementation-evidence-m4-t453), SDD [§4.2](design/SDD.md#42-comp-adp-m4-parser-implementation-evidence-partial) |
| T4.54 | OpenCode auth preflight | FR-AUTH-01, 02, NFR-SEC-06 | T4.52 | As T4.24 **Status: Done (2026-10-14, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved; fixtures only, no `opencode` was run by this task).** Evidence: `authPreflight` in `src/adapters/opencode/adapter.ts` with its spellings in `src/adapters/opencode/protocol.ts`; `src/adapters/opencode/opencode.test.ts`: `OK` for a provider holding a non-environment connection, `AUTH_REQUIRED` naming `opencode auth login` for an empty list and for environment-only connections (`env` and `environment`, both spellings recorded by the T4.51 live check), `UNKNOWN` for text output, a non-array, an entry without connections, a connection without a type, another exit code, a timeout and a missing binary, with no provider or account name in any reason; only `auth list --format json --standalone` is run, with a 15 s timeout, and the source never names `auth export`. **Delegated deviations from Q-10:** the exit code cannot be the key (exit 0 in every state), so the JSON connection types are; environment-only providers count as logged out, because a run gets no provider key from the environment; the type a stored login shows was not recorded, so any non-environment type is read as a login (**Assumption**, T4.58 to confirm); `--standalone` keeps the call off the host's shared service. Limit: the free tier answered without any login in the live check (row 4b), so `AUTH_REQUIRED` can refuse a run that would have worked on it (fail closed) |

**T4.1 status note (2026-10-13, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).** Merged (PR #56, `e3d42f1`; the note was
first written on branch `m4-registry`): the provider registry table `BUILTIN_PROVIDERS` in `src/adapters/providers.ts`
now derives `ProviderId`,
`PROVIDER_IDS` and the provider parts of the config schema, `CONFIG_TO_ADAPTER_ID`, and the `serve` and `doctor`
registrations; each entry declares its bypass-class flags, which the argv audit adds to the unchanged `BYPASS_FLAGS`
([ADR-0035](adr/0035-provider-registry-table-and-declared-bypass-flags.md); [LLD §2](design/LLD.md#2-adapter-contract)
table updated). Evidence: `src/adapters/providers.test.ts` (scratch adapter from
`src/adapters/fixtures/scratch-provider.ts` in `doctor`, `list_capabilities` and config validation; the T3.17 matrix
over every declared flag). Limits: Claude Code and Codex stay exported from `src/adapters/index.ts` (new adapters are
not added there); a runtime-registered id is outside the TypeScript `ProviderId` union, and the MCP dynamic-project
schema keeps the built-in config ids.

**T4.9 status note (2026-10-13, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).** Implemented on branch `m4-macos`, not yet
merged; the macOS path was **Unverified** (written on Linux, fixtures only) until T4.10 ran it (2026-10-14: it runs on
the macOS CI leg; the `EPERM` finding is in T4.10). One platform port, `src/exec/proc-info.ts`, answers "start time of
a PID" and "does this group have a live non-zombie member": `/proc` on Linux (the M1 behaviour, moved unchanged), BSD
`/bin/ps -A` with fixed `-o` columns under `LC_ALL=C` and `TZ=UTC` on macOS (start time stored as UTC epoch seconds
from `lstart`), and nothing elsewhere. Any failure or unparseable output means "cannot verify": the orphan check
reports `unverifiable` and never signals (before, a missing `/proc` read as "leader gone"), and the cancel loop counts
the group as alive. Mechanism, sources and limits: [LLD §8.3](design/LLD.md#83-process-table-per-platform-m4-t49); TDD
§2 and the README requirements line updated. Evidence: `src/exec/proc-info.test.ts` (both parsers, grandchild by
group, zombies, PID reuse by start time, unparseable or failed `ps`, unsupported platforms) and
`src/reconcile/process.test.ts` (every orphan rule over injected Linux, macOS and unsupported tables). Further
Linux-only assumptions, listed, not fixed: the `alive()` helper in `src/exec/supervisor.test.ts` reads `/proc` and
returns false on macOS, so its child and grandchild kill checks would passed vacuously there (made portable by T4.10);
a Linux host whose `/proc` cannot be read still treats the leader as gone (M1 behaviour kept); the socket path cap of
100 bytes (`src/git/tool-protocol.ts`) is assumed to fit the macOS `sun_path` limit, which is not cited;
case-insensitive APFS volumes were not examined for path and ref comparisons; the signing tests skip without `gpg`
(T4.10 lists skips). `/dev/null`, `detached` sessions, `kill(-pgid, …)` and the data-directory lock are POSIX and need
no change.

#### 6.8.1 Order, waves and hot files

- **Wave A (agents, now; nothing needs a provider CLI):** T4.1, T4.7, T4.9 and the spikes T4.20, T4.30, T4.40, T4.50,
  T4.60 and T4.70.
- **Wave B:** T4.2, T4.3, T4.5 and T4.6 after T4.1; T4.4 after T4.3; T4.8 after T4.7; T4.10 after T4.9; each
  provider's `T4.n2` after T4.1 and its spike, in [R2]'s order (L946): Grok Build, Antigravity, Cursor, OpenCode.
  Provider groups can run in parallel because each lives in its own module directory once T4.1 is done.
- **Wave C:** `T4.n3` to `T4.n7` and `T4.n9` per provider. Kimi Code waits for B-1; Muse Code (Should) goes last.
- **Operator, whenever the CLIs are available:** T4.11, T4.12, each `T4.n1` (after its spike) and each `T4.n8`.
- **Hot files:** `src/adapters/audit.ts`, `src/adapters/registry.ts`, `src/adapters/types.ts`, `src/config/schema.ts`
  and `src/routing/collect.ts` (T4.1 only; afterwards a provider adds one registry entry); `src/exec/` (T4.8, T4.9);
  `src/adapters/conformance.ts` and `test/malicious/` (T4.3 before any `T4.n5` or `T4.n6`); `src/cli/` (T4.4);
  `docs/design/TDD.md` (each spike edits only its own §4 subsection; merge one at a time); `README.md` (`T4.n9` and
  T4.90). Tasks sharing a hot file run one after another, never in parallel (AGENTS.md).
- No code that emits a new provider's bypass-class flag lands before T4.1.

#### 6.8.2 Blocks Done: what needs the operator or zemdregon

M4 can reach `Partial` on fakes and fixtures (NFR-MAINT-01 by diff inspection, FR-OPS-02's mechanics, FR-EXE-03 on a
fake agent, FR-ADP-05, FR-ADP-10, FR-ADP-11 on fixtures, NFR-PORT-02 on the macOS CI leg). `Done` also needs:

1. **The operator:** Grok Build, Antigravity, Cursor and OpenCode installed with native logins, for T4.21, T4.31,
   T4.41 and T4.51 and the live legs T4.28, T4.38, T4.48 and T4.58: NFR-SEC-03's "no escape" for these adapters and
   the criterion "`adapters validate` accepts a new CLI version only after its live checks pass" need real CLIs.
   zemdregon holds the subscriptions (owner decision 5); which of these CLIs are installed on the operator's machine is
   not recorded anywhere.
2. **B-1 (Kimi Code):** Moonshot documentation (T4.60) or a validated installed version (T4.61). Per the M4 row, M4 may
   be `Partial` with only Kimi Code outstanding.
3. **zemdregon:** nothing outstanding from Q-58 (answered, owner decision 46: a bypass cell may rest on FR-GIT-03
   alone). Any `injected` channel for a new provider (not planned here) needs its own ADR, and zemdregon's answer if
   it changes a trust or credential boundary (AGENTS.md).
4. **Muse Code (Should):** Q-27 (Meta access and login method) before T4.71 and T4.78. FR-ADP-12 is not in the M4
   acceptance criteria, so M4 can be `Done` without it (Delegated reading).
5. **macOS:** T4.10 is agent work; T4.12 needs a Mac and is optional for the criterion "the test suite passes on
   macOS".

Later milestones (M7 and M8) are intentionally kept at the level of the table in §6; break them into tasks when the
preceding milestone completes. M4's breakdown (§6.8) was written early, on 2026-10-13, while M3 and M9 were `Partial`,
because its spikes and shared groundwork need no live provider. M5 now has a breakdown too (§6.9, 2026-10-14, while M4
was `Partial`), for the same reason and under §9.1 step 2: most of its tasks run on fake adapters. M6 has one as
well (§6.11, 2026-10-14), written when M5 reached `Partial` with only an operator task left.

### 6.9 M5 Continuity: tasks

*Proposed breakdown (2026-10-14, Delegated agent work, documentation only; not owner-approved), written under §9.1
step 2 from the M5 row in §6 and the requirements it lists.* M5 depends on M4, which is `Planned`. Only T5.8 uses M4
output (each new adapter's resume); the other tasks run on the fake adapter, Claude Code and Codex, so under §9.1 step 1
agents may start them when no M4 task is available (Proposed). Design:
[ADR-0001](adr/0001-controller-owned-canonical-state.md) (controller-owned state, no transcript parsing),
[ADR-0006](adr/0006-worktree-checkpoints-no-blind-replay.md), [FRD WF-2](requirements/FRD.md#wf-2-continue-on-the-same-provider),
[FRD WF-3](requirements/FRD.md#wf-3-cross-provider-handoff), [FRD §3](requirements/FRD.md#3-business-rules) (BR-8,
BR-18, BR-40), [FRD §5](requirements/FRD.md#5-meta-session-lifecycle),
[FRD §7.2](requirements/FRD.md#72-continue_task), [HLD §6.2](design/HLD.md#62-cross-provider-handoff-wf-3),
[SDD §2](design/SDD.md#2-component-collaboration-per-workflow), [LLD §1](design/LLD.md#1-data-model),
[LLD §2](design/LLD.md#2-adapter-contract), [LLD §3](design/LLD.md#3-mcp-tool-contracts),
[LLD §5](design/LLD.md#5-workspace-and-lease-algorithm) and
[TDD §5](design/TDD.md#5-implementation-strategy-by-milestone). No LLD section defines the handoff bundle yet; T5.1
writes it. *(2026-10-14: T5.1 wrote it, [LLD §15](design/LLD.md#15-continuity-and-the-handoff-bundle-m5), under
[ADR-0038](adr/0038-handoff-bundle-and-continuity.md), Proposed.)*

**Facts the breakdown relies on (checked against `main` at `ce40972`, 2026-10-14).**

- FR-SES-01 is done (M1). FR-SES-02 is **partly built**: `meta_session` has `objective`, `constraints_json`,
  `repo_root`, `base_commit`, `head_commit` and `open_work_json` (`src/ledger/migrations/0001_initial.ts`). Objective
  and constraints are written at creation (`src/ledger/repo.ts`), `repo_root` and `base_commit` by the workspace
  manager and `head_commit` at finalize (`src/workspace/manager.ts`). Not built: nothing writes `open_work_json`; the
  `decision` table and its append-only triggers exist (migration 0001) but no code inserts a row; changed files and
  verification results exist per run only (`changed_files` artifact, verification event and `test_report`), not as
  meta-session state; `provider_binding` rows are written (`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`, "FR-SES-02/03: remember the
  native session") but `recordProviderBinding` stores `harness` equal to `provider` and no backing provider or model,
  although `usage_observation` already carries both (T4.6).
- FR-SES-03 is **partly built**: `continue_task` (`continueTask` in `src/mcp/server.ts`) resumes the latest binding of
  the bound provider through the adapter's `resumeArgs` (`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`). `resumeArgs` exists for Claude
  Code, Codex (its form is an Assumption in `src/adapters/codex/adapter.ts`) and the fake adapter, and `resume` is one
  of the conformance suite's `REQUIRED_CASE_KINDS` (`src/adapters/conformance.ts`). Each continuation runs in a new
  worktree, `<data>/worktrees/<sess>/<run>` from the head checkpoint (`src/workspace/manager.ts`), not in the previous
  run's worktree as FRD WF-2's Proposed text says. Whether a provider's resume depends on the working directory is the
  WF-2 **Assumption**, still unchecked; the M4 spikes list it as an operator check for Antigravity
  ([TDD §4.4.3](design/TDD.md#443-t431-operator-checklist-antigravity), C9), Cursor
  ([TDD §4.5.3](design/TDD.md#453-t441-operator-checklist), item 8) and Muse Code (`--allow-workspace-switch`,
  [TDD §4.8](design/TDD.md#48-muse-code-m4)).
- FR-SES-04 is **not built**: `continueTask` refuses a different provider ("cross-provider continuation ... is not
  available yet") and a meta-session with no binding. `CompileRequest.handoff` is declared
  (`src/adapters/types.ts`) and unused. The `handoff_view` artifact kind is allowed by the ledger
  (`src/ledger/migrations/0009_pr_draft_artifact.ts`) and listed by `get_artifacts` (`src/mcp/schemas.ts`), but nothing
  writes it. The only bundle text in the LLD is the `handoff_condense` input of
  [LLD §13.6](design/LLD.md#136-task-contracts).
- FR-SES-05: `run_event` is append-only (M1); no handoff view exists.
- Only one fake provider exists (`fake`). The T4.1 runtime registration (`registerProvider` in
  `src/adapters/providers.ts`, used by `src/adapters/fixtures/scratch-provider.ts`) lets a test add a second one with no
  production source edit.

**Conventions (Proposed, Delegated).**

- T5.0 is a documentation-only spike. T5.1 is the design gate: no code that writes a bundle, a `decision` row or a
  `handoff_view` artifact lands before its ADR and LLD text merge, because the bundle is a new persisted format and
  carries one provider's reported text into another provider's prompt (AGENTS.md: persistence format and trust
  boundary).
- Every test runs on fake adapters; the second fake is registered by tests only. No task here calls a live provider
  except the operator task T5.12, which is skipped unless `SWITCHBOARD_LIVE_TESTS=1`.
- No number is invented: bundle size and excerpt bounds are configuration values whose defaults T5.1 chooses and labels
  as Assumptions.
- Until Q-61 is answered, a cross-provider continuation whose resolved profile is `privileged` is refused with
  `INVALID_REQUEST` (fail closed, Proposed default); same-provider `privileged` continuations keep FRD C7 unchanged.

Each cell is sourced or reads `Unverified: <operator task>`. No cell is observed: it is a documentation inventory, and the operator tasks settle the `Unverified` cells | FR-SES-03, FR-ADP-04, FRD WF-2 (the working-directory Assumption), [TDD §4.1](design/TDD.md#41-claude-code-m2), [§4.2](design/TDD.md#42-codex-m2), [§4.3.1](design/TDD.md#431-grok-build-native-controls-per-profile-and-platform-t420) (Resume row), §4.4.3 C9, §4.5.3 item 8, §4.6.3 item 13, §4.8, R-7 | — | A new TDD §4 subsection holds the table with a source or `Unverified` in every cell; the Claude Code and Codex checks are added to T5.12 and the others to the M4 operator checklists they belong to; `node scripts/check-docs.mjs` passes |
| T5.1 | Continuity design (documentation only). **Needs a new ADR first** (next free number, under ADR-0001): the bundle is a new persisted format (`handoff_view` content), it carries provider-reported text across a trust boundary into another provider's prompt ([HLD §3](design/HLD.md#3-trust-boundaries) TB-5, R-63), and `continue_task` gains new behaviour. Contract text in LLD (a new section, with §1 and §2 touched). **Open design questions the ADR must answer:** (1) the bundle schema: each FR-SES-04 field with its only source (ledger row, normalized `run_event`, Git object), field order (stable content first, FR-RTE-09), bounds, a version, and an `as_of` (head commit and last event sequence) so the view is a pure function of append-only records and Git (FR-SES-05); (2) the `handoff_view` content and media type; (3) which controller events become `decision` rows (Proposed: route choice, handoff, rollback, approval, verification outcome) and whether orchestrator-supplied decisions or open tasks need a new MCP input, which would be an external-interface change amending FR-MCP-03 and LLD §3; (4) how open work is derived, and whether `open_work_json` becomes a projection with an event for each change (SDD §1 pattern); (5) who renders the prompt (SDD WF-3 says COMP-ADP; Proposed: one provider-neutral renderer so adapters stay unchanged, NFR-MAINT-01), the "inspect the repository first" instruction, and how provider-reported text (summaries, failure details) is carried: bounded, redacted, labelled as untrusted data; (6) the resume-working-directory rule from T5.0: a stable per-meta-session worktree path, a resume-only `Ineligible` that BR-18 turns into a handoff, or a failure (FRD WF-2 keeps "no silent fallback" for a resume the provider rejects at run time); (7) the failure code when a bundle cannot be built (FRD WF-3 proposes `TOOL_FAILURE`); (8) `privileged` across providers, per Q-61 | FR-SES-02, 03, 04, 05, FR-INN-13, NFR-PRIV-02, FR-TEL-03, FR-RTE-09, ADR-0001, ADR-0005, ADR-0006, LLD §1, §2, §5, §13.6, FRD WF-2, WF-3, BR-18, BR-40, SDD §2 | T5.0 | ADR (Proposed) and LLD text merged; FRD WF-2 and WF-3 Proposed items, the SDD WF-2 and WF-3 rows and HLD §6.2 agree with them; any new ledger column or table is specified as a forward migration in LLD §1; R-63 updated; if (3) adds an MCP input, SRS FR-MCP-03 or a new FR is amended in the same change; `node scripts/check-docs.mjs` passes. *Status (2026-10-14, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved): design done.* [ADR-0038](adr/0038-handoff-bundle-and-continuity.md) (Proposed) answers (1) to (8) and [LLD §15](design/LLD.md#15-continuity-and-the-handoff-bundle-m5) holds the contract (bundle fields with one source each, `as_of`, bounds, decision kinds, open-work derivation, renderer, resume directory, `continue_task` steps), with pointers in LLD §1, §2, §3, §4 and §5. Answers in brief: (1) bundle `version` 1 from ledger rows, controller events, `changed_files` and `test_report` artifacts and Git plumbing, `as_of` = base, head and a per-run event watermark; (2) one `handoff_view` artifact, `application/json`, canonical JSON; (3) five controller decision kinds, **no new MCP input** (FR-MCP-03 unchanged); (4) open work derived, `open_work_json` a projection, no event; (5) one provider-neutral renderer in the lifecycle (adapters unchanged), untrusted text line-prefixed in labelled blocks; (6) a resume runs in the worktree path that created the native session unless the adapter is verified `independent`; (7) `TOOL_FAILURE` with `details.reason = "HANDOFF_BUNDLE_UNAVAILABLE"`, no new code; (8) Q-61 default kept as one constant. No ledger migration. FRD WF-2, WF-3 and §4.4, the SDD WF-2, WF-3, FR-SES-04 and FR-SES-05 rows and HLD §6.2 updated to agree; R-63 updated; Q-61 still open; `node scripts/check-docs.mjs` passes |

| T5.12 | **Operator-only.** Run T5.11 with native logins for Claude Code and Codex, plus the T5.0 checks for these two CLIs, each for Claude Code (`--resume <session_id>`) and Codex (`codex exec resume <SESSION_ID>`; the flag position and stdin prompt are the Assumption in `src/adapters/codex/adapter.ts`), results into the [TDD §4.10](design/TDD.md#410-native-resume-inventory-t50) cells: (1) resume from the same directory (baseline), recording the id returned and the exit code; (2) resume from a second worktree of the same repository and from an unrelated empty directory, recording whether the session is found, a new id is created or the CLI fails (exit code, error text); (3) resume with the sandbox and permission flags of the first run omitted, asking for an action the first run denied, recording whether the first run's mode still applies; (4) resume with different flags than the first run, recording which set applies; (5) an unknown id, for the exit code and error text (maps to `classifyFailure`, FRD WF-2) | M5 verification, FR-SES-03, 04, R-7, §6.2 "Blocks Done" item 1 | T5.11; the M2 live smoke run (§6.2 "Blocks Done" item 1) is recommended first | Results in a dated note under `docs/research/`; each check `PASS`, `FAIL` or `NOT_CHECKED` is recorded here, in TDD §4.1 and §4.2 and in the T5.0 table; no credential, token, account identifier or transcript content in any capture |

SDD WF-2 and WF-3, FRD WF-2 and WF-3 and HLD §6.2 were read against the code and already say what it does (T5.1, T5.7), so no edit was needed there. Remaining: after T5.12 the final M5 status and the Codex resume form |

#### 6.9.1 Order, waves and hot files

- **Wave A (agents, now; nothing needs a provider CLI):** T5.0 and T5.2.
- **Wave B:** T5.1 after T5.0. Q-61 is asked in the meantime; its fail-closed default lets T5.6 proceed.
- **Wave C:** T5.3 and T5.7 after T5.1; T5.4 after T5.3; T5.5 after T5.4; T5.6 after T5.2 and T5.5.
- **Wave D:** T5.9 after T5.6 and T5.7; T5.10 after T5.6; T5.11 after T5.9; T5.8 per adapter as M4's parser tasks
  land; T5.13 last.
- **Operator, whenever Claude Code and Codex are logged in:** T5.12.
- **Outside M5:** T11.10 (§6.6) waits for T5.4. M6's failover after `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` (BR-8, FR-CAP-06, FR-MCP-10)
  reuses T5.4 and T5.6. In M7 a handoff requested by the orchestrator is a `user_request` affinity switch (SDD routing
  note, FR-RTE-07); M5 writes no affinity.
- **Hot files:** `src/mcp/server.ts` and `src/mcp/schemas.ts` (T5.6, T5.10); `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` (T5.3, T5.5,
  T5.7); `src/ledger/repo.ts` and `src/ledger/migrations/` (T5.3, T5.4); `src/adapters/types.ts` (T5.5, T5.7);
  `src/workspace/manager.ts` (T5.7); `docs/design/LLD.md` (T5.1); `docs/design/TDD.md` (T5.0, T5.12). Tasks sharing a
  hot file run one after another, never in parallel (AGENTS.md).

#### 6.9.2 Blocks Done: what needs the operator or zemdregon

M5 can reach `Partial` on fakes: FR-SES-02 populated, FR-SES-04, the FR-SES-05 handoff view, the acceptance test T5.9,
and FR-SES-03 for Claude Code, Codex and the fakes on fixtures. `Done` also needs:

1. **The operator:** T5.12, the "one opt-in live pair" in the M5 row's verification column, with Claude Code and Codex
   installed and logged in. The Codex resume form is an Assumption in `src/adapters/codex/adapter.ts` until the M2 live
   smoke run (§6.2 "Blocks Done" item 1) confirms it.
2. **FR-SES-03 "(all)":** each M4 adapter's resume, on fixtures through T5.8 and live through that adapter's operator
   tasks (`T4.n1`, `T4.n8`). Whether each provider's resume is tied to the working directory is Unverified until those
   captures (T5.0). Delegated reading, as in §6.8.2 item 2: M5 may be `Partial` with only the adapters M4 has not
   delivered outstanding.
3. **zemdregon:** Q-61 (a durable `privileged` approval and a continuation on another provider). Until it is answered
   the fail-closed default applies. M5's acceptance criteria involve no `privileged` run, so the answer does not block
   `Done` (Delegated reading), but T5.6 changes if the answer is "covered".
4. **Not needed for M5:** T11.10 (condensation, M11) and the failover use of the bundle (M6).

### 6.10 M13 Network MCP transport: tasks

Proposed breakdown after owner decision Q-67 (2026-10-14). Each task names its evidence when Done.

| Task | Scope | Requirements | Depends on | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T13.1 | Spike the MCP SDK's Streamable HTTP and bearer-verification support, then accept ADR-0041 (owner) or revise it | FR-MCP-12, 13 | ADR-0040 | Spike note in TDD §3.14; ADR-0041 status updated with evidence |
| T13.2 | Config keys and listener: option off by default, loopback bind, `Origin` check | FR-MCP-12 | T13.1 | Tests: no listener when off; non-loopback refused without the explicit option; unlisted `Origin` gets 403 |
| T13.3 | Protected-resource metadata and token verifier (issuer, audience, expiry, signature) | FR-MCP-13 | T13.2 | Tests with generated fixtures: missing, expired, wrong-audience, wrong-issuer tokens refused; no token in responses |
| T13.4 | Subject-to-client mapping with frozen maximum profile; `privileged` never granted | FR-MCP-14 | T13.3 | Tests: token cannot raise profile; `privileged` claim refused; approval still needs the operator CLI |
| T13.5 | Session binding by subject and client; redaction at the telemetry boundary | FR-MCP-15 | T13.4 | Tests: session reuse by another subject refused; secret-pattern scan of responses and logs is clean |
| T13.6 | Threat model entry TB-12 reviewed; malicious-fixture suite extended for the HTTP path | FR-MCP-12..15, NFR-SEC-03 | T13.5 | HLD TB-12 linked to tests; suite passes |
| T13.7 | Dynamic Client Registration (RFC 7591) for public clients; each client pending until the operator approves it (added 2026-10-14, Delegated, Q-74) | FR-MCP-16 | T13.3 | Tests: only `https` redirect URIs, `none` auth and the code grant accepted; no secret issued; a registered client is `pending`; the pending cap refuses a flood |
| T13.8 | Authorization and token endpoints with the operator consent page: authorization code with PKCE S256 only, passphrase or one-time pairing code, refresh-token rotation, audience binding (added 2026-10-14, Delegated, Q-75, Q-76) | FR-MCP-17 | T13.7 | Tests: bad, missing or `plain` PKCE, wrong redirect URI, reused or expired code, wrong passphrase, reused or expired pairing code, lockout and a `privileged` scope are each refused; no secret value in logs, ledger rows or responses |
| T13.9 | End-to-end acceptance test: a client registers, completes PKCE `S256` on the consent page, exchanges the code, and with that token lists and calls tools over Streamable HTTP (added 2026-10-14, Delegated, to close the first M13 acceptance clause) | FR-MCP-12, 13, 16, 17 | T13.8, T13.4 | Test: the token from the code exchange lists the tools and a `delegate_task` call is admitted; no token, code or verifier in the responses or ledger (the serve log is covered by the T13.6 suite) |

Status (2026-10-14):

- **T13.1 Done.** The spike is recorded in [TDD §3.14](design/TDD.md#314-network-mcp-transport-m13) and the first
  slice is built: a strict `transport.http` config section (off by default; `https` public URL only; loopback bind unless
  `allow_non_loopback`), a `node:http` listener started by `serve` only when enabled, protected-resource metadata (RFC
  9728) and authorization-server metadata (RFC 8414), and a 401 challenge on the MCP endpoint that accepts no token yet.
  Evidence: `src/mcp/http.test.ts` (no server is created when off; both documents served; challenge names the metadata
  URL; no token echoed), `src/config/config.test.ts` (`transport.http` refusals).
  [ADR-0041](adr/0041-http-transport-with-oauth-2-1.md) is Accepted (Delegated, 2026-10-14). This slice also covers
  the "no listener when off" and "non-loopback refused" parts of T13.2 and the metadata part of T13.3; the other
  T13.3 parts are open.
- **T13.2 Done** (Delegated reading: every "Done when" test exists; ADR-0041 Accepted). No listener when off
  and non-loopback refused: the T13.1 tests above. Unlisted `Origin` gets 403 on every path before routing, including a
  DNS-rebinding `POST` and `Origin: null`; with no `allowed_origins` every request that has an `Origin` is refused:
  `src/mcp/http.test.ts`. Config refusals for a non-serialised origin: `src/config/config.test.ts`.
- **T13.3 Done** (Delegated reading under Q-73: tokens are opaque, so "wrong issuer" and "signature" are covered by the
  hash lookup: a token this server did not issue has no row). Protected-resource metadata: T13.1. Token verifier:
  `src/mcp/oauth-tokens.ts`, wired into the MCP endpoint in `src/mcp/http.ts` and into `serve` with the ledger-backed
  store; hashed token rows in migration 0010 (`oauth_token`, [LLD §3.3](design/LLD.md#33-network-mcp-transport-m13)).
  Evidence: `src/mcp/oauth-tokens.test.ts` (missing token gets the bare challenge; malformed, unknown, another
  issuer's, refresh-as-bearer, expired, revoked and wrong-audience tokens each get one identical 401 with
  `error="invalid_token"`; the log hook receives reason codes only; no token value in response headers or bodies or in
  any ledger file byte; a raw token in the hash column is refused by the schema), `src/ledger/ledger.test.ts`. A valid
  token gets 501 until T13.4 mounts the MCP server.
- **T13.7 Done** (Delegated, Q-74; added to the breakdown because ADR-0041 item 3 needs it and no task named it). `POST
  /register` and the `oauth_client` table (migration 0011): `src/mcp/oauth-clients.ts`, `src/mcp/http.ts`. Evidence:
  `src/mcp/oauth-clients.test.ts` (every refused metadata shape, including plain-HTTP and loopback redirect URIs, a
  client secret method, implicit, password and client-credentials grants, and control characters in the name; a valid
  registration is stored `pending` with no secret column or field; 400, 413, 415 and 405 store nothing; the pending
  cap gives 429 and frees as clients age out or are decided; the `Origin` check applies), `src/ledger/ledger.test.ts`.
  The consent page that approves a pending client is T13.8.
- **T13.8 Done** (Delegated, Q-75 and Q-76; added because ADR-0041 items 3 and 4 need it and no task named it).
  `/authorize` with the consent page and `/token`: `src/mcp/oauth-server.ts`, `src/mcp/oauth-consent-page.ts`; the
  scrypt passphrase hash (`transport.http.passphrase_hash`): `src/mcp/oauth-passphrase.ts`; one-time pairing codes
  (migration 0012, `oauth_pairing_code`): `src/mcp/oauth-pairing.ts`; `switchboard consent pair` and `switchboard
  consent hash-passphrase`: `src/cli/consent.ts`. Evidence: `src/mcp/oauth-server.test.ts` (happy path with
  audience-bound tokens and refresh rotation; missing, `plain` and malformed PKCE, a wrong or missing verifier, an
  unregistered redirect URI with no redirect, a reused code that also revokes its tokens, an expired code, a wrong
  passphrase, a reused, expired or superseded pairing code, the lockout refusing even the right passphrase, the
  pairing-code throttle, CSRF and cross-site refusals, error pages instead of redirects for unapproved clients,
  `privileged` and every other scope but `mcp`, refresh-token reuse revoking the client's tokens, concurrent rotation;
  anti-framing headers; after every test no secret in the audit events or any ledger file byte, and no secret in error
  responses), `src/cli/consent.test.ts`, `src/ledger/ledger.test.ts`.
- **T13.4 Done** (Delegated, Q-77). The MCP endpoint serves MCP: a valid token's OAuth client maps through
  `transport.http.clients` (or `default_mcp_client`) to an `mcp_clients` entry, and a stateless MCP server for that
  entry handles the request: `src/mcp/http.ts`, `src/config/http-transport.ts`, `src/config/load.ts`,
  `src/cli/serve.ts`. Evidence: `src/mcp/mcp.test.ts` ("MCP over the HTTP transport": over Streamable HTTP a token
  whose scopes claim `privileged` still gets the mapped `inspect-untrusted` ceiling, with `edit-untrusted` and
  `privileged` requests `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` and nothing written; a `privileged` request from a client mapped to a
  `privileged` entry is `AWAITING_APPROVAL` with one `PENDING` approval and no tool can approve; an unmapped client and
  a token without `mcp` get 403 `insufficient_scope` without a server being built; a revoked client's unexpired token
  gets 401; a default mapping applies; `GET`, `DELETE` and `PUT` get 405; the per-client in-flight cap gives 503; the
  credential headers are gone before the MCP transport reads the request), `src/config/config.test.ts` (unknown
  target, duplicate mapping, malformed id, a `not_required` target and a privileged-ceiling default refused).
- **T13.5 Done** (Delegated, Q-78). A request with an `Mcp-Session-Id` is refused; each meta-session opened over HTTP
  is bound to the token's OAuth client in `http_subject` (migration 0013) and its runs follow it; the serve log passes
  the secret redactor, which now matches `sbat_`, `sbrt_` and `sbac_` values: `src/mcp/http-subjects.ts`,
  `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/mcp/http.ts`, `src/cli/serve.ts`, `src/artifacts/redact.ts`. Evidence: `src/mcp/mcp.test.ts`
  ("HTTP session binding by subject and client": two OAuth clients mapped to one MCP client, and stdio for that client,
  get the "unknown id" answer of an id that does not exist from `get_run`, `cancel_run`, `get_artifacts` and
  `continue_task` on the other subject's run and the run stays `QUEUED`; another subject's idempotency key neither
  replays nor reveals the run; a stdio run is unknown over HTTP; a binding never changes hands; an HTTP server without
  the bindings refuses to start; an `Mcp-Session-Id` gets 400; after a run over raw HTTP with a token planted in the
  objective, no response header or body and no ledger row holds the token or any `sbat_`/`sbrt_`/`sbac_` shape),
  `src/cli/serve.test.ts` (a token or code in a log field leaves as `[REDACTED]`), `src/ledger/ledger.test.ts`. Both
  guards were shown to fail their tests when disabled.
- **T13.6 Done** (Delegated). [HLD TB-12](design/HLD.md#3-trust-boundaries) now covers the built transport and the
  embedded authorization server (consent page, registration, codes, refresh tokens), lists each control with its
  requirement and test file, and names the residual risks below the table. Evidence: `test/malicious/controller.test.ts`
  runs the whole controller-invariant suite twice, in memory and over Streamable HTTP with real OAuth tokens and the
  `serve` logger (the leak scan covers the log too), and adds "T13.6 hostile requests on the HTTP path": unlisted
  origins (DNS rebinding) refused with a valid token and nothing run; forged, other-audience, refresh-as-bearer,
  doubled and query-string credentials each 401, logged by reason only; a token claiming `privileged` cannot pass the
  ceiling or create an approval; a session ID gets 400 and another subject cannot read, continue or cancel a run; no
  OAuth token, token shape or fixture canary in the ledger, artifacts, MCP responses or the log.
- **T13.9 Done** (added and done 2026-10-14, Delegated). `src/mcp/mcp.test.ts` ("a client that completed PKCE against
  the embedded authorization server lists and calls tools (T13.9)"): over the real listener a client registers through
  `POST /register`, opens the consent page with a PKCE `S256` challenge, is approved with the passphrase, exchanges
  the code at `/token`, and with that access token an MCP client over Streamable HTTP lists the tools and calls
  `delegate_task`, whose run is admitted under the mapped MCP client and reaches a terminal state; afterwards no
  access token, refresh token, code, verifier or passphrase is in any response other than the redirect and the token
  answer, or in any ledger row. With it, every M13 acceptance clause maps to a passing test, and M13 is `Done` (row in
  §6). The other clauses: another audience (`src/mcp/oauth-tokens.test.ts`, T13.6 suite), unlisted `Origin`
  (`src/mcp/http.test.ts`), a `privileged` claim (`src/mcp/mcp.test.ts`, T13.6 suite), no token in responses, ledger
  or logs (T13.5 and T13.6 evidence above), and no listener when the option is off (`src/mcp/http.test.ts`).

### 6.11 M6 Capacity and resilience: tasks

*Proposed breakdown (2026-10-14, Delegated agent work, documentation only; not owner-approved), written under §9.1
step 2 from the M6 row in §6 and the requirements it lists.* M6 depends on M5, which is `Partial` with only the
operator task T5.12 left, so under §9.1 step 1 agents start M6 tasks that do not need that item (Proposed). It is
numbered after §6.10 so that the M13 section keeps its anchor. Design: [ADR-0007](adr/0007-capacity-as-confidence-estimate.md)
(capacity is a confidence-weighted estimate), [ADR-0032](adr/0032-provider-model-affinity.md) (affinity; cache and
switch counts in M6, the affinity step in M7), [ADR-0006](adr/0006-worktree-checkpoints-no-blind-replay.md) (no blind
replay), [ADR-0038](adr/0038-handoff-bundle-and-continuity.md) (the bundle a failover reuses),
[FRD WF-5](requirements/FRD.md#wf-5-failure-handling-and-retry), [FRD §3](requirements/FRD.md#3-business-rules) (BR-3,
BR-6 to BR-9, BR-41, BR-45), [FRD §4](requirements/FRD.md#4-canonical-run-state-machine) (T4, T7, T16, T18),
[SDD §1](design/SDD.md#1-shared-design-patterns) (circuit breaker, bounded queue, "estimate, not authority"),
[LLD §1](design/LLD.md#1-data-model) (`usage_observation`, `provider_health`, `run.parent_run_id`, `next_attempt_at`),
[LLD §3](design/LLD.md#3-mcp-tool-contracts) (`get_capacity`), [LLD §3.2](design/LLD.md#32-provider-and-model-affinity-m7)
(FR-CAP-08 accounting), [LLD §4](design/LLD.md#4-failure-taxonomy), [LLD §9](design/LLD.md#9-retryback-off-algorithm)
and [TDD §5](design/TDD.md#5-implementation-strategy-by-milestone) (M6 row). LLD §3 says of `get_capacity` and LLD §9
says of breakers, adaptive concurrency and failover "to be detailed in M6"; T6.1 writes that.

**Facts the breakdown relies on (checked against `main` at `5531c78`, 2026-10-14).**

- FR-CAP-01 (record) is **built** (M2, T2.5): `usage_observation` rows with `source` `provider_reported` from each
  adapter's `usage` events (`src/usage/observations.ts`); `source` also allows `controller_estimated` (never written)
  and `inner_agent` (T11.5, migration 0006). The MCP envelope's `usage.estimated` and `usage.capacity_confidence` are
  always `null` (`src/mcp/envelope.ts`). Nothing reads `usage_observation` to estimate anything.
- `provider_health` exists (migration 0001: `breaker_state` `CLOSED`/`OPEN`/`HALF_OPEN`, `breaker_until`,
  `consecutive_failures`, `last_failure_code`, `capacity_json`) and **no code reads or writes it**. The only circuit
  breaker in the code is the in-memory inner-agent one (`src/inner/breaker.ts`, T11.2), which is not a provider breaker.
- FR-CAP-04 is **partly built** (M1): the bounded queue (`QUEUE_FULL` at admission, Q-5), the global limit and one
  `limits.per_provider_concurrency` value applied to every provider in `pump()` (`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`; defaults
  4 and 1 in `src/config/load.ts`, the NFR-PERF-03 proposed targets). No per-provider breaker; the eligibility filter
  (`src/routing/eligibility.ts`) has no circuit or capacity reason. NFR-PERF-03 "per provider" is therefore met only as
  one shared value, not a value per provider.
- FR-CAP-05 and FR-MCP-10 are **not built**: at FINALIZING the lifecycle records `retry_eligible` and `auto_retry:
  false` in the `workspace_assessment` event (`RETRYABLE` in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` and
  `src/reconcile/reconcile.ts`); `run.parent_run_id`, `attempt` and `next_attempt_at` exist and nothing sets them for a
  retry; `WAITING_CAPACITY` is a valid state (cancel and reconciliation handle it) but no transition enters it; `get_run`
  has no successor field. The FRD's `retry_of` link has no column of that name; LLD §9 uses `parent_run_id`.
- FR-CAP-06: `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` is a failure code (LLD §4) and BR-3 maps "known quota exhaustion" to it, but nothing
  knows a quota is exhausted, nothing fails over and there is no `capacity.queue_horizon` key (named in
  [TDD §3.1](design/TDD.md#31-configuration), absent from `src/config/schema.ts`). The M5 handoff bundle and
  cross-provider `continue_task` (T5.4 to T5.6) exist and are what BR-8 failover reuses.
- Rate-limit signals: adapters emit a `rate_limit` event with an optional `resetAt` (Claude Code from
  `rate_limit_event.rate_limit_info`, `src/adapters/claude/parse.ts`; Codex and the fake adapter), stored as a bounded
  `run_event` payload with `reset_at` (`compactEvent` in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`). The Claude shape is an
  Assumption in that parser; no provider in the research reports remaining quota.
- Cache token counts (Q-53): reported keys seen so far are `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens`
  from Grok Build 1.0.50 ([live check 4a](research/live-checks-2026-10-14-grok.md)), `cacheReadTokens` and
  `cacheWriteTokens` from Cursor CLI ([live check 5a](research/live-checks-2026-10-14-cursor.md)), and `cache.read` and
  `cache.write` in OpenCode's documented `step_finish` ([TDD §4.6](design/TDD.md#46-opencode-m4)). Codex's
  `cached_input_tokens` is in a **synthetic** fixture only (`src/adapters/codex/fixtures/success_edit.jsonl`). Claude
  Code's parser copies every numeric `usage.*` key without naming cache keys. None is normalised.
- `get_capacity`, `switchboard capacity` and a `capacity` configuration section do not exist. `providers.<id>.weight`
  exists in the schema (a number in [0, 1]) and nothing reads it (ADR-0007's "relative weight ... as a prior").
- T11.5 (§6.6) left one clause for M6: `get_capacity` and the estimator must be byte-identical with and without inner
  calls, and must read `inner_agent` rows deliberately or ignore them, never as `provider_reported`.

**Conventions (Proposed, Delegated).**

- **Fail closed on unknown capacity.** No provider reports remaining quota in any source we hold, so an estimate built
  from nothing has `capacity_score` and `explicit_remaining` `null` and `confidence` 0, and every surface shows it as
  unknown, never as headroom. `explicit_remaining` is filled only from a key the adapter declares as provider-reported
  remaining capacity, and no adapter declares one until a source shows it (FR-CAP-03). "Known quota exhaustion" for
  BR-3 and FRD T7 needs positive evidence (a `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` failure with a `reset_at` still in the future, or an
  open breaker); an unknown estimate does not make a provider ineligible, because that would make every provider
  ineligible. Where a number is needed and no source gives one (breaker threshold and cool-down, backoff, queue
  horizon), the default is the conservative value LLD §9 or NFR-PERF-03 already proposes, or the most restrictive
  sensible one, labelled an **Assumption**, and configurable.
- **No invented provider numbers.** No task states a provider's limit, price, quota, reset window or tier size. T6.0
  sources what each CLI reports; anything unsourced reads `Unverified: <operator task>`. FR-CAP-07's rate gate applies
  only to a provider whose enforceable limit for the auth mode in use is sourced, and stays off otherwise.
- **Design gate.** T6.1 (documentation only) writes the contracts before code: the estimator's inputs and output, the
  `get_capacity` and `capacity` output, the breaker and retry state, the successor link and the `capacity` config keys.
  Any new table, column or event type is specified in LLD §1 first and gets an ADR (AGENTS.md: persistence format and
  external interface).
- Every test runs on fake adapters with an injected clock and random source; no task here calls a live provider except
  the operator task T6.11, which is skipped unless `SWITCHBOARD_LIVE_TESTS=1`. Adapter code (`src/adapters/**`) is not
  edited for M6 except to declare a field T6.0 sources; normalisation of reported keys lives in the capacity module.
- M6 writes no affinity row: the `project_affinity` table, the selection step and `affinity_switch_reason` are M7
  (LLD §3.2). FR-CAP-08's kept-versus-switched counts are computed from runs and `route_decision` rows, with the reason
  `unrecorded` until M7 writes one.

#### 6.11.1 Order, waves and hot files

- **Wave A (agents, now; nothing needs a provider CLI or an owner decision):** T6.0 and T6.1 (documentation), then
  T6.2, T6.4, T6.5 and T6.9 after T6.1, and T6.3 after T6.2.
- **Wave B:** T6.6 after T6.5 and T6.2; T6.7 after T6.0 and T6.3; T6.10 after T6.3 to T6.7 and T6.9; T6.12 last.
- **Blocked:** T6.8 until T6.0 sources an enforceable limit (else `Deferred`).
- **Operator, whenever Claude Code and Codex are logged in:** T6.11.
- **Outside M6:** M7 adds the affinity step and `affinity_switch_reason`; T6.6's failover is then a `capacity_exhausted`
  switch (FR-RTE-07) and T6.7's `unrecorded` reasons become recorded ones. T11.5's open clause (§6.6) closes with T6.2.
- **Hot files:** `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` (T6.4, T6.5, T6.6, T6.9; its admission redaction belongs to the Q-68
  work, which M6 does not touch); `src/routing/eligibility.ts` (T6.4, T6.6); `src/mcp/server.ts` and
  `src/mcp/schemas.ts` (T6.3, T6.5; the HTTP transport, OAuth and Origin code in `src/mcp/http.ts` is M13's and is not
  edited); `src/config/schema.ts` and `src/config/load.ts` (T6.2, T6.4, T6.9); `src/reconcile/reconcile.ts` (T6.5);
  `docs/design/LLD.md` (T6.1). M6 does not edit TDD §4 (M4's). Tasks sharing a hot file run one after another, never in
  parallel (AGENTS.md).

#### 6.11.2 Blocks Done: what needs the operator or zemdregon

M6 can reach `Partial` on fakes: the estimator, `get_capacity` and `capacity`, the breaker, retry with successors, the
quota ladder, per-provider limits and the acceptance test T6.10. `Done` also needs:

1. **The operator:** T6.11, so that the rate-limit and quota shapes the Claude Code and Codex parsers assume are
   checked; until then the retry and quota paths are proven on fakes and synthetic fixtures only.
2. **Sources for provider facts:** which CLIs report cache counts (Q-53) and whether any provider publishes an
   enforceable limit for its subscription login (FR-CAP-07). Without a source, cache figures stay absent for that
   provider and T6.8 is `Deferred`; neither blocks the M6 acceptance criteria (Delegated reading).
3. **zemdregon:** nothing in this breakdown changes what a bypass run may do, so no owner question is opened. A retry or
   failover of a `privileged` run follows the M5 rule: a failover to another provider is the cross-provider case of
   Q-61 and is refused while Q-61 is open (fail closed), and a same-provider retry runs under the approval as is.
4. **Not needed for M6:** the M7 affinity step (`project_affinity`, `affinity reset`, the replay test).

### 6.12 M7 Routing: tasks

*Proposed breakdown (2026-10-14, Delegated agent work, documentation only; not owner-approved), written under §9.1
step 2 from the M7 row in §6 and the requirements it lists.* M6 is `Partial` and its only remaining item is the
operator task T6.11, so under §9.1 step 1 agents start M7 tasks that do not need it. It is numbered after §6.11 so that
earlier anchors stay. Design: [ADR-0008](adr/0008-two-stage-routing.md) (two-stage routing: hard filter, then static
weighted score, every decision persisted), [ADR-0032](adr/0032-provider-model-affinity.md) (provider and model affinity,
owner decision 40), [ADR-0043](adr/0043-capacity-estimate-breaker-and-retry.md) (the capacity estimate, breaker and
retry M7 reads), [ADR-0007](adr/0007-capacity-as-confidence-estimate.md), [FRD §3](requirements/FRD.md#3-business-rules)
(BR-1 to BR-5, BR-39, BR-45), [LLD §1](design/LLD.md#1-data-model) (`route_decision`), [LLD §3](design/LLD.md#3-mcp-tool-contracts)
(`explain_route`, "to be detailed in M7"), [LLD §3.2](design/LLD.md#32-provider-and-model-affinity-m7) (affinity table,
algorithm, decision fields, events), [LLD §13.6](design/LLD.md#136-task-contracts) (`route_features` shape and the
per-feature `source`), [LLD §16](design/LLD.md#16-capacity-and-resilience-m6) (the estimate), [TDD §3.1](design/TDD.md#31-configuration)
(`routing.weights`), [TDD §3.13.3](design/TDD.md#3133-testing-affinity-and-retrieval) (affinity replay) and
[TDD §5](design/TDD.md#5-implementation-strategy-by-milestone) (M7 row).

**Facts the breakdown relies on (checked against `main` at `55e3d4b`, 2026-10-14).**

- Stage 1 is **built** (M2 T2.3, extended by T3.7, T6.4 and T6.6): `filterEligible` in `src/routing/eligibility.ts` is
  pure, and `collectFacts` in `src/routing/collect.ts` gathers the facts. Its reasons cover enablement, client ceiling,
  platform, binary and probe, manifest and version gate, auth, the adapter's compile verdict per profile,
  `PROFILE_PLATFORM_UNVERIFIED`, `CIRCUIT_OPEN` and `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED_KNOWN`. Not modelled: `minimum_capability` (the
  MCP server refuses it with `ELIGIBILITY_NONE`, "no model registry", in `checkRouting`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, although
  the T4.5 model registry now exists), required tools and sandbox strength as separate constraints, and concurrency,
  which queues rather than excludes (Q-5).
- **There is no stage 2.** `selectProvider` in `src/mcp/server.ts` picks `provider_preference` if it is eligible, else
  the first eligible provider in configured order (BR-4's M2 tie-break), and `routeRecord` stores the basis as
  `selection.basis`. `toRouteDecisionJson` writes `features_json` (request facts: candidates, required provider,
  profiles, requirements, platform), `candidates_json` (per provider: eligible and reasons), `weights_json` `NULL` and
  no score. `continue_task` (BR-18) and the lifecycle's quota failover (`submitFailover`, T6.6) write their own route
  decisions with `selection` and `continuation` objects.
- No heuristic classifier (FR-RTE-04), no `routing` configuration section (`src/config/schema.ts` has none, though TDD
  §3.1 names `routing.weights`), no `explain_route` tool, no `project_affinity` table and no `routing_affinity_*` event.
  The newest migration is `0013_http_subject.ts`, so the next is 0014. `delegate_task` takes no `model` argument
  (`src/mcp/schemas.ts`), so "an explicit model in the request" (FR-RTE-07, 08) cannot occur yet.
- M6 left hooks for M7: the capacity projection (`src/capacity/projection.ts`) gives per provider `throttle_risk`,
  `capacity_score` (`null` when unknown, Q-69) and `confidence`; T6.7's `switchCounts` (`src/capacity/cache.ts`) reads
  `reasons_json.affinity.affinity_switch_reason` and counts `unrecorded` until M7 writes one; the retry chain records
  `retry_not_scheduled` with `attempts_exhausted` (FR-CAP-05) and the quota ladder records `quota_failover`.
- No source gives a provider's price, a model ranking, a quality score or a sandbox-strength order (T6.0's
  [capacity-signals note](research/capacity-signals-2026-10-14.md) found no price or limit for the logins in use).
  The controller's own ledger holds run outcomes and durations per provider.
- The prompt each adapter sends is assembled inside the adapter (`promptText` in `src/adapters/claude/adapter.ts`, and
  the handoff renderer in `src/continuity/render.ts`), so FR-RTE-09 touches adapter code.

**Conventions (Proposed, Delegated).**

- **No invented provider facts.** A score term whose per-provider value has no source (monetary cost, security risk, any
  model ranking) takes the same neutral value for every candidate, so it can never decide a route, and each weight
  default is documented, configurable and an **Assumption**. Terms computed from the controller's own ledger (success
  rate and latency over the capacity window, the M6 estimate, handoff cost) are facts, not claims.
- **Unknown capacity is never headroom (Q-69).** A `null` `capacity_score` scores as the worst capacity risk, never as
  spare capacity, and never makes a provider ineligible on its own.
- **Fail closed, keep the provider.** Where the switch logic is unsure (a missing affinity row field, an estimate below
  the configured confidence, an unset low-water mark), affinity holds; where routing cannot decide (no candidate), the
  request is refused with the BR-3 code, as today. Replay never calls a model or a provider: it reads stored features.
- **Design gate.** T7.0 (documentation only) writes the contracts before code: the feature set, the score, the decision
  record, `explain_route`, the `routing` configuration section, the affinity data and the reset command. A new table,
  column or event type is specified in the LLD first and is covered by an ADR (AGENTS.md).
- Every test runs on fake adapters with an injected clock; no task calls a live provider. Routing code lives in
  `src/routing/`; edits to `src/mcp/server.ts` are limited to calling it and to the `explain_route` tool, and
  `src/adapters/**` is not edited while the M4 adapter work holds it.

| T7.11 | README (routing, `explain_route`, affinity and `affinity reset`, what scores never claim), corrections to any FRD, SDD, HLD or TDD text the work proved wrong, and status here | NFR-MAINT-02, 03 | T7.10 | The README matches the behaviour; the M7 row in §6 carries status and evidence; `node scripts/check-docs.mjs` passes **Status: Done (2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved).** Evidence: README "Routing and affinity" (two stages, the six score terms and their sources, what a score never claims, the closed list of affinity reasons, `explain_route`), the `routing` configuration row, the M7 status bullet and a known limitation (`minimum_capability` refused, model remembered not pinned, no cache benefit claimed); the "Cache and switch counts" text no longer says `unrecorded` until M7. Corrections: HLD COMP-RTE (scoring and classifier are built) and COMP-OPS (`affinity list`), SDD FR-RTE-08 and the two-stage row (model remembered, not pinned; Q-90), LLD §16.8 (when `unrecorded` still appears, checked against `switchCounts` in `src/capacity/cache.ts`) and LLD §17.6 (the model bullet points to the T7.5 "As built" note). FRD and TDD needed no change: BR-4 still states the requirement that T7.8 implements. The M7 row now reads `Partial`; `node scripts/check-docs.mjs` passes |
| T7.12 | FR-RTE-08 model pinning (added 2026-10-14 by T7.5, Delegated): when affinity holds and the request names no model, pass the project's remembered model to the adapter's `compile`, only for an adapter that declares that a model id its native session reports is accepted by its model flag; otherwise keep the adapter default and record why | FR-RTE-08, Q-90, LLD §2, §2.1, §17.6 | T7.5; an adapter-contract field in `src/adapters/**` (the M4 lane) | Tests: a held decision on an adapter that declares the field compiles with the remembered model; on one that does not (Codex today) the run compiles with no model and the decision records it; a model changes only for `user_request` or `ineligible` **Status: Done on fakes (2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved).** Evidence: `src/routing/model-pin.test.ts` (through the MCP server with scripted fake adapters: a held decision on a declaring adapter compiles with the remembered model, stored as `request_json.model`, with `affinity.model_pin` `pinned`; an undeclared adapter compiles with no model and records `adapter_not_declared`; a model the adapter refuses keeps the provider and its default with `model_refused`; after `user_request` or `ineligible` the new provider compiles with its adapter default; every decision replays). Code: the optional `reportedModelFlag` declaration and `acceptsReportedModel` in `src/adapters/types.ts` (absent means not accepted; Claude `accepted`, an **Assumption** that its init `model` is a full model ID, pending the live conformance subset; Cursor and Codex `not_accepted`), `planModelPin` in `src/routing/affinity.ts`, `withModelPin` in `src/routing/decision.ts`, `pinModel` in `src/mcp/server.ts` (re-checks stage 1 with the model before pinning; a `privileged` run is never pinned), and `model` through admission, preflight and compile in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`. LLD §17.6, SDD FR-RTE-08 and Q-90 still describe the T7.5 state; their update is left to the docs lane |

#### 6.12.1 Order, waves and hot files

- **Wave A (agents, now; nothing needs a provider CLI or an owner decision):** T7.0 first (documentation), then T7.1
  and T7.4 after it, T7.2 after T7.1, T7.3 after T7.2, T7.5 after T7.2 and T7.4, T7.6 after T7.5, T7.7 after T7.5.
- **Wave B:** T7.10 after T7.3 and T7.5 to T7.7; T7.11 last (both done). T7.8 is blocked on T7.12 (its row).
- **Waits for another lane:** T7.9 edits adapter prompt assembly, so it starts after the M4 adapter work releases
  `src/adapters/**` (§6.8.1); T7.12 (model pinning, added by T7.5) needs an adapter-contract field from the same lane.
- **Hot files:** `src/mcp/server.ts` (T7.2, T7.3, T7.5; only the routing call sites and the `explain_route` tool, never
  the HTTP transport); `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` (T7.2 and T7.5: the failover decision and the success hook);
  `src/config/schema.ts` and `src/config/load.ts` (T7.1, T7.4); `src/ledger/repo.ts` (T7.2, T7.4); `docs/design/LLD.md`
  (T7.0). Tasks sharing a hot file run one after another, never in parallel (AGENTS.md).

#### 6.12.2 Blocks Done: what needs the operator or zemdregon

M7 can reach `Done` on fakes; no task needs a provider login (Delegated reading: the acceptance criteria are all
replay, property and fault-injection tests). Open items that do not block it:

1. **zemdregon, optional:** Q-51 (stay on the new provider after a capacity switch) and Q-52 (no idle expiry) are
   Delegated defaults that M7 implements as written; zemdregon may revise them. The default weights and the
   `low_water_mark` and `min_confidence` defaults are T7.0's Delegated Assumptions. Nothing here changes what a bypass
   run may do: affinity never moves a `privileged` meta-session to another provider (the Q-61 rule stays in force).
2. **Sources for provider facts:** a price, model ranking or sandbox-strength order per provider. Without one the
   matching term stays neutral and `minimum_capability` needs an operator-declared capability (T7.8).
3. **Another lane:** T7.9 and T7.12 wait for `src/adapters/**` (T7.12: Q-90).

## 7. Risks, blockers and open questions

### 7.1 Risks

| ID | Risk | Effect on plan | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-1 | Provider CLIs change flags, output or auth behaviour (already happened with Kimi) | Adapters break or weaken security | Version gating, conformance suites, manifests (ADR-0009) |
| R-2 | Provider terms restrict automated or third-party use for some plans | A provider may be unusable | Compliance manifest review per provider; each user runs their own installation with their own logins (ADR-0003, ADR-0011) |
| R-11 | Publishing as open source invites users to run Switchboard in ways the terms do not allow (shared logins, hosted service) | Account risk for those users; reputational risk for the project | README states the single-operator model and that terms compliance is each user's responsibility (NFR-COMP-01, NFR-MAINT-03) |
| R-3 | Same-user credential exfiltration by a prompt-injected agent | Credential theft | FR-POL-06; provider-native sandboxes; untrusted profiles without shell; M3 fixtures |
| R-4 | Native sandbox support differs by platform | Fewer eligible providers on some hosts | Eligibility is per platform; Linux first |
| R-5 | Capacity is opaque | Throttling mid-task | Confidence estimates, backpressure, checkpointed retries (ADR-0007) |
| R-6 | Scope (seven adapters, all Must) is large for one maintainer | Slow delivery | Vertical slice with two adapters first; the rest added one at a time behind the same conformance suite; open-source contributors can add adapters (NFR-MAINT-01) |
| R-7 | Research facts are dated 2026-10-12 and not re-verified here | Flags in TDD may be stale by implementation | Each adapter task starts by verifying against the installed CLI |
| R-8 | No per-run disk or CPU limits | A runaway agent fills the disk or starves the host | Timeouts, concurrency limits and retention for now; revisit in M3 with the outer-sandbox evaluation ([TDD §3.4](design/TDD.md#34-security-engineering)) |
| R-9 | MCP client identity over stdio is only as strong as the orchestrator's launch config (`serve --client <id>`) | A misconfigured orchestrator gets another client's trust ceiling | Keep ceilings low by default; `privileged` always needs operator approval (FR-POL-09) |
| R-10 | Claude Code has no documented native sandbox in the research | Claude may be ineligible for `test-sandboxed` (FR-POL-06); M2's two profiles are unaffected because they allow no arbitrary shell | Settle in M3 with the malicious-fixture suite; other adapters cover those profiles if needed |
| R-12 | A controller crash between spawning a provider process and committing its `pid`, `pgid` and start ticks leaves an orphan that startup reconciliation cannot find (LLD §8) | An unsupervised provider process may keep writing to a worktree until it exits; the run is still assessed and ended | Known limit of M1 (the pid is only known after spawn). **Proposed:** revisit with a launcher or cgroup-based tracking if it matters in M3 |
| R-13 | Container environments need the provider CLI's login inside the container, and FR-AUTH-01 forbids the controller copying or moving credentials | `container` projects may be unusable, or need a per-container native login | Proposed in-volume native login (Q-6, delegated); T10.0 verifies it; host environments unaffected (ADR-0013) |
| R-14 | The message sanitiser is a pattern list; attribution in unusual wording, or written into file contents, is not caught | Commits or files may carry a marker contrary to owner decision 7 | Fixtures of typical output; operator-added patterns; file contents out of scope by design; review of published commits (ADR-0014) |
| R-15 | In a `host` environment a same-user agent shell can read ambient Git credentials | An agent could push around the tool | Withhold the `push` tool there (FR-GIT-06, BR-31); no credentials in the agent environment; containers in M10 |
| R-16 | Removing provider attribution from commits may conflict with a provider's terms; not reviewed | Account or policy risk for the operator | Record the review in the compliance manifest notes (FR-POL-07); no compliance claim (NFR-COMP-01); the operator's call |
| R-17 | Provenance links to commits by SHA; an operator rebase or squash breaks the link | Gaps in the audit trail | Also store tree hash and patch ID (FR-GIT-07); best effort |
| R-18 | A per-run injected MCP channel may be impossible without a bypass flag, or blocked by a provider sandbox, for some providers | Those adapters fall back to controller-made commits | T9.0 spike; finalize-only mode (FR-GIT-09). *T9.0 spike (2026-10-13; Delegated, partial).* Claude Code: `claude --help` 2.1.296 documents `--mcp-config` and `--strict-mcp-config`, so a per-run definition needs no global configuration and, if an MCP tool needs no bypass flag, none; whether it does, the JSON shape, the allow-rule form and whether `--safe-mode` also disables the server are **unverified** (live check). ADR-0015 and the argv audit bar `--mcp-config`, so the channel needs a narrowing ADR first (Q-40). Socket reach from any provider sandbox: `Unknown: live check`. Codex: **Unverified** (not installed; nothing in [R2]). Until checked, no adapter may declare an injected channel and the finalize-only mode stays the fallback. *Update 2026-10-13 (decision 36):* Q-40 is decided yes ([ADR-0029](adr/0029-claude-code-receives-tool-shim-via-mcp-config.md); T9.4b), so the Claude channel no longer needs a further ADR, only the live checks; zemdregon chose finalize-only as the fallback and wants it for his projects (T9.7). *Update 2026-10-13 (T9.4b, Delegated):* the Claude channel is implemented behind the unsupported opt-in `providers.claude-code.git_channel` (default off); the live checks (a) to (i) listed under the T9.4b status in §6.3 have not run, so this risk is unchanged. *Leads from T3 Code (MIT; studied, not copied; observed in its source at commit 6524a8d, unverified on our CLIs; see [TDD §3.10](design/TDD.md#310-git-commits-attribution-and-publication)):* Claude via the SDK control channel with allow rules `mcp__<server>__*` and additive MCP config; Codex via thread-start `config.mcp_servers` to `codex app-server` (its own doc is stale); Cursor SDK `mcpServers`; OpenCode per-turn `client.mcp.add` with session allow/deny rules; Grok Build and Antigravity via an ACP stdio MCP bridge with the endpoint and credential in its environment; Kimi Code unknown. R-50 covers the staleness risk. *Update 2026-10-13 (live check on the operator's machine, `docs/research/live-checks-2026-10-13.md`, PR #41; Claude Code 2.1.296 with a hand-made stdio server, not Switchboard's shim):* the `mcpServers` wrapper is required; `${VAR}` expands and an unset variable stays literal; the server inherits the claude environment; exact-name rules work under `dontAsk` (wildcards too; still refused); **`--safe-mode` stops the server from starting**. Owner decision 41 ([ADR-0033](adr/0033-claude-channel-bypass-only-without-safe-mode.md)): the Claude channel is offered only to approved bypass runs, which omit `--safe-mode`; every other Claude run falls back to finalize-only (T9.7). Socket reach from the real provider and the Git deny rules with the shim present remain unverified through Switchboard, so this risk stays open *Update 2026-10-13 (spikes T4.20 to T4.70, Delegated, documentation only):* Antigravity is not documented as an ACP agent in Google's documentation read on 2026-10-13 ([TDD §4.4](design/TDD.md#44-antigravity-agy-m4)), so the T3 Code lead for it is unconfirmed; Grok Build ([§4.3.1](design/TDD.md#431-grok-build-native-controls-per-profile-and-platform-t420)), Cursor ([§4.5](design/TDD.md#45-cursor-cli-m4)), OpenCode ([§4.6](design/TDD.md#46-opencode-m4)) and Kimi Code ([§4.7](design/TDD.md#47-kimi-code-m4-blocked-by-b-1)) document ACP entry points. No per-run MCP definition is documented for the `-p` surfaces of Grok Build, Antigravity or Cursor, so those stay finalize-only *Note 2026-10-13 (owner lead, Delegated research):* the owner reports a separate ACP download for Antigravity that is not part of the native `agy`. Research found the ACP registry listing `antigravity-acp` ("Google Antigravity", 1.3.0, author Google LLC, binary archives on `dl.google.com`, linked from Google's Zed guide) and several third-party `agy` wrappers; details, sources and confidence are in [TDD §4.4.4](design/TDD.md#444-separate-acp-adapter-owner-lead-2026-10-13). Its behaviour (what it wraps, login reuse, permission requests, per-session `mcpServers`) is Unverified until T4.31 (checklist C14 to C17), so the statements above that Antigravity is not documented as an ACP agent remain true of Google's CLI documentation only; the transport decision is unchanged. |
| R-19 | The verified research documents no provider setting for commit behaviour or attribution, nor how to supply a per-run MCP definition | Provider-specific controls cannot be relied on until checked | The layered design does not depend on them (TDD §3.10); each adapter verifies against the installed CLI (T2.2, T9.0). T2.2 result: `claude --help` 2.1.296 documents deny rules (`--disallowedTools`) but names attribution only under `--bare`, which is unusable (ADR-0015); Codex: nothing in [R2], CLI not installed. T9.0 result (2026-10-13): `claude mcp --help`, `claude mcp add --help` and `claude --help` 2.1.296 add `--mcp-config` and `--strict-mcp-config` (above) and nothing on commit behaviour; attribution is still named only under `--bare`. Codex: still nothing in [R2], CLI not installed, every cell Unverified |
| R-20 | An orchestrator that can create projects widens what a compromised or prompt-injected orchestrator can reach | New mounts, providers, ceilings or push rights through a project it created | `manage_projects` off by default; limits are the client's own; operator approval for containers, push and widening; creator-only visibility; every call recorded (FR-PRJ-10, HLD TB-9) |
| R-21 | Claude stream-json event shapes and error category strings are unverified; the adapter was written against `claude --help` 2.1.296 text and synthetic fixtures (`src/adapters/claude/parse.ts`; [ADR-0015](adr/0015-claude-untrusted-isolation-flags.md)) | Misparsed events or misclassified failures on a real CLI | Live smoke test (T2.6) before M2 is called done; classify unknown shapes as `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`, never success *Live check 2026-10-13 (checks 4a, 4b).* Held on Claude Code 2.1.296: C-1 to C-11 and G-1 (14 HELD); C-12 and C-G2 still need a failing run. The shipped smoke script polled `get_run` with an unsupported argument; fixed in the same branch. |
| R-22 | Codex event shapes and the `--sandbox read-only` spelling are unverified (`src/adapters/codex/protocol.ts`); no Codex binary was available | Misparsed events, or a sandbox flag that does not mean what the adapter assumes | Live smoke test (T2.6); the probe requires the flags in `codex exec --help` *Live check 2026-10-13 (check 4c).* X-1 to X-3 held on codex-cli 0.162.1. | R-24 | *For zemdregon, not blocking.* Owner decision 11 lets verification commands run on the host under `edit-untrusted`. Those commands execute code the untrusted agent just wrote (tests, build scripts) as the operator's user, outside any provider sandbox | Code from an untrusted run executes on the host with the operator's file access, including provider credential files and network | Argv only, no shell; environment allowlist without `GIT_*`, signing or agent variables; worktree cwd; the run's wall-clock bound with process-group kill; redacted, bounded output; refused under `inspect-untrusted` (T3.10, implemented 2026-10-13, [LLD §8.1](design/LLD.md#81-verification-stage-m3-t310)); confined verification optional (T3.11); orchestrators should supply verification only for repositories they trust. Still open: the commands are not confined (R-61; Q-57 decided by owner decision 45) |
| R-25 | *For zemdregon, not blocking.* Owner decision 12 lets `profiles.<name>` loosen defaults. A malicious or mistaken config change can widen every run of a profile (for example shell or network for `edit-untrusted`) | Weaker isolation than the profile name suggests, applied silently to later runs | Loosening must be explicit and validated, appears in every run's policy snapshot and in `doctor` (T3.2); the config file is operator-owned |
| R-26 | *For zemdregon, not blocking.* Owner decision 13 allows bypass flags under an approved `privileged` scope. A bypass flag disables the provider's native approvals and some of its controls for that run, and a scope approved without careful reading grants that to an orchestrator's request | A privileged run can do whatever the provider allows without prompts, including Git writes the deny rules would otherwise block, if the provider's bypass overrides them | Bypass only when the scope sets it explicitly and the approval's scope hash matches (T3.4, T3.17); `runs show` displays the scope before `approve`; no expiry and no automatic approval; [ADR-0017](adr/0017-approved-privileged-scope-bypass.md) and [LLD §3.1](design/LLD.md#31-privileged-scope-m3) record the limits (T3.16, done) |
| R-27 | *Proposed (owner decision 14).* A cheap model writes a vague, wrong or misleading commit message or PR draft, and it enters published history | Poor history; reviewers misled | Agent text wins by default; sanitiser and non-empty check (FR-INN-10); `inner status` shows rejection rates and suspension (FR-INN-09); `never` mode; the provenance record notes the drafting source |
| R-28 | *Proposed.* Repository content or an orchestrator's request contains prompt injection aimed at the inner model | A bad draft, a misleading title or summary, or a skewed routing feature | The inner agent has no authority (FR-INN-06), no tools (FR-INN-02), validated output (FR-INN-07), and routing features cannot touch eligibility (FR-INN-12) |
| R-29 | *Proposed.* Source code or logs are sent to a `remote` inner backend that the operator or the provider may retain or train on; deterministic redaction misses a secret | Data leaves the host from the controller itself | `local`/`remote` classification, `allow_remote`, per-task `allow_source_content`, per-project `local_only`, redaction before every call, no body logging (FR-INN-16); the examples default to local |
| R-30 | *Proposed.* A condensed handoff or log summary drops or invents a fact, and the next agent or the orchestrator trusts it | Wasted work or a wrong conclusion | Additive and labelled, raw artifact pointers, statuses from records only, the next agent still inspects the repository (FR-INN-13) |
| R-31 | *Proposed.* The inner backend draws on the same subscription as a routed provider (for example a Claude Code or OpenCode login also used for coding) | Capacity estimates are blind to real consumption; the saving is lost | `shares_subscription_with` records an `inner_agent` observation and `doctor` warns (FR-INN-15) |
| R-32 | *Proposed.* Model or server churn changes output quality or format under a fixed prompt template; the `cli` kind's flags change (as R-1) | Rising fallback rates, silent quality drift | Versioned templates, per-task outcome records and suspension (FR-INN-05, 09), `inner test`; the `cli` kind is version-gated (FR-INN-04) |

| R-34 | *For zemdregon, not blocking.* Owner decision 17 makes the first `privileged` approval in a meta-session durable. Per-run oversight is gone: a later `continue_task` continuation runs with the same bypass and broad privileges without the operator taking a fresh look, and the instruction it carries was never reviewed | A prompt-injected or mistaken orchestrator uses a long-lived approved meta-session for work the operator would not have approved, with provider approvals bypassed if the scope sets `bypass` | The approval is bound to one client, one meta-session and one canonical scope, so a continuation cannot widen it and any other scope or session needs a new approval ([LLD §3.1](design/LLD.md#31-privileged-scope-m3)); every covered run records the approval ID in its policy snapshot and ledger events, and `runs show` lists the runs an approval covers; the per-client ceiling is re-checked on every request (FRD C4), so lowering it stops further continuations; the operator can cancel any run; an explicit revoke command is open (Q-30) |
