Controller `status`, `diff` and `add -A` then started a child `git status` that uses that repository's own configuration, including filter drivers, which the controller's `-c` overrides do not reach (reproduced on Git 2.43.0 with a benign marker-file filter: `status`, `diff HEAD` and `add -A` each ran it; an untracked nested repository that is not yet a gitlink is not entered by `status`). Gap in the driver hardening of [TDD §3.4](design/TDD.md#34-security-engineering) | Code from an untrusted run could execute inside a controller Git call as the operator's user, outside any provider sandbox | **Mitigated** by [ADR-0022](adr/0022-nested-repositories-excluded-from-controller-git.md): nested repositories are excluded from every worktree-scanning controller Git call; evidence `src/workspace/nested.test.ts` (marker absent after `assessMutation`, `finalize` and `rollback`; positive controls show the driver runs under direct Git). Residual: files inside a nested repository are not captured in checkpoints, diffs or commits; a repository created after the scan and before the Git call is not covered (the provider process has ended by then, but this is not enforced); the scan does not cover Git calls outside `src/workspace/manager.ts` (none scan the worktree today). Not verified live: T3.8b adversarial scenarios (owner-reviewed) remain out of scope |

For Claude, network `off` holds only at the tool level (no `WebFetch`); any compiled `Bash` command, such as an operator's `edit-untrusted` allowlist entry, can use the network. | R-37 | [R2] states neither the Linux host prerequisites of Codex's bubblewrap sandbox nor what Codex does when the sandbox cannot start, nor how `codex exec` handles an action that would need approval (T3.0, [TDD §4.2.1](design/TDD.md#421-codex-native-controls-per-profile-and-platform-t30)) | On some hosts Codex may fail every run, or run without the confinement decision 10 relies on; an action needing approval may hang or be handled in a way the adapter does not classify | Live check T3.9 (sandbox start, network off, approval handling); the probe and `doctor` report the prerequisites once known; run timeouts bound a hang (FR-POL-05) |
| R-38 | *For zemdregon, not blocking (owner decision 19).* An approved `bypass: true` Claude Code run omits `--restricted` ([ADR-0018](adr/0018-claude-bypass-omits-restricted.md)), so it loses the protections `--restricted` gives the settings files and Git files, and runs with the provider's permission bypass. Whether the `--disallowedTools` Git deny rules (FR-GIT-01) still hold under bypass is **unverified**: the live check C-G2 in `scripts/live-smoke.mjs` reports `NOT_CHECKED` today and does not run a bypass run | If the deny rules do not hold under bypass, FR-GIT-01 is not enforced in bypass runs, and only FR-GIT-03 (ref snapshot and violation detection, M9) and FR-GIT-06 (no Git credentials in the agent environment) remain; the agent could commit, or alter Git configuration, inside its worktree | Bypass only for an approved scope that sets `bypass: true` (decisions 13, 22), worktree isolation (FR-WS-05), the argv audit (T3.17); extend C-G2 to a deliberate Git write in an approved bypass run (T3.9); if it fails, make Claude bypass runs `Ineligible` again or rely on FR-GIT-03 once M9 lands. *Scope (owner decision 24, 2026-10-13):* the risk does not apply in a `container` environment (M10, planned, not built), whose image has no Git client and which mounts neither the Git directory nor any credential store (FR-PRJ-05), so FR-GIT-01 does not rest on the deny rules there (R-40 covers what remains). For host-run bypass it stays open until C-G2 records a result *Live check 2026-10-13 (checks 14, 16).* **Held:** under `--dangerously-skip-permissions` the `--disallowedTools` Git deny rules denied all six tested spellings and `--tools` still removed unlisted tools, directly and through `serve` with an approved bypass scope. A control without the rules committed. *Note 2026-10-13 (owner decision 46, answering Q-58):* the same exposure, a bypass run with no agent-side Git-write denial and only FR-GIT-03 and FR-GIT-06 remaining, is accepted by zemdregon for his own bypass runs on any provider whose Git-write control is `none-documented` or not shown to hold under bypass; the R-38 mitigation "make bypass runs `Ineligible` again" is not adopted for those providers. |
| R-39 | *For zemdregon, not blocking (owner decision 22, Delegated realisation).* A client configured `privileged_approval: "not_required"` gets `privileged` runs, including `bypass: true` runs, with no operator review of the scope ([ADR-0019](adr/0019-per-client-privileged-approval.md)). A prompt-injected or mistaken orchestrator on that client can start any scope it can express, at once, and continue it without limit (decision 17) | Broad, unattended privileged work on the operator's machine for anything that can reach that client's stdio session (R-9) | Off by default (`required`); refused by config validation unless the client's ceiling is `privileged`; `doctor` warns for every such client; the scope is still validated, hashed and recorded, the row names `client_policy:<client_id>`, and `runs show` lists it; Git-write denial (FR-GIT-01, subject to R-38) and worktree isolation (FR-WS-05) stay enforced; the operator can switch the client back to `required` or lower its ceiling and cancel runs |
| R-40 | *For zemdregon, not blocking (owner decision 24, Delegated mechanism).* Two limits of "no Git client in the container image". (1) A provider CLI may need a Git client for its own work (repository detection, status or diff context); whether each one runs without it is unverified. (2) Absence of a Git client is a property of the image, not a boundary: an agent with a shell and network (a bypass run) can fetch a Git client or write Git files directly | (1) Some providers may be usable only in `host` environments. (2) The agent can create a nested repository in its worktree (which the controller's host-side Git calls would then meet, R-35), but cannot reach the operator's refs or credentials, because the Git directory and credential stores are not mounted (FR-PRJ-05) | (1) T10.0 checks each provider and marks one that needs Git `host`-only. (2) The no-Git-client check is for the image as supplied (T10.1); FR-GIT-01 in containers rests on the mounts, with FR-GIT-03 detection kept; the nested-repository default of Q-31 applies; container leg of the malicious-fixture suite (T10.3) |
| R-41 | *For zemdregon, not blocking (owner decision 26, Delegated realisation).* An approved bypass run with `repository_config` `approved` or `enabled` loads repository content: instruction files, skills, plugins and hooks (no `--safe-mode`), and for `enabled` the repository's MCP definitions (no `--strict-mcp-config`), on top of the provider's permission bypass and the absent `--restricted` (R-38). Whether omitting those flags loads exactly those items is **unverified**: `claude --help` 2.1.296 does not say, and the live check is not yet written (T3.9) | Hostile or mistaken repository content (a hook that runs a command, an MCP server, instructions that steer the agent) executes or steers with the operator's permissions and without prompts; zemdregon accepts this for his own workloads | Only for an approved scope that sets `bypass: true` and `repository_config` `approved` or `enabled` (decisions 13, 22, 26); `approved` keeps `--strict-mcp-config`; Git-write denial (FR-GIT-01, subject to R-38), worktree isolation (FR-WS-05) and the argv audit (T3.17) unchanged; extend the T3.9 live check to a bypass run in a fixture repository with a hook, an instruction file and an `.mcp.json`; if the flags do not behave as assumed, make these cells `Ineligible` again. *Update 2026-10-13 (live check 15, `docs/research/live-checks-2026-10-13.md`, PR #41; observed under `--dangerously-skip-permissions`):* with neither flag, `CLAUDE.md`, a hook and a repository `.mcp.json` server all loaded and the hook ran with no prompt; with `--strict-mcp-config` only, the first two loaded and the server did not; with `--safe-mode`, none loaded. So the flags behave as assumed. Owner decision 41 extends the `--safe-mode` omission to an approved bypass run carrying the Claude tool channel (R-59, ADR-0033) |
| R-42 | *Delegated, not blocking; mitigated 2026-10-13 (decision 28).* Retention GC's `serve` interval pass was not excluded from `continue_task`: a meta-session idle past `retention.days` is chosen by the ledger query, and a `continue_task` admitted while that unit was being collected could find its worktree, checkpoint branches or artifacts gone. The startup pass cannot race it (it runs before MCP accepts calls) | Rare (needs a continuation of a session idle for the whole retention period, within one unit's collection window); before the fix the continued run failed or started from a missing checkpoint | **Mitigated:** GC marks first. `ledger.beginGc` re-checks idleness and sets `ARCHIVED` in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction before any removal, so a session with a newly admitted run is skipped; admission refuses an `ARCHIVED` meta-session (`INVALID_REQUEST`, in validation and again inside the admission transaction); a marked but unfinished session rolls forward on the next pass. Evidence: `src/workspace/gc.test.ts`, `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.test.ts` (see T3.12). Residual: a refused client sees `INVALID_REQUEST` and must start a new task (a collected session cannot be continued by design); no process-level test of two real processes racing (SQLite serialises the two transactions) |
| R-43 | *Delegated, not blocking; mitigated 2026-10-13 (T9.0 follow-up), matcher normalisation still unverified.* The Claude Git deny rules (`Bash(git <verb>)`, `Bash(git <verb> *)`) are lexical and list the subcommand directly after `git`. Git accepts global options before the subcommand: on Git 2.43.0 `git --no-pager commit`, `git -P commit` and `git --no-optional-locks commit` each created a commit in a test repository, and `--config-env`, `--exec-path`, `--namespace` and `--bare` are not denied either. `GIT_WRITE_SUBCOMMANDS` also lacks ref-affecting `reflog`, `pack-refs`, `gc`, `prune` and `maintenance` (`git pack-refs --all` and `git reflog expire` ran). Whether Claude's matcher normalises global options is unverified | A Claude run with a shell could still commit while the rules look complete; FR-GIT-01 on a host would rest on FR-GIT-03 detection and the layer 3 guard | **Mitigated (2026-10-13):** `src/adapters/git-write.ts` adds `GIT_GLOBAL_OPTIONS_WITH_VALUE` and `GIT_GLOBAL_FLAGS` (every global option in the Git 2.43.0 usage text: `-C`, `-c`, `--git-dir`, `--work-tree`, `--namespace`, `--config-env`, `--exec-path`, `--attr-source`, `-p`, `-P`, `--paginate`, `--no-pager`, `--no-optional-locks`, `--literal-pathspecs`, `--glob-pathspecs`, `--noglob-pathspecs`, `--icase-pathspecs`, `--no-replace-objects`, `--bare`) and the write subcommands `reflog`, `pack-refs`, `gc`, `prune`, `maintenance`, `repack`, `hash-object`; the Claude adapter emits `Bash(git <opt> *)` (and `Bash(git <opt>=*)` for long value options) for each, keeping the existing rule syntax; this only denies more and applies in bypass runs too (decision 22). Evidence: tests in `src/adapters/git-write.test.ts` and `src/adapters/claude/claude.test.ts`; behaviour probed on Git 2.43.0 in a throwaway repository (each listed option except `--bare` let `git branch` create a ref; each listed subcommand changed refs, packed-refs, reflogs or objects). `add`, `rm`, `mv`, `restore` and `clean` change the index or work tree only and are not FR-GIT-01 writes. **Still unverified:** whether Claude's matcher normalises global options (the help text does not say), so the explicit spellings are denied as belt and braces; a spelling not in the Git 2.43.0 usage text (a newer Git option) is not denied by the Claude rules; run the live check C-G2 (T3.9). The layer 3 guard (TDD §3.10) blocks the `-c`-less cases via the environment *Live check 2026-10-13 (checks 9, 10).* Claude's matcher does **not** normalise global options: with only `Bash(git commit *)` the `--no-pager`, `-P` and `--no-optional-locks` spellings committed. The explicit 99-rule list denied all tested spellings. |
| R-44 | *Delegated, not blocking; mitigated 2026-10-13 (decision 30, ADR-0023).* `MIN_GIT_VERSION` was 2.30 but `scrubbedGitEnv()` sets `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, which the Git 2.32.0 release notes introduce; on 2.30 or 2.31 the controller's Git calls might read the operator's global configuration (inferred from the notes; no such Git is installed to test) | A controller call could pick up an operator alias, identity, hook path or filter on an old Git that `doctor` accepts | **Mitigated:** floor raised to 2.32 (Q-39 decided; [ADR-0023](adr/0023-minimum-git-version-2-32.md); `src/workspace/git.ts`, `doctor` and the version-gate test). The 2.32 introduction version still rests on the release notes |
| R-45 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 32, ADR-0025).* The operator sanitiser patterns run on a backtracking engine (Node has no RE2) and are only protected by a validated subset, a cost estimate, 1024-character windows and a one-second budget; the cost model is an estimate from one measurement (`.*a.*b` took 36 s on 4096 characters), not a proof. A pattern in the subset can still be slow within the budget, and a pattern that is too slow fails closed (commits are refused) rather than slowing the controller further | An operator pattern could make every commit slow or refused; an accepted pattern cannot hang the controller beyond the budget plus one regular-expression call of at most about 67 million steps | **Open, accepted.** Revisit with an RE2 binding if the dependency is accepted; the built-in rules are linear or bounded (tests in `src/git/sanitize.test.ts`) |
| R-46 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 33, ADR-0026).* The tool channel cannot identify its peer (Node.js has no `SO_PEERCRED`), and the token reaches the provider process through its environment. Any process of the same OS user that can read that environment (or the shim's, which deletes it from its own copy only at start) can call `commit` for that run while it is RUNNING | A same-user process could make commits on the run's publish ref with the run's identity; it cannot push (no `push` tool yet), cannot reach another run (tokens are per run and bound to run, meta-session and worktree), cannot use the token after the run leaves RUNNING, and every commit still passes the path checks, sanitiser and provenance record | **Open, accepted.** Same reach the agent itself has. Controls: `0700` directory, `0600` socket, 256-bit per-run token compared in constant time, run-state re-check per call, rate limit. Revisit if Node.js gains a peer-credential API or when `push` is added (T9.6, BR-31). *T9.6 (2026-10-13):* the token now also reaches `push` where BR-31 offers it, so a same-user process could push the run's verified publish ref to the configured remote while the run is RUNNING; it still cannot choose the remote, ref or content, force, or push after a violation, and runs with a shell are never offered `push` |
| R-47 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 34, ADR-0027).* The ref check cannot tell who changed a ref. The operator's own work in the repository during a run (a commit or fetch, a new tag, switching the main checkout's branch), refs beyond the 5000-ref listing cap, and a short window in which another run's checkpoint commit is in Git before its ledger row, all produce `git_policy_violation` events although the agent did nothing. Conversely a push the agent made with an explicit URL or to an unconfigured remote leaves no local ref and is not detected (BR-27) | False positives in `runs show` and `get_run`; no run fails and no operator ref is changed, so the cost is noise, not lost work. A missed push is not undone | **Open, accepted.** Prevention stays the control (FR-GIT-01 deny rules, no push credentials, BR-29/BR-31; containers in M10). Revisit with T9.6 (Q-44) or if false positives prove frequent (an attribution guard, TDD §3.10 layer 3) |
| R-48 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 35, ADR-0028).* BR-31 and FR-POL-06 speak of commands; a profile "without arbitrary shell" can still give the agent the provider's own file-read tool. Whether that tool can read `~/.ssh`, a credential helper's store or `~/.git-credentials` depends on the provider's path scoping, which is unverified. The controller offers `push` to such runs | An agent of a push-enabled context could read a push credential through a file-read tool and use it outside Switchboard | **Open.** Controls: `push` is off by default per context, only `shell: none` or an empty allowlist qualifies, except an approved bypass run (decision 37, R-51); the credential itself is never in the agent's environment (FR-GIT-06). Revisit with the live checks (T3.8b) or containers (M10, which mount no credential store) |
| R-49 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 35, ADR-0028).* `switchboard git push` refuses while `serve` holds the data-directory lock, and a repository whose own configuration sets a credential helper, `core.sshCommand` or `insteadOf` is refused (`REPO_CONFIG_UNSAFE`) | The operator must stop `serve` to push, and must move such settings to the global configuration | **Open, accepted.** Fail closed. Revisit if stopping `serve` proves impractical (a daemon-applied operator push request) or if repository-level credential settings are common |
| R-50 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 36).* Reliance on third-party observed behaviour that may be stale or wrong. The per-provider tool-injection leads in [TDD §3.10](design/TDD.md#310-git-commits-attribution-and-publication) come from reading another project's source at one commit (6524a8d) and from issue and documentation text; that project's own documentation was already stale for Codex (it describes `-c mcp_servers.*` with a bearer environment variable, while its code passes `config.mcp_servers` with `http_headers` at thread start). It drives SDKs and `codex app-server`, not the raw CLI argv Switchboard builds | An adapter built on a lead could fail, or be less safe than assumed (for example an allow rule that does not cover the tool, or a config merge that loads operator MCP servers) | **Open, accepted.** Every lead is a hypothesis for the T9.0 open items and the adapter tasks (T9.4b and later); the authority is the installed CLI's help, the provider's documentation and the opt-in live checks; nothing is declared `injected` on a lead alone; Switchboard copies no code, comments or documentation from that project *Update 2026-10-13 (spikes, Delegated):* Antigravity is not documented as an ACP agent in Google's documentation read on 2026-10-13 ([TDD §4.4](design/TDD.md#44-antigravity-agy-m4)), so the T3 Code lead that it is one is unconfirmed; Grok Build (§4.3.1), Cursor (§4.5), OpenCode (§4.6) and Kimi Code (§4.7) document ACP entry points *Note 2026-10-13 (owner lead, Delegated research):* the owner reports a separate ACP download for Antigravity that is not part of the native `agy`. Research found the ACP registry listing `antigravity-acp` ("Google Antigravity", 1.3.0, author Google LLC, binary archives on `dl.google.com`, linked from Google's Zed guide) and several third-party `agy` wrappers; details, sources and confidence are in [TDD §4.4.4](design/TDD.md#444-separate-acp-adapter-owner-lead-2026-10-13). Its behaviour (what it wraps, login reuse, permission requests, per-session `mcpServers`) is Unverified until T4.31 (checklist C14 to C17), so the statements above that Antigravity is not documented as an ACP agent remain true of Google's CLI documentation only; the transport decision is unchanged. |
| R-51 | *Delegated reading of an owner decision, accepted by the owner for bypass (decision 37, ADR-0028 "Amended before merge").* An approved bypass agent with a shell can read the operator's push credentials (`~/.ssh`, a credential helper's store, `~/.git-credentials`) while the controller `push` tool is offered to it, because BR-31 no longer withholds `push` from approved bypass runs. This is the one place a bypass run's agent can reach push credentials by design of the owner's choice; the controller still never passes credentials to it and the controller's own push is unchanged | A bypass agent, or an injected prompt in one, could push with the operator's credentials around the tool (any ref, force, other remotes), bypassing the publish-ref-only rule | **Accepted by the owner for bypass runs; not blocking.** Controls that stay: bypass needs a per-meta-session approval of the exact scope (decision 17), policy `branch` is off by default per context, the provider's Git-write denial and FR-GIT-03 ref snapshots, worktree isolation, and the controller push itself (verified refspec, never forced). Non-bypass runs keep BR-31. Revisit with containers (M10, no credential store mounted) |
| R-52 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 38, T9.7).* Finalize-only mode publishes the agent's final summary as the commit message and the whole run as one commit. The sanitiser removes only what its rules describe (R-14), so a summary can carry text the operator would not have written (including text an injected prompt asked for); and the fail-closed limits (no commit for more than 256 changed paths, a summary over 8192 characters, any refused path, no usable summary, any violation in the meta-session) mean an opted-in run can end with no commit, only the diff and checkpoint | An unwanted message on the publish ref (visible once pushed), or an expected commit missing; neither changes the run's outcome, and each skip is recorded with its reason in `git_finalize_commit` and `runs show` | **Open, accepted.** Controls: opt-in per context (default off), the same FR-GIT-04 sanitiser and provenance as the `commit` tool, nothing pushed without the context's push policy and a separate push, the publish ref is separate from the operator's branches (FR-WS-05). A crash after the ref update leaves the commit published and recovery writes its row even though reconciliation then finishes the run; a crash before the intent leaves no commit (reconciliation does not retry it). Q-45 is decided (decision 44): the 256-path and 8192-character limits no longer skip an approved bypass run, which commits all paths and a truncated, sanitised, noted message; the limits still skip every other run |
| R-53 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 39, ADR-0030).* A dynamic project lets an LLM orchestrator choose the repository agents run in. A prompt-injected or compromised orchestrator can point agents at any Git repository under the allowed roots (which a `workspace_path` request can already do) and, through a project, keep doing so across runs; a repository it points at can carry instructions that become a prompt-injection source for the agent | Agents edit (in their worktree) or read repositories the operator did not intend for that orchestrator; with an approved identity or push, commits could be published under that project | **Open, accepted.** Controls: `manage_projects` off by default; allowed roots re-checked at every admission (no `..` or symlink escape, nothing inside `data_dir`); limits never above the client's; `container`, push, identity, signing and any widening wait for `projects approve` on the CLI with `--revision` once there are several; creator-only visibility; every call recorded in `project_event`. Keep `allowed_roots` narrow for clients with `manage_projects` |
| R-54 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 39).* The widening gate compares a new revision with the revision in force of the same project. A client can create a new project with any ceiling up to its own instead of widening an old one, and FR-PRJ-10 lets a fresh `host` project without push or identity run at once | The approval gate on widening does not stop a client from reaching its own limits through a new project id | **Open, accepted.** The client's own limits are the bound (FR-PRJ-10); the gate only protects what the operator approved from being widened silently. Lower `max_trust_profile` for clients with `manage_projects` if that matters |
| R-55 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 40, ADR-0031).* Indexed repository content, and under the global index content from other projects, is untrusted text placed in later prompts, so it is a prompt-injection path into runs | A planted instruction in a file or another project's contribution can mislead an agent in a later run, within that run's profile | **Open, accepted.** Every snippet is labelled untrusted reference data with path and origin; retrieved content has no authority over any policy decision (FR-RAG-07, import-boundary test); the global index is off by default and opt-in on both sides; `untrusted` contexts do not contribute by default (FR-RAG-09) |
| R-56 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 40, ADR-0031).* Secrets or sensitive content may be indexed because the denylist and redaction are best effort, and chunk text and vectors sit unencrypted in the data directory; a remote embedding backend could receive them | A secret in a source file could be stored, retrieved into a prompt or sent to an embedding service | **Open, accepted.** Denylist and FR-INN-16 redaction before embedding (FR-RAG-02); `remote` needs both `allow_remote` and `allow_source_content` (FR-RAG-04); the store is outside the repository, per project, clearable (FR-RAG-14); `doctor` reports backend and locality |
| R-57 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 40, ADR-0031).* Retrieval may be stale or wrong, and the token saving is unmeasured; the in-process store and cap are unverified until T12.0 | Misleading context, or no real saving for the added complexity | **Open.** Freshness checks and index revisions (FR-RAG-08); fallback to no retrieval; snippet ids and hashes in the ledger; measure before claiming a saving. *2026-10-14 (T12.0):* the in-process store and cap are measured ([TDD §3.13.4](design/TDD.md#3134-t120-spike-result-2026-10-14)): 25,000 chunks per store, scanned from memory in about 25 to 108 ms at p95 on the Ampere VM depending on dimension; the token saving is still unmeasured |
| R-58 | *Delegated, not blocking (decision 40, ADR-0032).* Affinity concentrates a project on one provider so its quota is spent first; the cache benefit is provider-specific and unverified; "maxed out" is a confidence estimate (ADR-0007), so the switch can fire late or early | Earlier exhaustion of one provider while others sit idle; a failed run before the switch; a cache gain that does not appear | **Open, accepted.** Closed reason list including `failed_after_retry` and `ineligible` as floors (FR-RTE-07); `affinity_switch_reason` and kept-versus-switched counts are visible (FR-RTE-03, FR-CAP-08); thresholds are configurable and chosen in M6/M7; `explain_route` shows why; revisit with real data (Q-51 to Q-53) |
| R-59 | *Delegated reading of an owner decision, not blocking (decision 41, ADR-0033).* An approved bypass run that carries the Claude tool channel runs without `--safe-mode`, so the repository's `CLAUDE.md`, skills, plugins and hooks load (live check 15 in `docs/research/live-checks-2026-10-13.md`, PR #41: a repository hook ran a command with no prompt) even when the scope says `repository_config: disabled`; and the injected server inherits the claude process environment | Hostile repository content steers or executes in that bypass run, on top of the bypass itself (R-26, R-41) | **Open, accepted for bypass.** Only with the operator opt-in and only for an approved scope that sets `bypass`; every other run keeps `--safe-mode` and has no channel (argv audit refuses `--mcp-config` and a missing `--safe-mode` outside that case); `--strict-mcp-config` kept except under decision 26 `enabled`; Git-write denial and worktree isolation unchanged (R-38 HELD in the live check); the child environment is only `PATH`, `HOME` and the token; the opt-in stays UNSUPPORTED until a live run through Switchboard |
| R-60 | *Delegated, not blocking (T9.10, ADR-0034).* Signing gives the controller's `git` child the operator's signing setup; in an approved bypass run (exempt from BR-31, decision 37, read as covering signing: Q-56) an agent whose shell can reach the operator's keyring or agent could sign content of its own with the operator's key, and an unlocked `gpg-agent` or `ssh-agent` would not prompt | Commits or other data signed with the operator's key outside the controller | **Open.** Signing off by default; BR-31 refuses every non-bypass run whose agent could reach the key; the provider process never receives the key or agent variables; containers in M10 keep the key out of reach; the operator can use a dedicated signing key or leave `sign` off for bypass projects |
| R-61 | *Delegated, not blocking (T3.10, owner decision 11).* Verification commands run on the host as the operator's user with the run's allowlist (`PATH`, and `HOME` for Claude Code and Codex), so code the agent wrote can read the operator's Git configuration, SSH keys, credential helpers and provider credential files, write Git refs and push. Git-write denial cannot be enforced on arbitrary host code | A verification `git commit`, `tag` or ref move is detected by the T9.5 check after VERIFYING (controller refs restored, others reported); a push to a remote, or exfiltration over the network, is not seen. A context with push policy `branch` withholds `push` from the agent (BR-31) but not from verification commands (Q-57) | `GIT_*`, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, `GPG_AGENT_INFO` and `GNUPGHOME` are dropped from the environment and adapter-declared variables (the channel token) are never passed; the tool channel is closed before VERIFYING; detection and restore as above; confined verification (T3.11); README warns to supply verification only for trusted repositories. **Assumption:** dropping these variables lowers but does not remove credential reach, because `HOME` still locates key files. **Dated note, 2026-10-13 (owner decision 45):** the residual risk is accepted by zemdregon for approved bypass runs; it stays open for other deployments until the opt-in rule (after T3.11) exists. |
| R-62 | *Proposed (Delegated, M4 T4.7), not blocking.* If ACP lets the agent ask the client for permission during a run (Unverified: the ACP specification is not in [R2]), then under an ACP transport the controller, not a provider flag, answers those requests. A bug or an over-broad answer grants what the compiled profile does not allow, and the argv audit (FR-POL-03), which inspects argv only, does not see it | A run could write, execute or reach the network beyond its profile with no bypass-class flag on its argv | Deny by default; answers derived only from the frozen policy snapshot; an answer that allows everything is bypass-class and refused outside an approved bypass scope; the T4.7 ADR; fake-agent tests (T4.8); the live legs of the providers that use ACP (§6.8) *Update 2026-10-13 (T4.7, Delegated):* the ACP specification v1 (agentclientprotocol.com, retrieved 2026-10-13) confirms the premise: `session/request_permission` is a client method the agent may call, the client answers with one of the agent's options (`allow_once`, `allow_always`, `reject_once`, `reject_always`) or `cancelled`, and may answer automatically. [ADR-0036](adr/0036-per-run-acp-stdio-client.md) mitigates by: a pure `decidePermission` over the frozen snapshot only; deny by default; only `allow_once` or `reject_once`, never an `always` option; locations checked against the worktree; an answer within a bound, never left pending; no `fs` or `terminal` capability; declared bypass-class modes audited; native controls stay required, so answers only narrow. Remains: option kinds are "a hint" and tool kinds and locations are reported by the agent itself, so a mislabelled request can be allowed within the profile's kinds; whether each provider honours a rejection, inherits its environment into MCP servers, or blocks after `-32601` is Unverified until its live leg; the approved-bypass answer is open for zemdregon (ADR-0036 open item 1) *Update 2026-10-13 (T4.8, Delegated):* the client and `decidePermission` are built and tested against a scripted fake agent only (`src/exec/acp/client.test.ts`, `src/policy/acp-permission.test.ts`): out-of-profile requests get `reject_once` (or `cancelled` with `session/cancel`), `allow_always` is never selected, a decision over 1 s is a rejection, and the allow-once-for-everything answer of owner decision 48 needs an approved bypass run. The mislabelling and per-provider items above remain |
| R-63 | *Proposed (Delegated, M5 T5.1), not blocking.* Provider-reported text carried in a handoff bundle (a previous agent's summary or failure details) can be steered by a hostile repository, and the bundle moves it from provider A's session into provider B's prompt | A prompt injection crosses providers and sessions; under an approved bypass scope the receiving agent has broad powers | Bundle fields come from controller records (FR-SES-04, ADR-0001); provider-reported text is bounded, redacted and labelled as untrusted data; the receiving run keeps its own frozen profile (FR-POL-02); a cross-provider `privileged` continuation is refused until Q-61 is answered; the T5.1 ADR decides what text is carried. *Update 2026-10-14 (T5.1, [ADR-0038](adr/0038-handoff-bundle-and-continuity.md), Delegated):* the only provider-reported text carried is each run's structured result `summary` and its `failure_detail`; repository-produced text is file paths, diff excerpts and verification output excerpts. Each is redacted, bounded (`continuity.max_text_chars`, default 2000, Assumption), stripped of control and bidirectional characters and rendered only inside a labelled block whose every line is prefixed, so it cannot forge the prompt's structure; the preamble tells the agent to verify against the repository. Remains: an agent may still follow injected text within its own profile; labels do not prevent that |
| R-64 | *Proposed (Delegated, M5 T5.4), not blocking.* LLD §15.4 first claimed that truncation never fails a handoff build; it does not hold, because 64 constraints of 2,000 characters alone exceed the 16 KiB minimum and `as_of` grows with the number of runs | A session with very many runs cannot be handed off: `continue_task` returns `TOOL_FAILURE` with `HANDOFF_BUNDLE_UNAVAILABLE` and writes nothing | The builder drops further fields in a fixed order and then fails closed ([LLD §15.1](design/LLD.md#151-bundle-schema-version-1) "As built"); §15.4 now says so. Open: cap `as_of` (or summarise old runs) if real sessions reach it; evidence `src/continuity/bundle.test.ts`. Delegated; owner not asked |

Numbering: R-36, R-37 and Q-32 to Q-35 (§7.3) were written on the M3 wave B branch as R-29, R-30 and Q-27 to
Q-30. PR #17 merged all but Q-30 into main under those numbers, which main already used for other items; they were
renumbered on 2026-10-13 when main was merged back into that branch. Main's own R-29, R-30 and Q-27 to Q-30 keep
their meaning.

It clears when the operator completes T4.61 for those cells | FR-ADP-09 | Moonshot documentation or a validated installed version (M4 tasks T4.60 and T4.61, §6.8) |

No blocker affects M1, M2 or M9.

| Q-48 | *Delegated, answered (decision 40, T12.0).* Storage and embedding backend for the retrieval index: SQLite per project with BLOB vectors and brute-force cosine search under a hard chunk cap (no new native dependency), or an approximate-nearest-neighbour index or vector extension. Which embedding backend shapes (`local`, `http`) first, and the default context-pack budget | **Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated; answered by the orchestrator's worker under its fail-closed instruction; not owner-approved; no bypass effect).** SQLite per project with float32 BLOB vectors and a brute-force cosine scan of vectors loaded into memory (per-query streaming from SQLite measured 5 to 13 times slower); no vector extension, approximate-nearest-neighbour index or new native dependency. Cap 25,000 chunks per store; `top_k` 8; `max_context_tokens` 4,000 (controller estimate); `http` backend first, `local` refused at load until an in-process model has an ADR. Model and dimension stay operator choices. Measurements: [TDD §3.13.4](design/TDD.md#3134-t120-spike-result-2026-10-14) |

| Q-61 | *Delegated, open; for zemdregon because it changes what bypass runs may do (M5, T5.1, T5.6).* Owner decision 17 makes a `privileged` approval durable for later runs of the same meta-session, MCP client and scope (FR-POL-09, [FRD C7](requirements/FRD.md#72-continue_task)), and the approved scope names no provider ([LLD §3.1](design/LLD.md#31-privileged-scope-m3)). Read literally, FR-POL-09 says yes (the target provider must still compile the scope, FR-POL-02, ADR-0017); the operator may, however, have approved the scope with one provider in mind | *Proposed default (fail closed, Delegated) until answered:* refused with `INVALID_REQUEST`. The orchestrator can start a new `privileged` `delegate_task` for the other provider, which needs its own approval (or none for a client with `privileged_approval: "not_required"`, owner decision 22) but starts a new meta-session, so continuity is by hand. Same-provider `privileged` continuations are unchanged. *Note 2026-10-14 (T5.1):* [ADR-0038](adr/0038-handoff-bundle-and-continuity.md) item 8 keeps this default as one constant, so an answer of "allowed" is a one-line change; still open |
| Q-62 | *Delegated finding, open; security-relevant, for zemdregon (2026-10-14, T4.21).* Grok Build 1.0.50 on Ampere: the Git-write deny rule `--deny 'Bash(git commit*)'` is prefix-matched, so `git -C . commit` and `/usr/bin/git commit` created commits (plain `git commit`, `sh -c 'git commit'` and `git push` were denied); a sandboxed process can list `~/.grok` and `test -r` its login file under `--sandbox strict` and `workspace`; and `--permission-mode dontAsk` with no allow rule did not deny `echo hi` (cause not isolated). Should `provider-deny-rules` be declared for Grok at all? | **Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the orchestrator, worker Claude Code; not zemdregon, nothing here restricts bypass beyond the Git-write denial already kept; [ADR-0039](adr/0039-switchboard-side-tool-call-guard.md)).** `provider-deny-rules` is not declared as Grok's Git-write control: a deny rule is at most defence in depth. The control is Switchboard's own: the tool-call guard matches Git writes on parsed words (`git -C`, absolute paths, `env`/`sh -c` wrappers; `src/adapters/tool-guard.ts`, `src/adapters/git-write.ts`) and fails the run, and the FR-GIT-03 ref snapshot restores refs. Provider state (`~/.grok` and the other provider directories) is kept out by refusing a working directory inside or containing one and by failing a run whose tool call names one (`src/policy/provider-state.ts`); the sandbox is not relied on. `dontAsk` is not relied on to deny: Grok profiles stay `Ineligible` until an adapter live check shows a deny. Tests: `src/adapters/tool-guard.test.ts`. Live re-check: not run (no Grok adapter exists; the fix is adapter-independent). Original status: Unanswered. Evidence: [live-checks-2026-10-14-grok.md](research/live-checks-2026-10-14-grok.md) (checks were run without `--always-approve` and `--trust`; those parts of TDD §4.3.2 steps 9 and 10 are not run). TDD §4.3 cells not edited. |

Does Antigravity stay `Ineligible` for untrusted profiles? | **Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the orchestrator, worker Claude Code; not zemdregon; [ADR-0039](adr/0039-switchboard-side-tool-call-guard.md)).** Antigravity stays `Ineligible` for the untrusted profiles: `init.permission_mode` was `request-review` in every run and no per-run permission control was shown to hold, so nothing can be compiled for them. Independently of the provider, a worktree holding a repo-local `.agents` MCP or hook file is refused before spawn while `repoConfig` is `disabled` (`src/policy/repo-agent-config.ts`, lifecycle pre-spawn check); an approved scope with `repository_config` `approved` or `enabled` lifts it, `disabled` keeps it. The bypass variants are unchanged and were not run. Tests: `src/adapters/tool-guard.test.ts` ("repo-local agent config") and, through the lifecycle with the fake CLI, `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.test.ts` ("pre-spawn refusals"; found and fixed a fail-open for a privileged scope with `repository_config` `disabled`, 2026-10-14). Live re-check: not run (agy not driven again; the gate does not depend on it). Original status: Unanswered. Evidence: [live-checks-2026-10-14-antigravity.md](research/live-checks-2026-10-14-antigravity.md). The bypass variants (C7b, C8, C11, C18) were not run. |
| Q-64 | *Delegated finding, open; security-relevant (2026-10-14, T4.51).* OpenCode v2.0.23 (not the v1 surface TDD §4.6 describes: no `--pure`, `--dir`, `--attach`): `OPENCODE_PERMISSION='{"*":"deny"}'` did not deny a `read` tool call on the shared service or with `--standalone`, nor via `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`; shell commands including `git commit` and `git push` ran with no prompt; a recognised deny in project config made tool runs fail with a 403 `provider.auth` error. Is a per-run deny compilable at all? | **Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the orchestrator, worker Claude Code; not zemdregon; [ADR-0039](adr/0039-switchboard-side-tool-call-guard.md)).** A per-run deny is not compilable for OpenCode v2.0.23 on the evidence (the env and inline-config denials did not deny, and a recognised deny in config made tool runs fail 403): `OPENCODE_PERMISSION` and `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` are never used as a control (ADR-0037 limits stay), and OpenCode's untrusted profiles stay `Ineligible`. What Switchboard enforces itself applies to any OpenCode run: Git writes (shell `git commit`/`git push` ran with no prompt) and provider-state paths are failed by the tool-call guard whatever OpenCode honoured, and repo-local `opencode.json` and `.opencode` are refused while `repoConfig` is `disabled`. Tests: `src/adapters/tool-guard.test.ts` ("a run whose provider ignored its deny rules"). Live re-check: not run (no adapter). Original status: Unanswered. Evidence: [live-checks-2026-10-14-opencode.md](research/live-checks-2026-10-14-opencode.md). Rows 6 `--auto`, 7 deny, 8, 9 not run or not checkable. |
| Q-65 | *Delegated finding, open (2026-10-14, T4.41).* Cursor CLI 2026.10.12-d47ca18: edits apply without `--force` (TDD [C3] is wrong), a refused shell call still yields `result` `success` and exit 0, `status` exits 0 logged out (use `isAuthenticated`), `--sandbox enabled` hard-fails on this host (AppArmor user-namespace restriction), and `agent` in a login shell on this host is Grok's binary (use `cursor-agent`). `agent acp` exists and answers `initialize` (`authMethods` `cursor_login`). Do the §4.5 cells change? | **Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the orchestrator, worker Claude Code; not zemdregon; no bypass effect; [ADR-0039](adr/0039-switchboard-side-tool-call-guard.md)).** The §4.5 cells change as follows when the adapter (T4.42) is written: omitting `--force` is not a read-only control (edits applied), so `inspect-untrusted` and `edit-untrusted` are `Ineligible` for writing limits until a check shows one; a refused shell call is detected from events, never from `result` `success` or exit 0; authentication uses `isAuthenticated`, not `status` exit code; `--sandbox enabled` is treated as unavailable where it hard-fails (this host), so `test-sandboxed` is `Ineligible` there; the binary is `cursor-agent`, never `agent`. Repo `.cursor/mcp.json` stays inert without `--approve-mcps` (HELD) and is refused while `repoConfig` is `disabled` anyway. TDD cells are not edited here; see the 2026-10-14 notes under TDD §4.5. Live re-check: not run. Original status: Unanswered. Evidence: [live-checks-2026-10-14-cursor.md](research/live-checks-2026-10-14-cursor.md). No ESCAPED result; the `--force`, `-f`, `--approve-mcps` rows were not run. |

| Q-68 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the orchestrator on PR #108; not owner-approved): redact at admission, fail closed. Applied in [ADR-0042](adr/0042-objective-redaction-at-admission.md); see the NFR-SEC-01 row in §6.1.1. Originally: Delegated finding, open (2026-10-14, NFR-SEC-01 scan).* The run objective is stored verbatim in `meta_session.objective` and `run.request_json` as the continuity record, so a credential an orchestrator pastes into a prompt reaches the ledger (`test/secret-scan.test.ts` records this as an expected failure). Redacting it at admission changes what a continuation re-sends to the provider; not redacting it leaves NFR-SEC-01 `Partial`. Redact at admission, or accept and document? | Answered: the objective and constraints are redacted with the artifact secret patterns at admission; the original is not kept and the provider receives the redacted text on the first run and on continuation; `admission_redactions` in `run.request_json` records the count. | Q-69 | *Delegated, answered by the orchestrator (2026-10-14, T6.1; not zemdregon, no bypass effect; [ADR-0043](adr/0043-capacity-estimate-breaker-and-retry.md)).* How is the FR-CAP-02 estimate derived when no provider reports remaining quota (T6.0)? | **Decided:** from controller facts and provider-reported signals over `capacity.window_seconds` (default 3600 s, **Assumption**); no evidence gives `capacity_score` `null`, `confidence` 0, `throttle_risk` `unknown`, never shown as headroom and never a reason for ineligibility; `high` gives score 0, `elevated` half the `weight` prior, `low` the prior; confidence scales with terminal runs up to `capacity.confidence_runs` (default 10, **Assumption**); `explicit_remaining` only from a declared provider-reported key (none today). [LLD §16.2](design/LLD.md#162-estimator) |
| Q-70 | *Delegated, answered by the orchestrator (2026-10-14, T6.1; not zemdregon, no bypass effect).* Which failures open a provider breaker, after how many, and for how long? | **Decided:** `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`, `PROVIDER_OVERLOADED` and `PROCESS_CRASH` not caused by a controller restart; 3 in a row (**Assumption**); open 300 s (**Assumption**), then one probe run; a healthy outcome (success, or a test, tool, permission or sandbox failure after the provider ran) closes it; persisted in `provider_health`. [LLD §16.4](design/LLD.md#164-per-provider-circuit-breaker) |
| Q-71 | *Delegated, answered by the orchestrator (2026-10-14, T6.1; not zemdregon, no bypass effect).* Retry parameters, and are runs orphaned by a controller restart retried? | **Decided:** LLD §9 values (3 attempts, base 2 s, cap 120 s, full jitter; **Assumptions**); a provider-reported reset beyond `max(cap, queue horizon)` or an open breaker ends the chain; orphaned runs are **not** retried automatically (fail closed: what the provider did outside the worktree is unknown). [LLD §16.5](design/LLD.md#165-retry-with-successor-runs) |
| Q-72 | *Delegated, answered by the orchestrator (2026-10-14, T6.1; not zemdregon, no bypass effect).* Default BR-9 queue horizon for a known reset after `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`? | **Decided:** 0 (never hold a run waiting for a provider reset unless the operator sets `capacity.queue_horizon_seconds`); the orchestrator gets `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` with the reset time and decides. [LLD §16.6](design/LLD.md#166-quota-exhaustion-ladder-t66) |
| Q-73 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the orchestrator, Linus, for T13.3; not owner-approved).* What form do the embedded authorization server's tokens take, and how are they stored and checked? Access and refresh tokens are opaque random values of at least 256 bits from a CSPRNG, never JWTs. Only a SHA-256 hash of each token is stored, in the ledger (forward-only migration 0010, `oauth_token`), with client, audience, scopes, expiry and a revoked flag; lookup is by hash with a constant-time compare. Unknown, expired, revoked, wrong-audience and malformed tokens all get the same 401 with a `WWW-Authenticate` header naming the protected-resource metadata; only a reason code is logged. Applied in [LLD §3.3](design/LLD.md#33-network-mcp-transport-m13) and an as-built note in [ADR-0041](adr/0041-http-transport-with-oauth-2-1.md) item 5. | — |
| Q-74 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the worker under the orchestrator's T13.\* instruction to pick the safer, fail-closed option; not owner-approved, no bypass effect).* How open is Dynamic Client Registration (ADR-0041 item 3)? | **Decided:** open registration (no initial access token, so the Grok Bot embed can register itself), but public clients only (no secret), `https` redirect URIs only (plain `http`, loopback included, refused until a native client needs it), the authorization-code grant with optional refresh only, and every new client `pending` with no grant until the operator approves it on the consent page. At most 16 pending clients from the last 24 hours (**Assumption**), a 16 KiB body cap (**Assumption**); `scope` and other unknown fields are ignored. [LLD §3.3](design/LLD.md#33-network-mcp-transport-m13), FR-MCP-16. Revisit if a client needs a loopback redirect or the cap is too tight. |
| Q-75 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the orchestrator, Linus, for T13.8; not owner-approved).* How is the consent-page passphrase hashed without a new dependency? | **Decided:** no new npm dependency, native or not. Node's built-in `crypto.argon2` exists in Node 24 (checked on 24.21.0) but not in Node 22, the bottom of `engines` (`>=22`) and the version CI pins, so ADR-0041's argon2id is replaced by the built-in scrypt with N=2^17, r=8, p=1, a 16-byte random salt and a 32-byte key, compared with `timingSafeEqual`. The hash (`scrypt$17$8$1$<salt>$<key>`) is the only form `transport.http.passphrase_hash` accepts; `switchboard consent hash-passphrase` prints it from a passphrase read on stdin. As-built note in [ADR-0041](adr/0041-http-transport-with-oauth-2-1.md) item 3; [LLD §3.3](design/LLD.md#33-network-mcp-transport-m13). Revisit when the supported Node range starts at a version with a stable `crypto.argon2`. |
| Q-76 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the worker under the orchestrator's T13.8 instruction to pick the safer, fail-closed option; not owner-approved, no bypass effect).* How do the authorization endpoint, consent page and token endpoint behave? | **Decided:** PKCE `S256` only (a missing method is `plain` and refused); `redirect_uri` and `resource` required at `/authorize` and exact; the only grantable scope is `mcp`, so `privileged` and every other scope get `invalid_scope`; no login session, so every authorization needs the passphrase or a pairing code; a pending client is approved by the first successful consent, a denied or revoked one is refused; "Deny" needs no credential and changes no status. Errors redirect only for an approved client (no open redirector through a self-registered URI). CSRF: server-side request id and token, a `__Host-` `SameSite=Strict` cookie, the issuer's own `Origin` only and no cross-site `Sec-Fetch-Site`. Five wrong passphrases in 15 minutes lock passphrase approval for 15 minutes, even for the right passphrase; one scrypt check at a time. Wrong pairing codes are throttled separately (ten a minute) and never locked, so a stranger who holds the passphrase lockout cannot also block the operator's recovery by pairing code (an attacker can still delay passphrase approval; accepted as the fail-closed cost). Pairing codes: 60 bits, hashed, single use, 10 minutes, a new one ends older ones. Codes: in memory, hashed, single use, 60 seconds; a reused code revokes the client's tokens. Access tokens 15 minutes, refresh tokens 7 days and rotated (checks before the token is spent), a reused refresh token revokes the client's tokens (lifetimes and limits are **Assumptions**). The pairing command is `switchboard consent pair`, not ADR-0041's `auth pair`, because FR-OPS-03 and its test forbid an `auth` command. **For zemdregon (not blocking):** FR-OPS-03 says "no web interface", and the consent page that ADR-0041 (Q-67) requires is one; this task read ADR-0041 as the narrower, later decision. The orchestrator (Linus) confirmed that reading on 2026-10-14 (Delegated, not owner-approved), and FR-OPS-03 now names the consent page as its one exception. [LLD §3.3](design/LLD.md#33-network-mcp-transport-m13), FR-MCP-17. |
| Q-77 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the worker under the orchestrator's T13.\* instruction to pick the safer, fail-closed option; not owner-approved; no bypass effect: privileged runs over HTTP still need the operator's approval, and bypass stays an approved-scope matter).* How does an authenticated HTTP client map to a configured MCP client, and how is the MCP server mounted? | **Decided:** the token subject is the OAuth client id; `transport.http.clients` maps it to an `mcp_clients` id, and `default_mcp_client` (operator opt-in) covers approved clients with no mapping; with neither the request gets 403 `insufficient_scope`, as does a token without the `mcp` scope. Config load refuses an unknown target, a target with `privileged_approval: not_required`, so no remote client can skip the operator's privileged approval (FR-MCP-14), and a `default_mcp_client` with a `privileged` ceiling (only an explicit per-client mapping may reach one). Each request re-checks that the OAuth client is still approved. Only `POST` is served; 16 requests in flight, 4 per OAuth client and a 256 KiB body (**Assumptions**); the `Authorization` and `Cookie` headers are removed before the MCP layer. The mount is the SDK's stateless Streamable HTTP mode: a fresh server and transport per request, JSON responses, no session ID ever issued or accepted, so a session cannot authenticate on its own (FR-MCP-15). OAuth clients mapped to the same entry share its runs (**Assumption**: acceptable for a single operator; superseded by Q-78, which binds them per OAuth client). [LLD §3.3](design/LLD.md#33-network-mcp-transport-m13). Revisit if a client needs server-sent notifications (stateful sessions, T13.5). |
| Q-78 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the worker under the orchestrator's T13.5 instruction to decide fail-closed; not owner-approved; no bypass effect: it only narrows who can see a run).* What does FR-MCP-15's "session bound to the subject and client" mean on a stateless HTTP mount (Q-77)? | **Decided:** there is no MCP session, so a request carrying an `Mcp-Session-Id` header is refused (400) rather than ignored. What a caller comes back to is a meta-session and its runs, so those are bound: each meta-session opened over HTTP is recorded with the token's OAuth client (`http_subject`, migration 0013, the one ledger change) right after admission, and a run belongs to its meta-session (retry successors included). It is known only to its own configured MCP client and subject: over HTTP the binding must name the caller's OAuth client, over stdio there must be none. Anything else gets the "unknown id" answer of an id that does not exist (no existence oracle; not LLD's earlier 403); another subject's idempotency key reads as a key reused with different parameters. A crash between admission and the binding write leaves the meta-session stdio-only, which fails closed for HTTP callers. The serve log is redacted at the boundary, and the redactor now matches Switchboard's own token and code prefixes. Not bound: dynamic projects (T9.9), which stay per MCP client (**Assumption**), and no audit event is written for a refused lookup (it is indistinguishable from a typo). [LLD §3.3](design/LLD.md#33-network-mcp-transport-m13), FR-MCP-15. Revisit if stateful sessions (server-sent notifications) are ever needed. |
| Q-79 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the worker under the orchestrator's M4 instruction to choose the safer, fail-closed option; not owner-approved; it compiles the bypass cell owner decision 46 asks for and restricts nothing a bypass run could do before).* How does the Cursor adapter compile an approved `privileged` bypass scope (T4.42), given that `--sandbox enabled` exits 1 on the operator's host and what `--force` does to the sandbox was never observed (T4.41 rows 9 and 16)? | **Decided:** `cursor-agent --print --output-format stream-json --trust --force`, prompt on stdin, no `--sandbox` flag, and only for worktree files, shell `broad`, network `on` and repository configuration `enabled` with no requested model. Every narrower scope is `Ineligible: CAPABILITY_MISSING`, because without a sandbox Cursor enforces no read-only files, no shell limit and no network limit, and `--trust` (needed to start) loads the repository's rules; claiming any of those would claim a control that is not enforced. Cursor confines nothing in this run: what remains is the controller's worktree as working directory, the tool-call guard (ADR-0039), FR-GIT-03 and FR-GIT-06. Revisit when an operator capture shows `--sandbox enabled` confining under `--force` on a host where it starts (then network `off` and read-only files could compile with it). [TDD §4.5.1](design/TDD.md#451-cursor-native-controls-per-profile-and-platform-t440) "Today" row |
| Q-80 | *Delegated, answered by the orchestrator (2026-10-14, T7.0; not zemdregon, no bypass effect; [ADR-0044](adr/0044-deterministic-route-scoring-and-replayable-decisions.md)).* What values do the FR-RTE-02 score terms and weights take when no source gives a provider's price, model ranking or sandbox strength? | **Decided:** `security` and `cost` are 0.5 for every candidate (they never decide a route); `success`, `latency` and `handoff` come from the controller's ledger; an unknown capacity estimate scores 0, never headroom (Q-69); every weight defaults to 1 (**Assumption**), configurable 0 to 100; with no history the configured order decides, as in M2. [LLD §17.3](design/LLD.md#173-score-fr-rte-02) |
| Q-81 | *Delegated, answered by the orchestrator (2026-10-14, T7.0; not zemdregon, no bypass effect).* The affinity points LLD §3.2 leaves open: thresholds, when the row is written, continuations, expiry. | **Decided (fail closed, keep the provider):** `low_water_mark` and `min_confidence` default to `null`, so only positive evidence (known quota exhaustion, a `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` run, an exhausted retry chain) switches; the row is written only when a run whose decision applied affinity succeeds (a failed first run creates none); `continue_task` decisions never move it (the quota failover successor does, as `capacity_exhausted`); `idle_ttl` accepts only `null` (Q-52). [LLD §17.6](design/LLD.md#176-affinity-details-fills-32) |
| Q-82 | *Delegated, answered by the orchestrator (2026-10-14, T7.0; not zemdregon, no bypass effect).* What does the FR-RTE-04 heuristic classifier read, and do its features change routes? | **Decided:** the request only (objective, counts of constraints and verification commands, whether the profile writes), with fixed thresholds and keyword lists (**Assumptions**, `heuristic.v1`); no repository metadata in M7. The features are recorded with `source: "heuristic"` but rank nothing, because no source maps a feature to a provider's fitness. [LLD §17.2](design/LLD.md#172-heuristic-features-fr-rte-04) |
| Q-83 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the worker under the orchestrator's M4 instruction to choose the safer, fail-closed option; not owner-approved; it compiles the bypass cell owner decision 46 asks for).* How does the OpenCode adapter compile an approved `privileged` bypass scope (T4.52), given that no per-run permission set took effect on v2.0.23 (Q-64) and that its default already runs shell commands and edits without asking? | **Decided:** `opencode run --format json --standalone -- <prompt>` with `OPENCODE_DISABLE_AUTOUPDATE=1`, and only for worktree files, shell `broad`, network `on` and repository configuration `enabled` with no requested model; every narrower scope is `Ineligible: CAPABILITY_MISSING`, since nothing enforces it. `--auto` stays declared bypass-class but is **not** emitted: the cell is reachable without it, and it would only approve what the default refuses (reads and writes outside the working directory, live check row 11), so leaving it out is the least privilege that still runs. `--standalone` is always emitted, because without it the tools run in the host's shared background service, outside the run's process group, environment allowlist, timeouts and cancel. OpenCode confines nothing in this run: what remains is the controller's worktree as working directory, the tool-call guard (ADR-0039), FR-GIT-03 and FR-GIT-06. Revisit if a headless `ask` is ever seen to hang a bypass run (then `--auto` may be needed), or when a per-run deny is shown to hold. [TDD §4.6.1](design/TDD.md#461-opencode-native-controls-per-profile-and-platform-t450) "Today" row |
| Q-84 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the worker under the orchestrator's M4 instruction to choose the safer, fail-closed option; not owner-approved; it compiles the bypass cell owner decision 46 asks for).* How does the Antigravity adapter compile an approved `privileged` bypass scope (T4.32), given that `toolPermission` is a settings value and that whether `--sandbox` or deny rules still hold under `--dangerously-skip-permissions` was not observed (T4.31 C8)? | **Decided:** `agy --prompt=<prompt> --output-format stream-json --dangerously-skip-permissions` with `AGY_CLI_DISABLE_AUTO_UPDATE=true`, and only for worktree files, shell `broad`, network `on` and repository configuration `enabled` with no requested model; every narrower scope is `Ineligible: CAPABILITY_MISSING`, since nothing shown enforces it, and `--sandbox` is not compiled so that no confinement is claimed. Antigravity confines nothing in this run: what remains is the controller's worktree as working directory, the tool-call guard (ADR-0039), FR-GIT-03 and FR-GIT-06. The one-dash spelling of each bypass-class flag is declared to the audit ([ADR-0045](adr/0045-one-dash-long-bypass-flag-spellings.md)). Revisit when T4.38 records what `--sandbox` does under the bypass flag. [TDD §4.4.2](design/TDD.md#442-antigravity-native-controls-per-profile-and-platform-t430) "Today" row |
| Q-85 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the worker under the orchestrator's M4 instruction to choose the safer, fail-closed option; not owner-approved; no bypass effect).* When is an OpenCode run a success (T4.53), given that the T4.51 live check saw correct answers exit 0 with no `step_finish` (TDD §4.6.4, contradiction 12)? | **Decided:** only a clean expected exit with a `step_finish` whose `part.reason` is `stop` and no `error` event is a success; a run that exits 0 with text but no `stop` ends `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`. Some good runs will fail (the live check saw the missing `step_finish` intermittently); that is the fail-closed cost, since exit 0 alone also follows a run cut short after a tool step ([I-38661], [I-27697] in TDD §4.6). Revisit when a capture shows a reliable terminal marker. [TDD §4.6](design/TDD.md#46-opencode-m4) "Success rule" row |
| Q-86 | *Answered 2026-10-14 (Delegated by the worker under the orchestrator's M4 instruction to choose the safer, fail-closed option; not owner-approved; no bypass effect).* What makes an Antigravity run a success, and is `--conversation <id>` resume compiled (T4.33), given that a soft denial, a print timeout and an unknown conversation id all exit 0 with `status` `SUCCESS` (T4.31 C7, C9, C10)? | **Decided:** success needs a clean expected exit, a `result` with `status` `SUCCESS`, a reported `total_tokens` above 0 (a print timeout reported zero usage) and no `denied_actions` entry or `error` text; anything else fails. Resume is **not** compiled: an unknown id silently starts a new conversation, so a continuation that lost its history would look like one that kept it, and only a stderr warning (not passed to the adapter) says otherwise. Revisit when a capture shows a structured marker of a resumed conversation (for example a `num_turns` that counts earlier turns, seen in C9 but not yet a documented contract). [TDD §4.4.1](design/TDD.md#441-antigravity-output-shapes-and-exit-codes-t430) |
| Q-90 | *Delegated, answered by the orchestrator (2026-10-14, T7.5; not zemdregon, no bypass effect).* FR-RTE-08 asks to reuse a project's last model; should the remembered model be passed to the adapter's `compile`? | **Decided (fail closed, keep the provider):** not yet. The model each run's native session reported is remembered in `project_affinity.model` and shown by `explain_route`, but not passed to `compile`, because the Codex adapter refuses any model (`CAPABILITY_MISSING`) and no adapter declares that a reported model id is accepted by its model flag; pinning could make a held provider ineligible. T7.12 adds pinning once adapters declare it. (Numbered Q-90, not the next free number, because the parallel M4 work takes consecutive numbers.) [LLD §17.6](design/LLD.md#176-affinity-details-fills-32) |

| Upstream ACP server | Deferred | MCP satisfies C-7 | ADR-0002 |
| Learned (contextual-bandit) routing | Deferred | Needs outcome data | FR-RTE-05 |
| OpenCode persistent `serve`/`attach` transport | Deferred | `opencode run` suffices first | FR-EXE-04 |
| Native Windows host | Deferred | Linux first | NFR-PORT-03 |

| Automatic merge of result branches | Deferred | Needs judgement | ADR-0006 |
| Multi-user or hosted operation | Excluded | Compliance and credential risk | ADR-0011 |
| Context kinds other than `repository`; remote-clone contexts; multiple writable contexts in one run | Deferred | Not asked for yet; the schema is extensible | FR-PRJ-02 |
| Rewriting attribution inside file contents | Excluded | Out of the commit boundary; the controller does not edit what agents write | FR-GIT-04, R-14 |
| Model-assisted secret or PII redaction (inner agent) | Deferred | A miss is a silent leak and sending content to find secrets is itself exposure; deterministic redaction stays the control. Revisit as an add-only advisory pass on a `local` backend | FR-INN-18 |
| Inner agent exposed to orchestrators as a tool | Excluded | Makes Switchboard a model proxy and widens TB-1; no requirement behind it | ADR-0016 |
| Inner-agent tasks IT-10 to IT-14 (prompt rewriting, test selection, diff pre-review, commit splitting, changelogs) | Deferred or excluded | Each changes intent, verification or authority, or has no requirement; see [PRD §3.1](requirements/PRD.md#31-inner-agent-task-catalogue) | — |
| Token-bucket rate gate per provider | Deferred (T6.8, 2026-10-14) | No source publishes an enforceable rate limit for the subscription logins in use (T6.0 inventory); a gate without one would invent a number | FR-CAP-07 |
| Approximate-nearest-neighbour or vector-database index for retrieval | Deferred | The first version is SQLite with in-process search under a hard cap; revisit only if the T12.0 spike shows the cap is too low | FR-RAG-01, Q-48 |
| Learned reranking of retrieved snippets | Deferred | No measurement yet that retrieval helps (R-57); no requirement | — |
| Code-graph or symbol-level retrieval | Deferred | Not asked for; text chunks first | — |
| Retrieval index shared across operators or installations | Excluded | Multi-user and hosted operation are excluded; the global index is one installation's | ADR-0011, FR-RAG-09 |
| Agent subscriptions to PR and merge events (CI pass or fail, another agent's commit to the PR, merge) | Deferred (Proposed, optional; owner's words in decision 43) | Optional, after everything else in the plan. Needs PRD use case, SRS requirements and a milestone before it is built; the GitHub webhook path and its trust rules are unspecified | CAP-14 (PRD §3); FR-SUB-01 (SRS §3.16, Deferred) |

## 9. Next actionable milestone

**Close M2, then M3.** M2 is `Partial` (§6, §6.2). Its code and documentation are in place; the remaining work needs the
operator or zemdregon, in this order:

1. **Operator live smoke run.** Run `SWITCHBOARD_LIVE_TESTS=1 SWITCHBOARD_LIVE_PROVIDER=claude npm run test:live` (and
   `codex`) with native logins. Record which assumptions held; correct the fixtures, TDD §4.1 to §4.2, R-21, R-22,
   Q-10 and Q-13 with the result.
2. **Codex version (Q-12).** Done 2026-10-13 as a reviewed manifest edit (`0.162.1`, Delegated; see Q-12). To validate
   another version, run `switchboard adapters validate codex` (T4.4), which writes that record after a passing live
   check; running it is the operator task T4.11.
3. **NFR-PERF-02 (Q-14).** Decided and implemented as T3.21 (Delegated decision 29, 2026-10-13); zemdregon may revise the
   60 s TTL default or the successes-only rule.
4. Re-run `doctor` on a machine with a provider installed and update the "Blocks Done" list in §6.2.
5. **Git write denial (FR-GIT-01).** Argv check done (T2.2 row; Delegated agent work, 2026-10-12). What remains is
   the live confirmation in step 1 (checks G-1, C-G2, X-G1) and the M9 detection layer (FR-GIT-03).

**M3 Isolation is planned** (§6.5, 2026-10-12; owner decisions 10 to 13 and 17 in §4.2). Every remaining M2 item above needs
the operator or zemdregon, so agent work proceeds meanwhile on M3 **wave A** (§6.5.1), none of which needs a live provider:
T3.0 (native-controls spike), T3.1 (outer-sandbox evaluation), T3.2 (profile defaults and config keys), the BR-3
mapping part of T3.7, T3.8 (controller-side malicious-fixture invariants) and T3.12 (GC algorithm). T3.14 (README
Codex correction) and T3.16 (privileged scope contract in [LLD §3.1](design/LLD.md#31-privileged-scope-m3) and
[ADR-0017](adr/0017-approved-privileged-scope-bypass.md)) are done, so T3.17 (argv audit) and, after T3.2, T3.3 can
start. No code that emits a bypass flag lands before T3.17. Open M3 questions for zemdregon: Q-15 to Q-22 and Q-30 (Q-31
was decided by decision 27; Q-28 and Q-29 were answered on 2026-10-13, Q-33 by decision 19); residual risks of decisions
10 to 13, 17, 19 and 22: R-23 to R-26, R-34, R-38 and R-39; R-35 (the nested-repository gap) is mitigated by T3.20. T3.18
(decisions 19 and 22 in the controller) is done. T3.10 (verification stage) is done (2026-10-13, Delegated); its open
items are R-61 (accepted for approved bypass runs by decision 45, open otherwise) and confinement is T3.11; Q-57
is answered by decision 45.
Notes carried from M2:

- **`QUEUE_FULL`** maps to `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` with a reason (Q-5, Proposed); a dedicated code needs an LLD §4 and SRS
  change.

### 9.1 How an agent picks up work

1. Read [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md), then this roadmap's §6 to find the first milestone whose status is not `Done`. A
   `Partial` milestone is closed from its "Blocks Done" list before the next one starts, unless every remaining item
   needs the operator or zemdregon; agents then work on the next milestone's tasks that do not depend on those items (as
   M3 wave A does while M2 waits for the live smoke run).
2. Take the first task in that milestone's task table whose dependencies are done. If the milestone has no task table
   yet (M8 onward; M4's is §6.8, M5's §6.9, M6's §6.11 and M7's §6.12), break it into tasks in the same format first,
   from its row in §6 and the requirements it lists, and add them here before coding.
3. Read every reference in the task row: the SRS requirements, the FRD workflow or rule, the LLD/TDD section and the
   ADRs. Those are the contract; this roadmap only sequences them.
4. Implement with tests that prove the task's "Done when" checks and the cited requirements' verification method.
5. In the same change, update the task's status here with evidence, and fix any document the work proved wrong (or add
   an ADR if an architectural decision changed).
6. Run `node scripts/check-docs.mjs` and the test suite before opening the pull request.

## 10. Keeping this roadmap current

- Change status in §6 in the same change as the work, with evidence.
- New scope goes through the PRD and SRS first, then gets a milestone here.
- A conflict between documents is resolved in favour of the owner named in §5, and the losing document is corrected in
  the same change.
